L299: T-cell Dependent Antibody Response (TDAR) Evaluation	
All animals were vaccinated with Anti-KLH on Day 36 (1st immunization) after B-cell depletion during the dosing period and all recovery animals on Day 195 (2nd immunization) in Week 15 of the recovery period. Blood samples were collected at several time points at pre- and each post-immunization. The levels of anti-KLH IgG and anti-LLH IgM in the samples were determined.
Anti-KLH IgM and IgG: The mean peak anti-KLH IgM and IgG responses to KLH vaccine were reduced in all dose groups by 60% to 85% in a non-dose dependent manner, as compared to the mean peak titer for the controls on Day 36. These effects were reversible.
Anti-TT IgG: All animals were vaccinated with TT on Day -35 (1st immunization) of the predose period, on Day 28 (2nd immunization) in Week 5 of the dosing period, and on Day 188 (3rd immunization) in Week 14 of the recovery period.
The mean peak anti-TT IgG responses were reduced in all dose groups by 60% to 90% in a non-dose dependent manner, as compared to the control group mean peak titer on Day 28. These effects were reversible.

Toxicokinetics	
See the section 1.4 ADME/PK for the toxicokinetic data.
L300: ## General toxicology; additional studies
L301: Tafasitamab was also assessed in three non-GLP single-dose studies (study #XC9-05707, #XC9-07907, #XC0-08007), and a GLP 8-week repeat-dose study (XC9-14408), each in cynomolgus monkeys. The results are summarized below. In general, findings were comparable to those observed in the 13-week toxicology study and expected based on the pharmacology of the product. See additional information below related to the 8-week toxicology, Study XC9-14408:
L302: In a GLP-compliant study, groups of 5/sex cynomolgus monkeys received 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg of tafasitamab by i.v. infusion once every 2 weeks x 4. Three animals/sex were necropsied at termination of dosing (Day 57) and 2/sex were sacrificed after 90 days of recovery (Day 147).
L303: ## Key Study Findings:
L304: - Tafasitamab caused marked decreases in peripheral blood B cells associated with decreased absolute lymphocytes, decreased white blood cell counts, decreased spleen weights, and altered spleen histology (decreased cellularity of the splenic germinal center). By the end of recovery (Day 90), depleted B cells at the low- and mid-dose had fully recovered but the high-dose had only partially recovered (to 53% of baseline).
- The splenic change was observed at all doses, and the severity increased with increasing dose. Recovery was apparent; one high-dose animal (1/4, a male) continued to exhibit decreased cellularity of the germinal center on Day 147.
L305: - ADAs were detected in 47% of tafasitamab treated monkeys (8/10 at 2 mg/kg, 3/10 at 10 mg/kg, and 3/10 at 50 mg/kg). ADAs enhanced the clearance of tafasitamab.
L306: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L307: ## The Applicant's Position:
L308: No studies were conducted to evaluate the genotoxicity of tafasitamab. According to ICH guideline S6(R1), the range and type of genotoxicity studies routinely conducted for pharmaceuticals are not applicable to biotechnology-derived pharmaceuticals and therefore are not needed for tafasitamab. In addition, it is not expected that tafasitamab would interact directly with DNA or other chromosomal material.
L309: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L310: The FDA concurs with the Applicant.
L311: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L312: ## The Applicant's Position:
L313: Carcinogenicity studies in animals were not conducted with tafasitamab. ICH guideline S9 outlines that carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support marketing for therapeutics intended to treat patients with advanced cancer, and as such no carcinogenicity studies are required.
L314: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L315: The FDA concurs with the Applicant.
L316: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L317: ## The Applicant's Position:
L318: In accordance with ICH S9, no embryofetal development or (enhanced) pre- and postnatal development studies were conducted, as the drug is given in the intended patient population in combination with a drug known to be teratogenic or causing embryo-fetal lethality (lenalidomide). The approach was agreed upon with FDA.
L319: Potential effects of tafasitamab on male and female fertility were examined by standard histopathology evaluation of the reproductive tract and by menstrual cycle assessment in females during the pivotal 13-week, repeat-dose, toxicity study in sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys (MOR208P008); no drug-related changes were observed.
L320: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L321: The FDA concurs with the Applicant that no embryo-fetal development (EFD) studies are needed when tafasitamab is given in combination with lenalidomide, a known teratogen and embryo-fetal toxicant.
L322: To address the potential effects of tafasitamab on embryo-fetal development, the Applicant submitted an assessment for reproductive and developmental risk using a weight-of-evidence approach and with the Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations Guidance for Industry. The relevant information is summarized below and supports that the main reproductive risk for tafasitamab is B-cell depletion in neonates.
L323: The WOE risk assessment include the following information; references cited were provided by the Applicant:
L324: - As CD19 expression is mainly restricted to cells of the B-cell lineage, and dendritic cells, the main reproductive risk for tafasitamab is immune related. While the impact of tafasitamab on fetal development is unknown; data in CD19 knockout mice and SCID mouse deficient in T and B cells, suggest that CD19 is not essential for developmental processes during embryofetal development 7,8,9. Tafasitamab is an IgG monoclonal antibody, and as a class, IgG monoclonal antibodies have the potential to cross the placental barrier permitting direct fetal exposure. Based on its mechanism of action and pharmacodynamic effects seen in animals, tafasitamab may cause depletion of B cells and impair the immune response after birth due to fetal exposure to tafasitamab.
- As described in toxicology studies in monkeys, the main toxicities of tafasitamab treatment were to the immune system with no evidence of off-target toxicity, including effects to the male and female reproductive organs.
- The dosing regimen for the intended patient population (relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma) consists of the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide. Lenalidomide is a teratogenic drug.
L325: 7 Rickert RC, Rajewsky K, Roes J. (1995). "Impairment of T-cell-dependent B-cell responses and B-1 cell development in CD19-deficient mice." *Nature*. 1995 Jul 27;376(6538):352-5
L326: 8 Gärdby E, Chen XJ, Lycke NY. (2001). "Impaired CD40-signalling in CD19-deficient mice selectively affects Th2-dependent isotype switching." *Scand J Immunol*. 2001 Jan;53(1):13-23
L327: 9 Kitamura D, Roes J, Kühn R, Rajewsky K. (1991). "A B cell-deficient mouse by targeted disruption of the membrane exon of the immunoglobulin mu chain gene." *Nature*. Apr 4;350(6317):423-6
L328: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L329: ## The Applicant's Position:
L330: To identify tissues with the potential to be associated with tafasitamab systemic toxicities, the binding of FITC labelled tafasitamab to cynomolgus monkey and human tissue panels was evaluated using immune-histochemical staining in frozen sections of normal tissues.
L331: Tafasitamab-FITC bound both to lymphocytes in the blood, and to mononuclear leukocytes which are presumed to be B-lymphocytes based on morphology and/or location, in numerous tissues. Some hematopoietic precursor cells in the bone marrow also stained. Under the conditions of the study, it was not possible to determine the stage of B-cell development for these precursor cells. No off-target binding was detected in tissue cross-reactivity studies on normal tissues from human and cynomolgus monkey.
L332: Staining in cynomolgus monkey tissues closely paralleled that seen in human tissues. The cell types stained, and the subcellular localization of the stain were similar although the staining intensity and frequency were not clear but may be attributable to the antigenic structure or distribution differences between species.
L333: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L334: The FDA agrees with Applicant.
L335: Compared to the human tissue cross-reactivity study, the binding intensity was reduced for the monkeys, likely due to a difference in binding affinity to the epitope.
L336: *Ramadevi Gudi*
*Primary Reviewer*
L337: *Brenda Gehrke*
*Supervisor*
L338: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L339: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L340: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L341: Tafasitamab is a Fc-modified monoclonal antibody that binds to CD19 antigen expressed on the surface of pre-B and mature B lymphocytes, and on several B-cell malignancies, including diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). The Applicant is seeking approval of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for, (b) (4) autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT). The proposed dosage of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg administered by intravenous infusion according to the following schedule (1 cycle = 28 days):
L342: - **Cycle 1:** Days 1, 4, 8, 15, and 22
- **Cycle 2-3:** Days 1, 8, 15, and 22
- **Cycle 4 and beyond:** Days 1 and 15
L343: Tafasitamab is proposed to be administered in combination with lenalidomide 25 mg on Days 1-21 of each cycle for a maximum of 12 cycles, followed by tafasitamab monotherapy until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L344: The Clinical Pharmacology section of the BLA is supported by analyses of single and multiple dose pharmacokinetics (PK) of tafasitamab in patients with DLBCL, immunogenicity, population pharmacokinetics (popPK), and exposure-response (E-R) relationships.
L345: ## RECOMMENDATIONS
L346: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information and data submitted in BLA 761163. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized in **Table 7** below.
L347: **Table 7: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues**
L348: REVIEW ISSUE	RECOMMENDATIONS/COMMENTS
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from pivotal Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) in the target population. An ORR of 60% (42% CR, 18% PR) was demonstrated out of 80 evaluable patients.
General dosing instructions	The proposed dosing regimen is 12 mg/kg tafasitamab administered by intravenous infusion on Days 1, 4, 8, 15, 22 in Cycle 1, Days 1, 8, 15, 22 in Cycles 2-3, Days 1, 15 in Cycles 4 and beyond) in combination with 12 mg lenalidomide on Days 1-21 of each 28-day cycle for up to 12 cycles is acceptable for approval. Body weight has a clinically significant effect on the PK of tafasitamab, with higher clearance and volume of distribution
L349: expected with higher body weight. Flat E-R relationships for ORR, PFS, and safety (Grade \ge 3 treatment emergent adverse events [TEAEs] and most evaluated TEAEs of special interest) were observed for tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide. Population PK and E-R analyses support the proposed dosing regimen.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dose adjustment is needed in patient subgroups. The effect of severe renal impairment to end-stage renal disease (CLcr < 30 mL/min) and moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5 x ULN, any AST) is not known.
Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement with the FDA revisions to the labeling.
L350: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L351: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L352: ## Data:
L353: ## Pharmacodynamics
L354: In patients with R/R-DLBCL, tafasitamab led to a reduction in peripheral blood B cell counts. The reduction relative to baseline B cell count reached 97 % after eight days of treatment in the L-MIND study (MOR208C203). Nadir with a reduction of 100% was reached within 16 weeks of treatment.
L355: ## Pharmacokinetics
L356: Based on a population pharmacokinetic analysis of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide, tafasitamab average serum trough concentrations (\pm standard deviation) were determined at 179 (\pm 53) \mug/mL during weekly intravenous administrations at 12 mg/kg including an additional dose at Day 4 of Cycle 1. During bi-weekly administrations from Cycle 4 onwards average trough serum concentrations of 153 (\pm 68) \mug/mL were determined. Overall maximum tafasitamab serum concentrations were determined at 483 (\pm109) \mug/mL.
L357: ## Distribution
L358: The total volume of distribution for tafasitamab was determined at 9.3 L.
L359: ## Elimination
L360: The general clearance of tafasitamab was determined at 0.41 L/day and terminal elimination half-life at 16.9 days. Following long-term observations, tafasitamab clearance was found to decrease over time to a value of 0.19 L/day after two years.
L361: ## Metabolism
L362: The exact pathway through which tafasitamab is metabolized has not been characterized. As a humanized IgG monoclonal antibody, tafasitamab is expected to be degraded into small peptides and amino acids via catabolic pathways in the same manner as endogenous IgG.
L363: ## Immunogenicity
L364: Overall, no treatment-emergent or treatment-boosted anti-tafasitamab antibodies were observed. Pre-existing anti-tafasitamab antibodies in 17/245 (6.9%) patients had no impact on pharmacokinetics, efficacy, or safety of tafasitamab.
L365: ## The Applicant's Position:
L366: The Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics program supports the BLA for the intended indication.
L367: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L368: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. To reflect the intended patient population, FDA updated the incidence and potential effect of immunogenicity to state that no clinically meaningful differences in the PK, efficacy, or safety profile of tafasitamab were observed in 2.5% (2/81) of patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) DLBCL with pre-existing anti-tafasitamab antibodies.
L369: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L370: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L371: The recommended dose of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg body weight administered as an intravenous infusion according to the following dosing schedule:
L372: - Cycle length: 28 days.
- Cycle 1: Administer tafasitamab on Day 1, 4, 8, 15 and 22.
- Cycles 2 - 3: Administer tafasitamab on Day 1, 8, 15 and 22 of each cycle.
- Cycle 4 until disease progression: Administer tafasitamab on Days 1 and 15.
L373: Patients should self-administer lenalidomide capsules at the recommended starting dose of 25 mg daily on Days 1 - 21 of each cycle. Tafasitamab plus lenalidomide in combination are given up to twelve cycles. Lenalidomide dose adaptations are permitted as per prescribing information. Refer to the lenalidomide prescribing information for dosing guidelines and recommendations.
L374: After a maximum of twelve (12) cycles of combination therapy, stop treatment with lenalidomide and continue tafasitamab infusions as single agent on Days 1 and 15 of each 28-day cycle, until disease progression.
L375: DER analysis for efficacy performed for MOR208C203/L-MIND showed that tafasitamab exhibited a flat exposure-response relationship (see section 6.3.2.2).
L376: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L377: The recommended dose and dosing schedule for the intended indication is supported by dose-exposure response analysis for efficacy and safety.
L378: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L379: FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen, which was evaluated in Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) and is supported by popPK and E-R analyses for efficacy and safety.
L380: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L381: In a population PK analysis for tafasitamab (see also below in 0), age (16 to 90 years), body weight, sex, tumor size, disease type, B-cell or absolute lymphocyte counts, anti-drug antibodies, lactate dehydrogenase, serum albumin, hepatic (AST, ALT, ALKP, GGT) and renal function(CrCL) had no clinically significant effect on the PK of tafasitamab. The influence of race and ethnicity is unknown. Lenalidomide co-administration was not found to influence tafasitamab PK in the Population PK analysis.
L382: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L383: No dose adjustments are recommended based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors.
L384: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L385: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustments are recommended based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors. The popPK analyses showed that body weight (40 to 163 kg) has a clinically significant effect on the PK of tafasitamab, which supports body weight-based dosing for tafasitamab. No clinically meaningful differences in the PK of tafasitamab were observed based on age (16 to 90 years), sex, mild to moderate renal impairment (CLcr 30-89 mL/min, estimated by the Cockcroft-Gault equation) and mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN, or total bilirubin 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST). The effect of severe renal impairment to end-stage renal disease (CLcr &lt; 30 mL/min), moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 times ULN and any AST), and race/ethnicity on tafasitamab PK is unknown (although no effect is expected).
L386: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L387: ## The Applicant's Position:
L388: There are no outstanding issues.
L389: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L390: FDA agrees that there are no outstanding issues.
L391: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L392: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L393: ## Data:
L394: **Table 8: Overview Clinical Pharmacology Characteristics Tafasitamab**
L395: General Information
Chemical Structure	Disulfide-linked glycosylated protein tetramer with two identical 451 amino acid heavy chains and two identical 219 amino acid kappa light chains
Fc part: human IgG1/2 with two single amino acid exchanges (S239D and I332E)
Molecular weight: approximately 150 kDa
Mechanism of Action	Tafasitamab binds to the human B-cell surface antigen cluster of differentiation (CD) 19. The engineered Fc part possesses significantly increased binding to Fc gamma receptors (FcγR) leading to enhanced in vitro antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) and antibody-dependent cellular phagocytosis (ADCP). Tafasitamab also mediates direct cytotoxic effects (apoptosis) on tumor cells relative to the unmodified antibody. Tafasitamab does not mediate complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC).
Bioanalysis
L396: Bioanalysis PK	Tafasitamab concentrations in human plasma or serum were quantified by validated ligand binding assays (PK assay). The first-generation PK assay (enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay - ELISA) employed capture with a mouse monoclonal anti-tafasitamab idiotypic antibody and detection with an anti-human IgG Fc antibody. The second-generation PK assay (electrochemiluminescence assay) employed capture with a human monoclonal anti-idiotypic antibody (Fab fragment) and detection with a mouse monoclonal anti-tafasitamab idiotypic antibody. The lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) in undiluted samples was 80 ng/mL and 50 ng/mL in the 1st and 2nd generation PK assay, respectively.
Bioanalysis ADA	Anti-tafasitamab antibodies were determined using a validated homogenous bridging electrochemiluminescence based ligand-binding assay. For the pivotal study L-MIND a Solid-Phase Extraction Followed by Acid Dissociation (SPEAD) approach was added for sample pre-treatment to increase drug tolerance. Study samples were analyzed using a multi-tiered testing approach consisting of a screening, a conformation, a specificity and a titer determination assay. For the pivotal study L-MIND confirmed positive samples were further tested in a validated cell based ADCC neutralizing antibody (NAb) assay.
Pharmacokinetic (PK) Characteristics
Pop-PK Model	For the population PK analysis incl. covariate assessments results from four different clinical studies were used (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202 and MOR208C203). The PK of tafasitamab following IV administration was well characterized by a 2-compartment linear disposition model. A time-dependent clearance term (CL \exp(t^\gamma/(T50^\gamma + t^\gamma))) was included in the model to describe the increasing trough concentrations observed in the L-MIND study (MOR208C203) beyond one year of treatment.
Drug Exposure	Based on the population pharmacokinetic analysis, tafasitamab average serum trough concentrations (\pm standard deviation) in the L-Mind study were determined to be 179 (\pm 53) \mug/mL during weekly intravenous administrations at the 12 mg/kg dose level including an additional dose at Day 4 of Cycle 1 in combination with lenalidomide. During bi-weekly administrations from Cycle 4 onwards, average trough serum concentrations of 153 (\pm 68) \mug/mL were determined. Overall, maximum tafasitamab serum concentrations were determined to be 483 (\pm109)\mug/mL.
Dose Linearity	Dose linearity was assessed in the Phase I dose escalation study XmAb5574-01. For dose levels below 3 mg/kg nonlinear pharmacokinetics were observed in patients with R/R CLL/SLL. Employing dose levels of 3 – 12 mg/kg, AUC and Cmax increased in a dose proportional manner.
Absorption	No bioavailability studies were performed as tafasitamab is administered IV.
Distribution	The total volume of distribution for tafasitamab was determined at 9.3 L.
L397: Elimination	The general clearance of tafasitamab was determined at 0.41 L/day; terminal elimination half-life at 16.9 days. Following long-term observations, tafasitamab clearance was found to decrease over time to a value of 0.19 L/day after two years.
Metabolism	The metabolic pathway of tafasitamab has not been characterized. As a human monoclonal antibody, tafasitamab is expected to be degraded into small peptides and amino acids via catabolic pathways in the same manner as endogenous IgGs.
Excretion	The excretion of tafasitamab has not been studied. Tafasitamab is a human monoclonal IgG1/2 antibody and has a molecular weight of approximately 150 kDa; therefore, intact tafasitamab is unlikely to be filtered by the kidney or excreted in the urine.
Immunogenicity
Incidence	Pre-existing ADA	Overall 17/245 subjects (6.9%) were tested ADA-positive before start of tafasitamab treatment
Treatment-boosted or treatment-emergent ADA	No treatment related boost of pre-existing ADAs, and no treatment-emergent ADAs were observed
Impact on PK, efficacy, safety	The presence of ADAs had no obvious impact on the pharmacokinetics of tafasitamab. Following a descriptive analysis, the presence of ADAs had no obvious impact on the clinical efficacy and safety of tafasitamab.
Pharmacodynamic (PD) characteristics
B-cell depletion	Peripheral B-cell depletion was demonstrated in whole blood samples within clinical studies MOR208C203 (L-MIND), MOR208C201, MOR208C202, MOR208C205 and XmAb5574-01. In most studies, substantial B-cell depletion was observed at the earliest consistently tested time point, Cycle 1 Day 8 (C1D8). The magnitude of the maximal reduction seen at nadir relative to baseline count ranged from -80% (MOR208C201) to -100% (MOR208C203, the pivotal study), exceeding -95% in all but one study (MOR208C201), which had a method related background limitation. The time to reach the nadir ranged from 6 to 16 weeks after treatment start.
QT effects	As tafasitamab is a human monoclonal antibody no "Thorough QT/QTc Study" was performed. Instead, periodic ECG monitoring was implemented in all company-sponsored clinical studies to capture potential ECG changes. Results showed that tafasitamab did not have a clinically relevant effect on cardiac repolarization or result in clinically significant cardiac AEs.
L398: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L399: The Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics program supports the BLA for the intended
L400: indication.
L401: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L402: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. To reflect the intended patient population, FDA updated the incidence and potential effect of immunogenicity to state that no clinically meaningful differences in the PK, efficacy, or safety profile of tafasitamab were observed in 2.5% (2/81) of patients with R/R DLBCL with pre-existing anti-tafasitamab antibodies.
L403: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L404: ## 6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L405: In clinical studies XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202, L-MIND, and MOR208C205, tafasitamab treatment led to a reduction in peripheral B-cells in subjects suffering from CLL, MCL, MZL, ALL and DLBCL with a maximum reduction that generally exceeded -95% vs. baseline. Substantial B-cell depletion was usually observed after 8 days, reaching a median nadir within 6 to 16 weeks after treatment start. The depletion was lasting under continued tafasitamab treatment.
L406: For the dose-exposure-response (DER) analysis for efficacy and safety of tafasitamab, refer to section 6.3.2.2.
L407: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L408: Depletion of peripheral B-cells is consistent with the proposed mode of action of tafasitamab as a mediator of ADCC, ADCP and direct cytotoxicity towards CD19 expressing cells. The dose-exposure response analysis for efficacy provides evidence for effectiveness.
L409: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L410: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on the time course of pharmacodynamics response. The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from pivotal Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) in the target population that showed an ORR of 60% (42% CR, 18% PR) in 80 evaluable patients.
L411: ## 6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L412: The dosing regimen for the combination therapy uses the maximum previously applied monotherapy doses for each agent in R/R DLBCL.
L413: ## **Tafasitamab**
L414: A dose-exposure-response (DER) analysis for efficacy and safety of tafasitamab was performed, with tafasitamab exposure metrics derived by simulation for each subject from the Pop-PK model. The individual exposure metrics were separated into exposure quartiles and analyzed against PFS and ORR, or against treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) and each AE of special interest (AEI). Furthermore, logistic regression analysis was performed in order assess the impact of tafasitamab exposure on safety and efficacy.
L415: ## Exposure-Response Relationship for Clinical Efficacy
L416: A post-hoc assessment of the DER relationship for efficacy (study MOR208L035) was performed for clinical trials MOR208C203/L-MIND, MOR208C201, and XmAb5574-01 with the following conclusions:
L417: - An exposure-response relationship was observed for tafasitamab monotherapy in clinical studies XmAb5574-01 (subjects with R/R CLL/SLL) and MOR208C201 (subjects with R/R NHL/DLBCL, see Figure 7). Within these studies, higher exposure to tafasitamab was associated with higher ORRs and prolonged median PFS.
- In combination with lenalidomide (MOR208C203), tafasitamab exhibited a flat exposure-response relationship (see Figure 7). Based on the results of the exposure-response analysis, increasing exposure to tafasitamab is unlikely to result in higher response rates or longer PFS when tafasitamab is used in combination with lenalidomide.
- Switching the dosing frequency of tafasitamab in combination with LEN from initially Q1W to Q2W, after three treatment cycles, as implemented in MOR208C203, had no pronounced effect on PFS based on this analysis.
L418: **Figure 7: Analysis of Objective Response Versus AUC28 for Clinical Study MOR208C201 (Left, Tafasitamab Monotherapy) and MOR208C203/L-MIND (Right, Tafasitamab in Combination with Lenalidomide)**
L419: Black solid line and grey shaded areas represent logistic model-predicted relationship and 95% confidence interval based on the sensitivity analysis population (N=73 for L-MIND; N=85 for MOR208C201). Quartile borders of AUC28 quartiles are displayed at the bottom. Quartile-wise ORR (red dots) and 95% Clopper-Pearson confidence intervals (red vertical lines) are overlaid for information. Number of responders versus total number of subjects per quartile are displayed at the top/bottom of 95% CIs. Black circles on top and bottom represent actual observations used for regression. Note that only subjects receiving all planned doses during Cycle 1 were included in this analysis in order to avoid bias of an early drop-out leading to low AUC28. Abbreviations: ORR = objective response rate; AUC28 = AUC from 0-28 days. Source: Report MOR208L035 Figure 5 and Figure 11.
L420: ## Exposure-Response Relationship for Clinical Safety
L421: A post-hoc assessment of the DER relationship for safety was performed for clinical trials MOR208C203/L-MIND and MOR208C201. Overall, there were no obvious trends for an increase in AESIs or TEAEs in any System Organ Class (SOC) (including the Blood and Lymphatic Disorders and Cardiac Disorder SOCs) with increasing values for AUC28 or Cmax, as the numbers and percentages of subjects with such events were equally or randomly distributed across the 4 exposure quartiles. This was also confirmed using logistic regression. For details, refer to m2.7.4.2.1.5.2.
L422: ## Lenalidomide
L423: The LEN starting dose was 25 mg daily. This dose was selected on the basis of published data and the recommended starting dose for patients with myeloma and MCL (REVLIMID USPI, 2019). Published studies indicate a consistent and manageable safety profile for this dose of
L424: LEN monotherapy in patients with R/R DLBCL (Wiernik et al., 2008; Witzig et al., 2011; Czuczman et al., 2017). Similar doses (20-25 mg) have also been used when lenalidomide was combined with rituximab (RTX) (Zinzani et al., 2011; Wang et al., 2013; Vose et al., 2013; Ivanov et al., 2014) or R-CHOP (Nowakowski et al., 2011; Nowakowski et al., 2015).
L425: Treatment with LEN is limited to 12 cycles (12 months), based on benefit-risk considerations. In terms of benefit, a similar duration of LEN treatment (12 months) was defined in a previous study in patients with R/R DLBCL (Zinzani et al., 2011) which demonstrated an ORR of 35%, a CR rate of 30%, and 1-year DFS rate 35%, 18-month OS rate 55%. Limiting treatment duration was introduced to avoid further cumulative risk though prolonged exposure to lenalidomide, e.g. the incidence of second primary malignancies with LEN seen in some clinical trials (REVLIMID USPI, 2019).
L426: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L427: The dosing regimen of LEN applied in the MOR208C203 (L-MIND) study is consistent with the USPI in other hemato-oncological indications (REVLIMID USPI, 2019). The duration of LEN therapy (approximately 12 months) is similar to that in many other combination studies including LEN in aggressive and indolent lymphoma and is therefore considered appropriate. The dose and regimen of tafasitamab is supported by exposure-response analysis for efficacy and safety.
L428: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L429: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the proposed dosing regimen is supported by popPK and E-R analyses for efficacy and safety.
L430: ## 6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L431: Intrinsic patient factors for tafasitamab exposure were evaluated during population PK analysis. As determined by stepwise covariate model building, body weight at baseline (WTB) was found to have a significant effect on CL and V. In addition, covariate effects of albumin (ALB) on clearance, sex and of the type of disease (NHL/ALL or CLL/SLL) on central volume were also included in the final Pop-PK model. Covariate effects were moderate in size (5th or 95th percentile of the respective covariate range caused a change of &lt; 50% of the respective primary PK parameter or derived exposure parameter, for most exposure parameters even &lt; 25%, see also Figure 7 for AUC0-28 as an exemplary exposure metric). In a dose/exposure assessment for clinical effectiveness of tafasitamab, (MOR208L035, see also section 6.3.2.2), tafasitamab exhibited a flat exposure-response relationship in combination with lenalidomide in R/R DLBCL subjects (MOR208C203, all subjects dosed at 12 mg/kg). In a dose/exposure response assessment for safety of tafasitamab, no obvious increase in safety events could be observed with increasing exposure.
L432: Thus, based on exposure-response analysis and based on the moderate size of PK covariate effects, the latter were considered to be clinically not relevant and not warranting dose adjustments for subpopulations.
L433: **Table 9: Summary of Covariate Assessment in the Population PK Analysis**
L434: Covariates investigated within the Population PK analysis	Results of Population PK analysis
Intrinsic Factors	Demographics: sex, age, and body weight	Body weight at baseline (WTB) was found to have a statistically significant effect on tafasitamab CL and central volume of distribution (V). Other statistically significant covariates identified were serum albumin (inverse correlation with clearance), sex (lower V in females), and disease type (subjects with NHL or ALL had a lower V than subjects with CLL/SLL).
The influence of race and ethnicity on the pharmacokinetics of tafasitamab could not be tested due to limited diversity in the patient population evaluated.
Disease-specific / disease-related: disease type (ALL, CLL/SLL, NHL), NHL subtypes (FL, MCL, DLBCL, other indolent lymphomas (iNHL)), tumor size (sum of area of indicator lesions, SPD), lactate dehydrogenase (LDH), cancer stage, ECOG performance status, B-cell count and absolute lymphocyte count (ALC).
Immunological: presence/absence of anti-drug antibodies (ADA)
Clinical chemistry: total protein, albumin, liver enzymes (ALT, AST, GGT, ALKP), creatinine clearance
Renal or Hepatic Impairment	No significant effect of renal function (creatinine clearance), or hepatic function (AST, ALT, ALKP, GGT) on tafasitamab PK could be detected.

L436: Source: Population PK Report MOR208L032 Figure 15

Note that the error bars (in blue) represent uncertainty in the population estimates, not IIV. Simulated exposure metrics (top row, in black: median and 95% CI) are derived from the post-hoc PK parameter estimates and thus include IIV.

L440: ## The Applicant's Position:
L441: No dose adjustments are recommended based on intrinsic patient factors.
L442: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L443: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The effect of severe renal impairment to end-stage renal disease (CLcr &lt; 30 mL/min) and moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 x ULN, any AST) is unknown (although no effect is expected).
L444: FCGR (Fc Fragment of IgG Receptor) Polymorphisms and Tumor Response: The Applicant explored whether germline FCGR2A and FCGR3A polymorphisms affect the response to tafasitamab (Appendix 19.4). The analyses were limited by small sample size and missing data and no conclusions could be drawn. More data are required to assess the role of FCGR polymorphisms on tafasitamab response. The applicant should continue to conduct FCGR genotyping in ongoing or planned trials.
L445: ## **6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L446: No dedicated food-drug or drug-drug interaction studies were performed.
L447: ## The Applicant's Position:
L448: As tafasitamab is given as an i.v. infusion, there is no potential for food effects on bioavailability (BA).
L449: As tafasitamab does not undergo extensive hepatic metabolism and the pharmacological modes of action do not offer the potential for a direct (anti-)inflammatory activity possibly modifying Cytochrome P450 function, no specific drug-drug interaction studies were performed.
L450: Considering the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide, the potential impact of lenalidomide on the PK of tafasitamab was evaluated during covariate analysis of the population PK analysis (extrinsic factor) with no effect of lenalidomide on tafasitamab PK.
L451: As LEN is reported to be excreted &gt; 90% without being metabolized via renal filtration and is not subject to any Phase 1 or 2 metabolism, no risk for a potential drug-drug interaction of tafasitamab on the lenalidomide PK is expected.
L452: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L453: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L460: ## 7 Sources of Clinical Data
L461: ## 7.1. Table of Clinical Studies

L474: ## The Applicant's Position:
L475: All studies listed above are relevant to the evaluation of efficacy and/or safety of tafasitamab to support the proposed indication.
L476: Other studies (ongoing or planned studies MOR208C107, [REDACTED](b)(4)) are not pertinent for the current evaluation of efficacy and safety of tafasitamab to support the proposed indication (no data available).
L477: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L478: Study MOR203 was the pivotal study supporting efficacy of tafasitamab in patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL. Studies MORC201 and MORC206 are relevant to the evaluation of efficacy. The safety evaluation focused on the pivotal trial MOR208C203 supported by a pooled evaluation of tafasitamab monotherapy in trials MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and XmAb5574-01.
L479: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L480: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L481: Data supporting the overall efficacy of 12 mg/kg IV tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide (LEN) in patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL) are based on 3 studies, MOR208C203 (L-MIND), MOR208C206 (RE-MIND), and MOR208C201 (Table 10). MOR208C203 studied the efficacy of tafasitamab in the intended regimen (in combination with LEN), the proposed dosing regimen, the proposed indication (adult patients with R/R DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for, [REDACTED](b)(4) autologous stem cell transplant) and utilizes the intended commercial drug formulation. The contribution of each of the single agents in the combination was determined via 1) MOR208C201, which demonstrated the activity of tafasitamab monotherapy in DLBCL patients; and 2) MOR208C206, in which the efficacy of LEN monotherapy was statistically assessed in a matching observational cohort to the combination in MOR208C203 study
L482: ## 8.1.1. MOR208C203, L-MIND
L483: ## Trial Design
L484: ## The Applicant's Description:
L485: This is an ongoing Phase 2, single-arm, open-label, multicenter study conducted at sites in the
L486: U.S. and Europe designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of tafasitamab + LEN in patients with R/R DLBCL. The study included patients who had relapsed after or were refractory to at least 1, but no more than 3 previous systemic regimens (one therapy line must have included a CD20-targeted therapy such as rituximab [RTX]), and who were not candidates for HDC and subsequent ASCT (or relapsed after ASCT). The study design is depicted in Figure 9.
L487: **Figure 9: Trial Design of Clinical Study MOR208C203**
L488: **EOT=end of treatment; FU=follow-up; LEN=lenalidomide; R-R DLBCL=relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma; SD=stable disease.**
L489: Tafasitamab was administered via IV infusion at a dose of 12.0 mg/kg. For the first 3 cycles (Cycles 1-3), tafasitamab was administered on Day 1, Day 8, Day 15, and Day 22 of each 28-day cycle, with an additional loading dose on Day 4 of Cycle 1. Thereafter, tafasitamab was administered on Day 1 and Day 15 of each 28-day cycle. LEN was self-administered by patients daily with a starting dose of 25 mg orally on Days 1 to 21 of each 28-day cycle. LEN was administered in combination with tafasitamab up to 12 cycles in total. In patients with at least SD after 12 cycles of combination therapy, tafasitamab was administered until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity or discontinuation for any other reason, whichever came first.
L490: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L491: FDA agrees with the above description. Further details of the trial are provided below.
L492: ## Patient population
L493: ## Inclusion criteria (summarized)
L494: - Age &gt;18 years
- Diagnosis of DLBCL not otherwise specified, T cell/histiocyte rich large B-cell lymphoma, Epstein-Barr positive DLBCL of the elderly, Grade 3B follicular lymphoma, composite lymphoma with a DLBCL component with a subsequent DLBCL relapse. Diagnosis was
L495: according the Revised European American Lymphoma/World Health Organization classification. Patients with transformation from previous low grade lymphoma to DLBCL with a subsequent relapse of DLBCL were also eligible.
L496: - Fresh tumor for central pathology review was to be provided, if not possible, archival tissue \le 3 years prior must be available.
- Patients must haveRelapsed and/or refractory disease
- At least one bidimensional measurable disease site
- Received at least 1 but no more than 3 previous systemic regimens for the treatment of DLBCL. A CD20 targeted therapy must have been included in one therapy line
- ECOG performance status of 0 to 2
- Patients not considered eligible, or unwilling to proceed with, intensive salvage therapy including ASCT. Documentation of the reason was to be provided.
- Laboratory values at screening including:ANC \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/L (unless due to bone marrow involvement)
- Platelet count \ge 90 \times 10^9/L (unless due to bone marrow involvement)
- Total serum bilirubin \le 2.5 \times ULN unless Gilbert's syndrome or liver involvement by lymphoma then total bilirubin \le 5 \times ULN
- ALT, AST and alkaline phosphatase \le 3 \times ULN or &lt; 5 \times ULN if documented liver involvement
- Serum creatinine clearance must be \ge60 mL/min using Cockcroft Gault formula
- Able to receive adequate prophylaxis for thromboembolic events
- Give written informed consent
L497: ## Exclusion criteria (summarized)
L498: - Other types of lymphoma including primary mediastinal (thymic) large B-cell or Burkitt lymphoma
- Primary refractory DLBCL
- History of double/triple hit genetics with detection of MYC with BCL2 and/or BCL6 translocations defined by FISH. MYC, BCL2, BCL6 testing prior to enrollment was not required.
- Patient within 14 days prior to first dose:Not discontinued CD20 targeted therapy, chemotherapy, radiotherapy or other lymphoma specific therapy
- Major surgery or significant trauma
- Received a live vaccine
- Required parenteral antimicrobial therapy for an active infection
- Patients who:Have not received from toxic effects of prior therapy
- Previously treated with CD19 targeted therapy or IMiDs
L499: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
## Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
L500: - Undergone ASCT within 3 months of signing the informed consent. Patients with a history of ASCT must have full hematologic recovery.
- Previous allogeneic transplantation
- History of deep vein thrombosis/embolism or at a high risk for thromboembolic event and not able to take prophylaxis
- Patients with a medical history of:Positive hepatitis B and/or hepatitis C serology
- Known seropositivity for HIV
- CNS lymphoma involvement
- Clinically significant cardiovascular, CNS and/or other systemic disease that prohibit trial participation
- History of rare hereditary problems of galactose malabsorption
- Severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 3 mg/dL), jaundice unless due to Gilbert's syndrome or documented liver involvement by lymphoma
L501: Treatment was administered as stated above. The investigator was to decide if the patient should continue therapy in case of disease progression.
L502: Prior to proceeding with a new cycle, the patient was to have met the following criteria:
L503: - ANC \ge 1.000/mm3 (unless due to bone marrow involvement)
- Platelets \ge 50000/mm3 (unless due to bone marrow involvement)
- No serious organ or other toxicity present
L504: For this trial primary refractory disease was defined as disease progressing in the course of the first line of treatment according to International Working Group response criteria and/or a response of less than a PR to first line treatment or disease recurrence/progression within \le6 months from the completion of first line treatment. Disease refractory to last treatment was defined as less than a partial response to the most recent therapy.
L505: Relapsed/progression/recurrent disease was defined as any new lesions or increase by \ge 50% according to International Working Group response criteria.
L506: ## **Dose Modifications:**

L520: ## Study Endpoints
L521: ## The Applicant's Description:
L522: The primary efficacy endpoint of this study was Best Objective Response Rate (ORR), defined as the proportion of complete and partial responders (ORR = CR + PR), as assessed by Independent Radiology/Clinical Review Committee (IRC) according to the International Working Group revised response criteria for malignant lymphoma (Cheson et al., 2007).
L523: Additional efficacy endpoints included:
L524: - Disease control rate (DCR), defined as the proportion of patients having CR or PR or SD (DCR = ORR + SD);
- Duration of response (DoR), defined as the time between the initial time point of tumor response and the first time point where a change in response to progression or relapse was detailed;
- Progression-free survival (PFS), defined as the time between first study drug dosing and tumor progression or death from any cause, whichever occurs first;
- Time to progression (TTP), defined as the time from first study drug dosing until time of progression;
- Overall survival (OS), defined as time from first study drug dosing to the date of death;
- Time to next treatment (TTNT).
L525: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L526: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints. The endpoints that FDA used to determine the benefit of tafa+LEN in the context of the single arm trial are ORR and DOR. The primary efficacy endpoint, as prespecified, was to be based on an independent, central radiographic review. Two independent radiologists reviewed the imaging findings with adjudication of discrepant responses. The disease assessments incorporated review by a clinical reviewer to determine the overall disease status.
L527: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L528: ## The Applicant's Description:
L529: For the determination of a suitable sample size, it was assumed that the combination treatment could improve the ORR of LEN from a value of 20% (under monotherapy) to 35% (under combination therapy). For an exact binomial test with a two-sided significance level of 5% and a power of 85% the calculated sample size was 73 patients. With an assumed drop-out rate of 10%, a total sample size of about 80 patients was estimated.
L530: The initial SAP Version 1.0 was amended after DCO for primary analysis but before database lock of the primary analysis (April 18, 2019), resulting in SAP Version 2.0 (April 8, 2019).
L531: SAP Version 2.0 was subject to an addendum (Addendum 1.0) after the database lock of the primary analysis. Changes to the SAP versions are summarized in the following table:
L532: **Table 11: Summary of Changes to SAP**
L533: SAP Version	Date	Changes to the previous SAP version
Version 1.0	November 09, 2015	Not applicable
Version 2.0	April 08, 2019	Paragraphs were rephrased to detail analysis strategies.
Multiple subgroup and sensitivity analyses were added.

Addendum 1.0 to SAP Version 2.0	August 09, 2019	Efficacy and safety analyses were added.
L534: Primary and secondary endpoints were not affected by these modifications, and the analysis strategies as initially specified in SAP Version 1.0 remained unchanged.
L535: The data cut-off (DCO) date for the analysis of MOR208C203 (i.e. approximately 12 months after the last patient has been enrolled) was Nov 30, 2018. The full analysis set (FAS) was defined as all patients who received \ge 1 dose of tafasitamab and \ge 1 one dose of LEN.
L536: For the primary endpoint (ORR), the number and percentage of patients classified as having best overall response of CR or PR as determined by IRC with 95% confidence limits (using the
L537: Clopper-Pearson exact method) was calculated. DCR (ORR+SD) was evaluated using the same methods as ORR. For the time-to-event endpoints DoR, TTP, PFS, OS, and TTNT, Kaplan-Meier methodology was applied. With the exception of OS and TTNT, all endpoints were analyzed based on both IRC and investigator response assessments. No formal statistical hypothesis testing was performed.
L538: Additional sensitivity assessments were conducted on the per protocol population (PPS), as well as on the FAS based on the Investigator assessment, and excluding patients with no post-baseline assessment of response.
L539: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L540: FDA agrees with the Applicant's general description. The L-MIND study was designed to rule out an ORR of 20%. The populations for analysis were defined as:
L541: - The full analysis set was all patients who received at least one dose of tafasitamab and one dose of lenalidomide.
- The safety analysis set was all patients who received at least 1 dose of tafasitamab or lenalidomide.
- The per protocol set was all patients in the full analysis set without any major protocol non-compliances. Patients without any post-baseline assessment of DLBCL response or having major protocol deviations were to be excluded from the per protocol set.
L542: The primary analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint was based on the full analysis set. To address the potential for outcome-dependent sampling where eligible patients who initiated study therapy may be more likely to respond than eligible patients who did not initiate study therapy, the Agency extended FAS to include one patient who was dosed with tafasitamab but not lenalidomide, hereafter referred to in this document as the enrolled set. As with FAS, any patient in the enrolled set without disease assessments are classified as non-responders.
L543: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L544: ## The Applicant's Description:
L545: The initial protocol was amended 3 times prior to the DCO for the primary analysis. These changes are not expected to have had an impact on the integrity of the trial or interpretation of the results. Key changes were as follows:
L546: - Amendment 1 (May 27, 2015): Amendment 1 included updates to correct minor inconsistencies and organizational changes made since release of the original protocol and updates/clarification made to eligibility criteria, patient compliance, and safety assessments.
- Amendment 2 (June 27, 2016): Changes were made to the eligibility criteria to allow up to three prior lines for DLBCL treatment (previously two prior lines), set an upper age
L547: limit on treatment (80 years), as well as to provide additional specifications for other inclusion/exclusion criteria. The definition of primary refractory DLBCL was revised to less than a PR to first-line therapy or progression within 6 months from completion of first-line therapy and removed the need to have DLBCL relapse/progression after at least 3 months from completion of prior CD20-containing therapy. This definition of primary refractory DLBCL matched the commonly applied period (6 months) for determining rituximab refractoriness, and was consistent with the scientific advice received from regulatory agencies.

- Amendment 3 (Oct 23, 2017): The primary change involved extension of tafasitamab therapy beyond Cycle 24 until disease progression, based on the decision of the treating investigator.
L548: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L549: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the protocol amendments.
L550: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L551: ## Patient Disposition
L552: A total of 81 patients were enrolled, of whom 80 (98.8%) received both tafasitamab and LEN. One patient received tafasitamab only and was excluded from the FAS. Overall, 30 of the 81 patients enrolled (37.0%) successfully completed the combination treatment phase on both study drugs (12 cycles). Fifty-one out of the 81 patients enrolled (63.0%) did not complete the combination treatment phase (Cycle 1 to Cycle 12), primarily due to progressive disease (32 patients, 39.5%). Ten patients did not complete the combination phase due to an adverse event, two due to patient withdrawal, and one due to other reasons. The primary reason for discontinuation of tafasitamab monotherapy beyond Cycle 12 was progressive disease (4 patients), followed by adverse event (1 patient), and patient withdrawal (1 patient). Twenty eight (34.6%) patients were still on treatment with tafasitamab monotherapy at the primary DCO of Nov 2018.
L553: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L554: A substantial number of patients were able to complete the combination phase and continued tafasitamab monotherapy beyond 12 months. The most common cause of discontinuation of study drug was disease progression.
L555: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L556: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L557: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L558: Key protocol deviations included deviations associated with prohibited concomitant medication (6.2%), informed consent forms (25.9%), eligibility criteria (24.7%), laboratory assessment (35.8%), procedure or test (76.5%), study drug and treatment (38.3%), visit schedule and assessment (1.2%), and other (27.2%). Three protocol deviations in the FAS were considered major protocol violations, leading to patient exclusion from the PPS. Two patients were excluded due to prohibited concomitant medication use, and one patient was excluded due to failing an inclusion criterion [PET-positive measurable disease could not be confirmed as a part of screening assessments].
L559: ## The Applicant's Position:
L560: The most frequently reported protocol violations were those relating to study procedures or tests. No patient was excluded from the FAS due to a protocol violation. Although a total of 191 protocol deviations occurred, none materially altered the assessment of safety or efficacy.
L561: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L562: There was high rate of protocol deviations reported in the L-MIND trial. Of the 81 patients, a total of 73 patients had at least 1 major protocol deviation. The categories of major protocol deviations are displayed in Table 12.
L563: An Information Request was sent to the Applicant regarding the high rate of protocol deviations on the L-MIND trial. The Applicant noted a conservative approach to protocol deviations and a potential over-reporting of deviations. The majority of the deviations were considered by the Applicant to not impact the integrity of the trial data. Protocol deviations and violations were also noted during the remote OSI inspections See Section 4.1. for reviewer comments regarding protocol deviations. Patients without any post-baseline assessment of DLBCL response or having major protocol deviations were to be excluded from the per protocol set.

L567: ## **Demographic Characteristics**
L568: The majority of patients were male (53.8%) and white (90.0%). The median age was 72.0 years (min, max: 41, 86); the majority (71.3%, 57/80) were ≥65 years of age.

L574: ## The Applicant's Position:
L575: Overall, the patient population enrolled in this study was representative of the proposed target population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL in US, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for or refuse, ASCT.
L576: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L577: The Agency agrees with the Applicants demographic characteristics of the patient population. However, Table 13 characterized patients in the enrolled set (n=81), not patients in FAS (n=80). FDA notes that the median age of the patients enrolled, 72 years, is higher than the median age of patients diagnosed with DLBCL in the US population reflecting the population who are likely ineligible to receive autologous stem cell transplantation due to age or comorbidities.
L578: FDA does not agree with the statement that the enrolled patients were representative of the proposed target population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL in the US for the following reasons: 1) the precise definition (taking into consideration the eligibility criteria and other comorbidities) of the target population was never specified and 2) there was no random selection of patients from the target population into the L-MIND trial. For example, the L-MIND study consisted mostly of Whites. Furthermore, patients in clinical practice may have more comorbidities that may not be reflected in the L-MIND trial.
L579: The patient population for the MOR208C203 was 88.9% white, 2.5% Asian, missing 7.4% and 1.2% other. A total of 6 patients in the L-MIND trial were enrolled in the U.S. As a result of a lack of diversity in the patient population a PMC will be issued to further evaluate the efficacy and safety of tafasitamab in the underrepresented racial subgroups. Refer to Section 13.

L586: ## The Applicant's Position:
L587: Overall, the patient population enrolled in this study was representative of the proposed target population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for or refuse, ASCT.
L588: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L589: The presentation of baseline disease characteristics in Table 14 is not entirely accurate. As with Table 13, the results pertain to the enrolled set (n = 81), not FAS (n = 80). With respect to elevated LDH in the enrolled set, 36 (44%) did not have elevated LDH at baseline while 45 (56%) had elevated LDH at baseline. FDA does not agree with the statement that the patients in the L-MIND study were representative of the target population as the precise definition of the target population was never specified and there was no random selection of patients from the target population into the L-MIND study. Additionally, there were limited representation of other racial subgroups other than whites.
L590: Although patients with primary refractory disease were enrolled in the L-MIND trial, they should have been excluded as this was an exclusion criterion. Per the protocol, the demographics for the primary refractory patient population will not be presented in the USPI. However, the FDA notes that patients with primary refractory disease have a very poor prognosis and have limited effective treatment options. The median time since last discontinuation of last DLBCL treatment or ASCT was 9 months reflects the treatment paradigm for DLBCL therapies where treatment with combination chemoimmunotherapy is stopped after a defined number of treatment cycles. The median time to initiation of tafasitamab and lenalidomide since last progression or relapse was 1.4 months (FDA analysis) which indicates that the L-MIND trial enrolled a patient population with an immediate need for treatment.
L591: Patients on trial MOR208C203 were enrolled and treated based on local pathology, however, retrospective central pathology review was done. Based on central pathology a total of 9 patients were non-DLBCL and 2 were unknown. Table 15 lists the patients with confirmed DLBCL and Table 16 lists the patients who were confirmed with a lymphoma type other than DLBCL. Patients with a diagnosis other than DLBCL were excluded from the efficacy analysis for the U.S. Prescribing Information. FDA also notes that DLBCL not otherwise specified was the predominant subtype in the L-MIND study. In response to an IR, the Applicant provided data from 9 patients who had a previous diagnosis of indolent lymphoma transformed to DLBCL. The indication for the U.S. Prescribing Information will reflect the lymphoma subtypes enrolled.

L598: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L599: Compliance was summarized per single infusion (for tafasitamab) and per cycle (for both tafasitamab and LEN). Compliance was reached if \ge 80\% and \le 120\% of the dose was administered. Planned dose reductions (LEN) and planned interruptions (LEN, tafasitamab) due to medical reasons were accounted for the determination of treatment compliance. The median compliance for both tafasitamab and LEN for each cycle was 100% until Cycle 34.
L600: All patients in the FAS reported the use of concomitant medications. The most frequently (\ge 20\%) reported concomitant medications were paracetamol (45.0%), filgrastim (38.8%), allopurinol (32.5%), acetylsalicylic acid (30.0%), enoxaparin sodium ([28.8%], Bactrim and ciprofloxacin (26.3%), acyclovir, amoxicillin/clavulanic acid, and furosemide (23.8%) and levofloxacin (21.3%). Protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited medications were reported in 5 (6.2%) patients.
L601: ## The Applicant's Position:
L602: Treatment compliance for tafasitamab was ensured as it was infused under the direct supervision of study personnel. Also for LEN, a high overall compliance to the treatment as prescribed by the protocol was recorded.
L603: Due to the patient population (primarily elderly with advanced disease), the proportion of patients taking concomitant medications is expected. There were a few cases of prohibited concomitant medication usage, the majority of which were associated with corticosteroid (prednisone) use. Two of these prohibited concomitant medication deviations were considered major deviations, resulting in patients' exclusion from the PPS (one patient had concomitant administration of corticosteroids in excess of a dose specified as per protocol; one patient received radiotherapy due to disease progression but did not stop study drug as per protocol).
L604: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L605: The U.S. Prescribing Information will reflect the use of premedication's to minimize infusion-related reactions, especially during the first three infusions.
L606: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L607: The best ORR based on IRC was 60% (95% CI: 48.4, 70.8), and CR rate was 42.5% (95% CI: 31.5, 54.1); 30/34 CRs were PET confirmed. A best response of PR was observed in 17.5% (95% CI: 9.9, 27.6), SD in 11 (13.8%), PD in 13 (16.3%) patients, and 8 (10.0%) patients were not evaluable. There was an 88.2% concordance between IRC and Investigator assessments in the FAS for best ORR. A sensitivity analysis on the per protocol set (PPS, N=70), excluding patients with no post-baseline assessment of response (n=8) or who received prohibited concomitant therapy (n=2) or did not have measurable disease at baseline (n=1), demonstrated robustness of the results with an ORR of 65.7% (95% CI: 53.4, 76.7) and CR for 33 (47.1%) patients.

L611: ## The Applicant's Position:
L612: 18.5% of patients had primary refractory DLBCL, 50% of patients had \ge two prior lines of therapy, and over 10% of patients had relapsed after HSCT. Thus, the patient population had disease that was both heavily pre-treated and demonstrated decreased sensitivity to standard of care chemotherapy regimens. The combination of tafasitamab and LEN demonstrated substantial efficacy in ASCT-ineligible R/R DLBCL patients with only limited therapeutic options.
L613: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L614: Not all patients in the L-MIND study had confirmed DLBCL at baseline. Out of the 81 patients in the enrolled set, 71 had confirmed DLBCL, hereafter referred to as the confirmed enrolled set. During the review, further information was requested from the Applicant regarding the adjudication of the response determination. As a result of this review by the FDA and the Applicant, it was determined an error was made as one patient who was previously considered to be a CR should have been a best response of SD. This will be reflected in the USPI. Among the 71 patients in the confirmed enrolled set, 39 (55%; 95% CI:43%, 67%) responded:
L615: - CR. 26 (37%; 95% CI: 25%, 49%)
- PR. 13 (18%; 95% CI: 10%, 29%)
L616: Note that results per FDA's analyses are similar to those presented by the Applicant. Note also that 1 of the 71 patients in the confirmed enrolled set was dosed with tafasitamab but not with lenalidomide. Per the SAP, the primary analysis set was FAS which was defined as consisting of patients who were dosed with both drugs at least once. This suggests that FDA's analyses should include only 70 patients in the denominator. However, as stated earlier, excluding patients who met eligibility criteria but were not treated is susceptible to outcome-dependent sampling in the sense that patients not dosed has the potential to be less likely to respond than those who were dosed. Therefore, FDA's analysis uses all 71 eligible patients in the denominator.

L627: a Kaplan Meier estimates
L628: With a median follow-up of 17.3 months, median PFS was 12.1 (95% CI: 5.7, NR) months. With a median follow-up of 19.6 months, the median for OS was not reached (95% CI: 18.3, NR). The Kaplan-Meier estimates of achieving OS at 12, 18, and 24 months were 73.7%, 63.6% and 56.6%, respectively. The median TTP was 16.2 months and the median TTNT was 15.4 months. Overall, 59 of 80 patients demonstrated a clinical benefit, 48 were responders and 11 showed SD as best response, resulting in a DCR of 73.8% (95% CI: 62.7, 83.0).
L629: ## The Applicant's Position:
L630: Secondary endpoints corroborate findings from the primary efficacy analysis, showing that the combination of tafasitamab and LEN demonstrates substantial efficacy in NTE patients with R/R DLBCL. DoR results show that patients who achieve a CR have a high probability to remain in remission for an extended period of time, with 93.2% of patients in response at 18 months. In contrast, patients achieving only a PR as best response tend to progress within the next 6 months. The time-to-event endpoints from this single-arm study are considered descriptive.
L631: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L632: There were 39 responders among DLBCL-confirmed patients. Estimated median DOR (in months) and its associated 95% confidence interval among these 39 responders is 21.7 (95% CI: 21.7, NE). The durability of response represents a meaningful treatment benefit in this patient population. However, the FDA notes that the durability of response was primarily observed in patients who achieved a complete response.
L633: FDA considers time-to-event endpoints such as PFS, OS, TTP, and TTNT in the context of single trials difficult to interpret. As noted in the Efficacy Endpoint section above, FDA evaluates
L634: ## Subgroup Analysis
L635: Analyses of ORR and other efficacy parameters were performed for several subpopulations, (i.e., IPI score, gender, RTX refractoriness, refractoriness to last prior treatment line, primary refractoriness, prior ASCT, number of prior treatment lines, and age, as well as cell of origin).
L636: The combination treatment with tafasitamab and LEN had consistent activity in patients irrespective of their age, or whether primary refractory, refractory to their last prior therapy, or refractory to RTX.
L637: **Figure 14: Forest Plot of Best Objective Response for Subgroups (IRC Evaluation) FAS (MOR208C203)**
L638: Abbreviations: CI = confidence interval, CR = complete response, FAS = full analysis set, IPI = International Prognostic Index, N# = number of patients within subgroup category, IRC = Independent Radiology/Clinical Review Committee; n = number of patients with objective response; ORR = objective response rate, PR = partial response.
L639: ## **Applicant's Position:**
L640: For the primary endpoint ORR, consistent results were observed across most patient subgroups of interest, such as those based on age, gender, primary refractory disease, and refractoriness to the last prior therapy.
L641: A trend towards a differential effect with overlapping CIs was observed in the subgroups based on IPI, prior therapy lines, cell of origin. As is typical in DLBCL and many other malignancies, there was a trend for higher response rates in patients who were less heavily pre-treated. However, it is difficult to draw robust conclusions from these results, given the small patient numbers.
L642: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L643: FDA agrees with the Applicant that statements about trends in subgroups are difficult to substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes in the L-MIND study are small, and in general lead to estimates that are unstable. The following table restricts ORR subgroup results to the 71 patients who were DLBCL-confirmed. FDA notes that the responses were consistent across the
L644: cell of origin subgroups. However, as previously stated the small sample size precludes a meaningful conclusion regarding efficacy in the subgroups.
