L664: ## 8.1.3. MOR208C206, RE-MIND
L665: ## Trial Design
L666: ## The Applicant's Description:
L667: This observational, retrospective, comparative cohort study MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) was conducted to generate a matched control cohort for MOR208C203 (L-MIND) to isolate the contribution of tafasitamab to the combination. The study was designed and conducted to minimize the potential bias for differences in patient populations, efficacy assessment techniques, definition of endpoints, source selection, and differences in follow-up time.
L668: Site and patient selection criteria were pre-specified in the study protocol. The same eligibility criteria as in the MOR208C203 study were applied to this study with respect to the histological subtypes, number of prior therapy lines, prior therapy types allowed, and ineligibility to receive ASCT.
L669: Eligibility criteria used in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) but not for MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) were justified with the following reasons:
L670: 1) Inclusion criterion 'ECOG score 0-2': ECOG score was expected to be not frequently documented on patient's medical records in routine clinical care.
L671: 2) Laboratory parameters, serology and patient's consent to receive anticoagulation therapy: Criteria deemed unnecessary in the context of a retrospective data collection. However, elevated LDH levels, Neutropenia and Anaemia were included as baseline covariates for cohort balancing.
L672: 3) Exclusion criterion 'patients with primary refractory disease': The definition of primary refractory DLBCL was revised during the L-MIND conduct resulting in enrollment and treatment of 15 patients with primary refractory disease. Consequently, primary refractory disease was not an exclusion criterion for MOR208C206 and included as baseline covariate for cohort balancing. Data were collected retrospectively from health records of patients in either real world, compassionate use and/or clinical trial settings. GCP principles were followed, patient confidentiality was maintained and the data collected underwent medical review.
L673: The statistical analyses were prospectively defined in the SAP. The effectiveness of LEN monotherapy was compared with the efficacy outcomes of tafasitamab+LEN combination therapy in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) after the cohorts were balanced on nine relevant baseline patient and disease characteristics via 1:1 *nearest neighbor* matching based on estimated propensity scores:
L674: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
## Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
L675: - Primary refractory (Y/N)
- Refractory to last treatment (Y/N)
- Prior ASCT (autologous stem cell transplant) (Y/N)
- Number of prior therapies (1 vs. 2/3)
- Age (&lt;70 vs &gt;=70)
- Ann Arbor Stage (I/II vs III/IV)
- Increased LDH (Y/N)
- Neutropenia (Y/N)
- Anemia (Y/N)
L676: Of the 490 patients who were enrolled in the observational study, 348 who fulfilled the eligibility criteria and had a sufficient follow-up (6-months) were included in the full analysis set for efficacy (FAS). Of these, 218 patients who had a LEN starting dose of 25 mg/day were included in the FAS25 analysis set. Of those, 140 patients had complete data available on 9 baseline covariates, and hence were used as a patient pool for 1:1 ePS based matching with the L-MIND cohort. For patients in the L-MIND study the same rules were applied. 5 patients out of the 81 enrolled patients were excluded from the comparative analysis (reasons: 1 patient did not fulfill the I/E criteria [had received 4 prior lines of therapy], 1 patient was not treated with lenalidomide and 3 patients discontinued without any post-baseline response assessment or recorded death).
L677: Thus, 76 patients from each cohort were matched 1:1 and included for analysis of the efficacy endpoints in the MAS25, the primary analysis set (Figure 15).
L678: Figure 15: Patient Flow (MOR208C206)
L679: For a subset of patients in the RE-MIND observational cohort, tumor response assessments were validated by independent reviewers (1 radiologist and 1 hemato-oncologist) based on tumor imaging and clinical data in order to evaluate whether response assessments collected in the observational cohort were accurate representations of patient response. Concordance between the independent tumor response assessments and the physician-recorded tumor response assessments in the validation set was measured by percent agreement.
L680: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L681: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the design of the RE-MIND observational study. However, whether the 9 covariates were "relevant" with respect to ORR heterogeneity and whether they represent a sufficient set in terms of confounder control are still open questions.
L682: The accuracy of the concordance results from the validation study is unclear given that the validation set was small. For example, there were only 22 investigator-assessed responders. Additionally, the concordance results have no direct connection to estimation as the Applicant did not provide a mechanism for translating the concordance results to the estimation of ORR based on IRC assessment.
L683: ## **Study Endpoints**
L684: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L685: The primary efficacy endpoint of this study was best overall/objective response rate ORR, defined as patients with CR or PR as best response achieved at any time between index date and date of initiation of a new anti-DLBCL medication or death.
L686: Additional efficacy endpoints included;
L687: - DoR, defined as the elapsed time between the date of first documented response (CR or PR) and the date of event defined as the first documented progression or death (from any cause).
- PFS, defined as the time from the index date to the date of tumor progression or death from any cause.
- OS, defined as the time from the index date until death from any cause (documented by the date of death).
- EFS, defined as the time from the index date to the date of disease progression, initiation of a new non-study anti-DLBCL medication, or death from any cause, whatever comes first.
- TTNT, defined as the time from index date to the start of next anti-DLBCL therapy (therapies include- medication, surgery or radiotherapy for any reason including disease progression, treatment toxicity and patient preference) or death due to any cause,
L688: whatever comes first.
L689: - Disease Control Rate (CR+PR+SD)
L690: Subgroups regarding age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last therapy line, number of prior therapy lines, primary refractoriness, and prior ASCT were evaluated for best ORR, CR, PFS, and OS.
L691: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L692: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description. Due to inherent differences in data elicitation between clinical trials and clinical practice with respect to what are being measured, how accurately they are being measured, and when they are measured, FDA considers information from time-to-event endpoints such as OS, PFS, EFS, and TTNT difficult to interpret and not relevant to the consideration of the contribution of effect question.
L693: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L694: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L695: The first objective of this study was to characterize the effectiveness of LEN monotherapy in the treatment of R/R DLBCL patients. For this purpose, all data of the observational cohort were summarized using appropriate statistics and tabular overviews. The second objective of this study was to compare the effectiveness of LEN monotherapy with the efficacy outcomes from tafasitamab-LEN combination therapy. For this purpose, the following analysis considerations were applied:
L696: - ePS-based *nearest neighbor* (NN) 1:1 matching methodology was utilized to balance cohorts (observational and MOR208C203 cohorts) using the following baseline covariates to estimate the propensity score: age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last therapy line, number of previous lines of therapy, history of primary refractoriness, prior ASCT, neutropenia, anemia, and elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH).
- Various analysis populations for the comparative analysis were defined based on the patient's follow-up time, LEN starting dose, and cohort balancing approach to address potential selection bias. The primary analysis set was the Nearest Neighbour (NN) 1:1 Matched Analysis Set of patients with a LEN starting dose of 25mg/day (MAS25).
- The primary endpoint, ORR, was estimated using logistic regression. The resulting odds ratio and its associated 95% CI were estimated. The primary analysis was based on Fisher's exact test and was performed on the MAS25.
- A sensitivity analysis was performed to achieve a higher degree of balance in a subgroup of patients by applying a caliper to the 1:1 NN matching algorithm resulting in 2 x 68 patients in the MAS25_Cal analysis population.
- McNemar's conditional test for paired data and conditional logistic regression were conducted as sensitivity analyses for ORR, with p-values reported associated with the
L697: treatment cohort effect. All time-to-event endpoints were analyzed using standard Kaplan-Meier methodology.
L698: - A sensitivity analysis was performed based on a *Balancing Overlap Weights* approach to balance cohorts using only the FAS and FAS with starting dose 25 mg of LEN monotherapy (FAS25).
- Subgroup analyses were performed determining ORR, OS and PFS in the respective subgroups.
- An inclusion of approximately 500 patients in the observational cohort was expected to be necessary to complete a successful NN 1:1 matching for cohort balancing before conducting comparative efficacy outcome analyses. As the MOR208C203 ITT cohort consisted of n=81 patients, the ePS-based 1:1 matching resulted in a sample size of n=162 at maximum for the primary analysis method.
L699: The SAP was amended once, on October 29, 2019. Changes since the original SAP dated September 09, 2019 addressed FDA comments, and included clarifications, and added details for statistical programming. There were no changes to the concept of cohort balancing or primary analysis methodology.
L700: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L701: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L702: ## Protocol Amendments
L703: ## The Applicant's Description:
L704: The initial protocol was amended once, on September 09, 2019 prior to the database lock, which also took place on September 09, 2019. These changes are not expected to have had an impact on the integrity of the trial or interpretation of the results. Key/Critical changes were as follows:
L705: - Definition of best overall/objective response rate (ORR) as primary endpoint (defined as "main endpoint" in previous version)
- Complete response rate added as a secondary endpoint
- New section added for Primary Analysis (defined as "main analysis" in previous version)
- Number of patients expected for validation adjusted
- Identification of Duplicate Records section updated to reflect change in methodology
- Analysis populations updates, including clarifications for the 6-month follow-up rule, clarification of the existing analysis sets and addition of further analysis sets
- Sensitivity Analyses updated to include definition of covariates and new reference added for clarification
- Comparative Efficacy Outcome Analysis section updated to include a description of analysis methods for time-to-event endpoints (log-rank test to be performed and p-values estimated; HR to be estimated using Cox PH model)
L706: - An additional analysis, considering the matched patients as paired data, Klein and Moeschberger (1997) test, was also added to the Comparative Efficacy Outcome Analysis section, along with two new references
- Validation of Response Assessment section updated to include a broader definition of patient qualification for validation purposes
- In addition, a country-specific protocol amendment (Version 2.0), which included a clarification on the observation period (included in Version 3.0), was generated and submitted in Spain.
L707: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L708: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L709: ## Study Results
L710: ## Patient Disposition
L711: In the MAS25, a total of 76 (100%) patients in each cohort (tafasitamab monotherapy and tafasitamab + LEN) received study treatment. A lower number (%) of patients in the tafasitamab+LEN cohort (45 [59.2%]) progressed, relapsed or died compared with the LEN-mono cohort (63 [82.9%]). The most common reason for permanent discontinuation of LEN-mono treatment was disease progression or death (30 [39.5%] patients in the tafasitamab+LEN cohort and 48 [63.2%] patients in the LEN mono cohort) (Table 20).

L737: Balance between the 2 treatment cohorts:
L738: The standardized mean difference (SMD) is a measure of balance achieved for the proportions of a certain dichotomized baseline characteristic.
L739: Of the 9 covariates used for NN 1:1 matching (age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last therapy line, number of previous lines of therapy, history of primary refractoriness, prior ASCT, neutropenia, anemia, and elevated LDH), 5 achieved a SMD &lt;0.1, 2 achieved a SMD &lt;0.2, and 2 achieved a SMD of ≤0.29 (Ann Arbor and Number of Prior Lines) in the MAS25. For each of the 9 baseline covariates, an SMD of ≤0.18 was achieved for MAS25_Cal.
L740: ## The Applicant's Position:
L741: The comparative cohorts are representative for patients with R/R DLBCL and have baseline characteristics representative of the target population. The two cohorts were well matched with a SMD &lt;0.20 for 7 of the 9 covariates in the MAS25, which allows for a robust estimation of the additional treatment effect attributable to tafasitamab when added to LEN as in the MOR208C203 trial. Sensitivity analysis achieved a higher degree of balance with an SMD of ≤0.18 for all 9 baseline covariates in the MAS25_Cal analysis population.
L742: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L743: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of baseline disease characteristics in
L744: Table 23. Below (Table 24) we restrict patients to those that were DLBCL-confirmed, which affects the tafa+LEN cohort. **NOTE:** Statements about two cohorts being balanced in the setting of the RE-MIND study is difficult to substantiate. From a marginal distribution point of view, the data show that LEN patients exhibit poorer Ann Arbor, ECOG, and number of prior lines. Additionally, even if the marginal distributions of these characteristics look similar, whether visually or by the SMD metric, it does not in any way guarantee that the joint distributions of multiple risk factors are similar. Unlike randomization which theoretically induces similar marginal and joint distributions of patient characteristics, the RE-MIND observational study cannot guarantee this. An assessment of the joint distribution cannot be rigorously performed as the sample size is too small to identify the joint distributions in a robust manner. As shown in Table 24, an estimate of the joint distribution of relapse to last line and number of prior lines suggest that jointly the baseline disease characteristics between the two cohorts may be imbalanced.

L751: With respect to the studied cohorts being representative of RR DLBCL patients in the target population, FDA does not agree that this statement is defensible, as it would require a precise definition of the DLBCL target population followed by a random sample of patients from a listing of DLBCL patients from the target population. L752: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L753: In the MAS25, the numbers (%) of patients with CR and PR were higher in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort (CR: 39.5% and PR: 27.6%) compared with the LEN monotherapy cohort (CR: 13.2% and PR: 21.1%). The primary endpoint was met comparing ORR of the matched cohorts: best ORR was 67.1% (95% CI: 55.4, 77.5) in the tafasitamab+LEN cohort and 34.2% (95% CI: 23.7, 46.0) in the LEN monotherapy cohort (Odds Ratio=3.885 [95% CI: 1.900, 8.142]). This difference was statistically significant (Fisher's exact test p&lt;0.0001); the magnitude of the difference was clinically meaningful.

L757: Sensitivity analysis performed with the MAS25_Cal set achieved a standardized difference of \le 0.18 for all 9 baseline covariates and led to highly consistent results with the primary analysis.
L758: Additional sensitivity analyses were performed and confirmed the primary analysis. These included using the assumption of correlated data for matched datasets and performing McNemar's instead of Fisher's exact test, analysis of PFS and EFS with different censoring rules, analyses of ORR, PFS and OS with cohorts balanced using overlap weights constructed from the ePS (i.e., instead of 1:1 NN matching), multiple imputation on missing baseline covariates and modified analysis sets including patients not satisfying the 6-month follow up rule.
L759: ## The Applicant's Position:
L760: Combination tafasitamab and LEN demonstrated substantial evidence of efficacy over LEN monotherapy cohort in R/R DLBCL for the MAS25 set, with ORR of 67.1% vs 34.2% in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort vs LEN monotherapy cohorts, respectively. The difference of almost doubling the ORR and tripling the CR rate between the two cohorts is clinically meaningful. These results were confirmed by all sensitivity analyses. The addition of tafasitamab to LEN results in increased activity in R/R DLBCL, demonstrating the contribution of tafasitamab to the combination regimen. Notably, outcomes from the observational cohort of LEN monotherapy
L761: were similar to the published literature with LEN monotherapy in R/R DLBCL with best ORR ranging from 21-29% (*Wiernik et al., 2008*; *Witzig et al., 2011*; *Czuczman et al., 2017*).
L762: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L763: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation in Table 25. However, due to the nature of the observational study, statements about evidence of substantial advantage of tafa+LEN over LEN monotherapy cannot be rigorously substantiated. Table 26 following provides additional FDA-generated results restricted to centrally-confirmed DLBCL, per IRC.

L767: ## **SOURCE FDA-analysis**
L768: FDA's results are similar to the Applicant's results. However, the interpretation remains ambiguous. The primary reasons stem from these sources. First, as noted previously, the distribution (marginal and joint) of the observable covariates, are not well-balanced. Second, the role of unmeasured confounders are unknown. Thus, how much of the observed difference in the results is due in part or in whole to factors favorable to the tafa+LEN arm is still unclear. Third, since not all patients in the LEN cohort had IRC-assessed response, the potential for outcome misclassification exists.
L769: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L770: In the MAS25, the effect size of all secondary endpoints was consistent with the primary endpoint. Median DoR (Kaplan-Meier estimate) was 20.5 (95% CI: 12.3, NR) months in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort and 6.6 (95% CI: 4.1, 17.2) months in the LEN monotherapy cohort (Figure 16). Median PFS (Kaplan-Meier estimate) in the MAS25 was 12.1 (95% CI: 5.9, NR) months in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort and 4.0 (95% CI: 3.1, 7.4) months in the LEN monotherapy cohort. The HR for PFS was 0.463 (0.307, 0.698) in favor of the tafasitamab + LEN
L771: cohort (Cox proportional hazard model p=0.0002). The median follow up time for PFS was 19.7 months in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort and 12.6 months in the LEN monotherapy cohort.
L772: **Figure 16: Duration of Response (Excluding Non-Responders) (MOR208C206)**
L773: Median OS (Kaplan-Meier estimate) was NR (95% CI: 15.5, NR) in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort and 9.4 (95% CI: 5.1, 20.0) months in the LEN monotherapy cohort. The HR for OS was 0.499 (95% CI: 0.317, 0.785) in favor of the tafasitamab + LEN cohort (Cox proportional hazard model p=0.0026). The median follow up time for OS was 21.5 months in the tafasitamab + LEN cohort and 21.8 months in the LEN monotherapy cohort.
L774: Results for the secondary efficacy endpoints DoR, PFS, OS, EFS, and DCR in the MAS25_Cal were consistent with those in the MAS25.
L775: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L776: Median DoR was extended by approximately 14 months in the combination tafasitamab+LEN compared with LEN monotherapy, a clinically meaningful prolongation of response duration. All time-to-event endpoints supported the primary analysis results, with clinically meaningful and statistically significant differences between tafasitamab + LEN combination and LEN monotherapy.
L777: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L778: Due to the limitations of the observational study, the secondary endpoints described by the Applicant are not utilized.
L779: ## **Subgroup Analysis**
L780: Best ORRs by subgroups for the MAS25 are summarized in Figure 17.
L781: **Figure 17: Forest Plot of Best Objective Response Rate by Subgroups (MAS25) (MOR208C206)**

L783: ## The Applicant's Position:
L784: Best ORR in subgroups were consistent with the best ORR in the overall population (MAS25), although the numbers are too small to draw definitive conclusions.
L785: OS and PFS subgroup analyses were consistent in the main matched analysis population across all presented subgroups.
L786: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L787: FDA agrees with the Applicant that subgroup sample sizes are small, precluding meaningful statements about subgroup heterogeneity with respect to ORR.
L788: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L789: Physician-reported, real-world tumor response assessments were validated in a subset of patients via independent radiology and clinical review assessments in the retrospective, observational cohort of R/R DLBCL patients treated with LEN monotherapy in the RE-MIND study. The combined concordance for the responders (CR+PR) + non-responders (SD+PD) was 79.8% in a subset of 79 patients assessed.
L790: ## The Applicant's Position:
L791: The validation assessment demonstrated that response assessment by an independent panel of experts was generally concordant with the response assessment by the treating physicians.
L792: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L793: Based on the Applicant's definition of concordance, FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement. However, as noted earlier, there were only 22 patients who were responders per INV. As such, it is not clear how reliable the concordance estimates are. In addition, it is not clear what the concordance threshold should be for the purpose of deciding whether the results are robust to misclassification.
L794: ## **8.1.4. MOR208C201**
L795: ## **Trial Design**
L796: ## The Applicant's Description:
L797: This trial was a phase 2a, 2-stage, open-label, single-arm, multicenter, study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of tafasitamab monotherapy in adult patients with R/R NHL who had received at least 1 prior therapy containing RTX.
L798: A two-stage design was used to minimize exposure of patients with NHL subtypes not adequately responding to MOR208. In stage 1, ten patients were to be enrolled into each of four NHL subtype-specific cohorts; DLBCL, FL, other iNHL and MCL. If \ge 2 patients (20%) in a cohort had a partial or complete response after 8–12 weeks of treatment, then \ge 20 additional patients with that NHL subtype were to be enrolled. Based on the data from stage 1, the DLBCL and FL subtype-specific cohorts were expanded in Stage 2. At the completion of enrolment (n=92), the subtype-specific cohorts included following number of patients: DLBCL n=35; FL n=34; other iNHL n=11; MCL n=12.
L799: Treatment consisted of tafasitamab monotherapy which was to be administered until 8 weeks (2 cycles) for all patients. Patients who had a response of at least SD or better, could receive on additional treatment cycle. At the end of cycle 3, patients who had a PR or CR, (regardless of Stage 1 or 2 or the histological subtype) were eligible to receive further study drug until progression (either every two or every four weeks, as decided by the investigator).
L800: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L801: As mentioned by the Applicant the primary objective of this trial was to assess the antitumor activity of single-agent tafasitamab in patients with relapsed or refractory NHL. The dose used was the same as the pivotal trial, 12 mg/kg. Patients were treated for an initial 2 cycles, each 28 days with tafasitamab administrated on day 1, 8, 15 and 22. Premedication was given for the first 3 infusions. Patients with stable disease would then qualify for cycle 3, again tafasitamab administrated on day 1, 8, 15 and 22. For patients with an ongoing response of at least a PR at the end of cycle 3, may then progression to receive maintenance treatment which was further treatment every 2 weeks or monthly until disease progression.
L802: Premedication consisted of antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor blockers, and glucocorticosteroids, which were given for the first 3 infusions.
L803: The secondary objectives for this trial included evaluation of the duration of response, safety and tolerability, evaluation of potential immunogenicity and evaluation of the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics in patients with relapsed or refractory NHL.
L804: ## Design
L805: The trial enrolled in two stages. Stage 1 consisted of at least 10 patients per NHL subtype:
L806: - FL
- DLBCL
- MCL
- Other indolent NHL
L807: For those NHL subtypes with 2 or more responders, an additional 20 patients will be enrolled for a total of 30 patients per subtype for stage 2. The cohorts may have been stopped for
L808: futility after stage 1.
L809: ## Inclusion criteria (summarized)
L810: - Age \ge 18 years of age
- Histologically confirmed diagnosis according to the Revised European American lymphoma/World Health Organization classification:Follicular lymphoma
- Other indolent NHL
- DLBCL
- MCL
- Progression of lymphoma after at least 1 prior rituximab containing regimen. This could be 1 completed cycle of combination therapy with rituximab and chemotherapy or at least 4 weekly administration of rituximab as monotherapy.
- If previous ASCT, patients must be at least 4 weeks following transplantation and have full hematologic recovery
- Life expectancy of &gt;3 months
- ECOG performance status of &lt; 3
- The following laboratory criteria at screening:ANC \ge 1.0 (1000/\text{mm}^3)
- Platelet count \ge 75 \times 10^9/\text{L}
- Hemoglobin \ge 8.0 g/dL
- Serum creatinine &lt; 2.0 \times \text{ULN}
- Total bilirubin \le 2.0 \times \text{ULN}
- ALT and AST \le 2.5 \times \text{ULN}
- Females of childbearing potential, a negative pregnancy test and use of a double-barrier contraception during and for 3 months after the last dose or documented total hysterectomy and/or oophorectomy, tubal ligation.
- If male, an effective barrier method must be used during the trial and for 3 months after the last dose if sexually active with a female of childbearing potential.
L811: ## Exclusion Criteria (summarized)
L812: - Previous treatment with an anti-CD19 antibody
- Previous allogeneic stem cell transplantation
- Clinically significant cardiovascular disease or cardiac insufficiency
- Positive hepatitis serology
- History of HIV infection
- Any active systemic infection requiring active parenteral antibiotic therapy within 4 weeks
- Current treatment with immunosuppressive agents other than corticosteroids, not more than 10 mg prednisone or equivalent.
L813: - History or clinical evidence of CNS meningeal or epidural disease including brain metastasis.
- Pregnancy or breastfeeding in women and women of childbearing potential not using acceptable birth control
L814: ## Management of Toxicities
L815: If a patient developed a study drug related grade 3 or 4 toxicity and the toxicity had not resolved to grade 1, or baseline if pre-existing, at the day of the next scheduled dose, treatment was to be postponed. If treatment was postponed for more than 15 days for the same toxicity, the patient's treatment was to be discontinued.
L816: Patients were to begin the next treatment cycle of criteria were met. These criteria are below:
L817: - ANC \ge 1.0 \times 10^9/L
- Platelet count \ge 75 \times 10^9/L
- Hemoglobin \ge 8.0 \text{ g/dL} (may be transfused)
- Serum creatinine &lt; 2.0 \times \text{ULN}
- Total bilirubin \le 2.0 \times \text{ULN}
- ALT and ASLT \le 2.5 \times \text{ULN}
- No treatment related grade 3 or 4 toxicity in CNS, cardiovascular, infections or gastrointestinal system organ class.
L818: Prophylactic antibiotics/antifungals were allowed at the discretion of the investigator. Medications for treatment of symptoms of NHL or other diseases were allowed at the discretion of the investigator.
L819: ## Study Endpoints
L820: ## The Applicant's Description:
L821: The primary efficacy endpoint was best ORR (CR + PR), assessed by Investigators. Additional efficacy endpoints included;
L822: - TTP, the time from study entry (first dosing) until documented lymphoma progression or death as a result of lymphoma. Deaths from other causes were censored at the time of death.
- DoR, defined as the time from first meeting of criteria for response (i.e., CR or PR) to first documentation of relapse or progression.
- PFS, defined as the time from study entry (first dosing) until lymphoma progression or death as a result of any cause.
- SD rate, defined as failure to attain CR/PR or PD.
L823: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L824: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the endpoints. Response was determined using the revised IWG response criteria for malignant lymphoma (Cheson response criteria), standard criteria for response assessment in DLBCL. TTP is considered to be exploratory. Stable disease is not considered clinically meaningful from a regulatory perspective. Since this is a single arm study, time-to event endpoints such as PFS and OS have limited interpretability.
L825: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L826: ## The Applicant's Description:
L827: The initial SAP was amended three times prior to the data cut-off of the interim analysis used for this BLA. The changes do not have any impact on the interpretation of previous trial results since modifications were restricted to rephrasing and the addition of analyses. SAP changes are summarized as follows:
L828: - SAP Version 2.0 (July 7, 2015): Few paragraphs were rephrased. No analyses were added or amended.
- SAP Version 3.0 (November 30, 2018):The analysis on patient disposition was modified to report a more granular patient flow.
- A tabulation of protocol non-compliances was added.
- Efficacy analyses were added.
- SAP Version 4.0 (April 03, 2019):Deletion of analyses on composite PK parameters since these had not been conducted before, and are not going to be performed in the future.
- Further refinement of disposition tables, addition of Listings.
L829: All analyses were performed using the ITT population, comprising all patients who received at least one dose of tafasitamab. The ITT and safety populations in this study were the same.
L830: Best ORR, overall and per NHL subtype, was summarized alongside with exact (Clopper-Pearson) 95% CIs. In addition, the tumor response categories were evaluated by visit. The corresponding numbers of patients in each of the response categories were summarized in a frequency table by visit.
L831: All secondary efficacy analyses were provided based on the response assessment of the investigator as well as based on the central radiology review assessment. For time-to-event endpoints DoR, TTP, and PFS, data were summarized using standard Kaplan Meier methodology.
L832: The DCO for this study occurred on September 28, 2018.
L833: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L834: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L835: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L836: ## The Applicant's Description:
L837: The initial protocol was amended four times prior to the completion of the study. These changes had no impact on the integrity of the trial or interpretation of the results. Key changes were as follows:
L838: - Amendment 1 (Date: January 07, 2013), submitted prior to patient enrollment, included changes to various safety assessments and reporting, as well as eliminated minimal disease assessment, removed a longitudinal assessment of CD19 expression on tumor cells, and introduced formal study termination rules.
- Amendment 2 (Date: October 02, 2013) was done after patient enrollment began. It allowed for patients who had an ongoing response of at least PR after Cycle 3 eligible to receive 12 mg/kg tafasitamab as extended therapy until disease progression; CT/PET examinations performed up to 3 weeks before Cycle 1 Day 1 could be used as a patient's baseline assessment; the futility decision if a further 20 patients in a subtype would be enrolled to continue enrollment after the first 10 patients in each subtype could now be based on response at both 8 and 12 weeks (since therapy with monoclonal antibodies is expected to have a slower effect than cytotoxic drugs); and radiographic investigations could now be performed 4 working days before or after the actual visit. Clarifications to CT/PET procedures, dosing/body weight calculations, lymphomas that could be included in the subtype of indolent lymphoma, concomitant medication entry into the eCRF were added.
- Amendment 3 (Date: April 15, 2014) introduced minor changes to various safety assessments; added a definition for having received at least 1 prior therapy containing RTX; updated eligibility and management of patients with positive HBV screens; excluded allergic reactions and infusion-related reactions grade 2 and less from the definition of AEs of special interest; widened the visit window (due to practical reasons) for radiographic assessment by 3 days in treatment period; added an inclusion criteria for transformed lymphomas that the subtype at screening for the MOR208C201 study was relevant for the assignment to the respective subtype (this ensured a correct classification of patients who had their DLBCL transformed from a prior indolent lymphoma to the DLBCL subtype cohort in the study); widened the window for postponements of visits; detailed the use of MRI; clarified that early termination of patients was defined as patients who terminated the study before the end of Cycle 2; and clarified that patients who terminated before the end of Cycle 2 would undergo an EOS visit, whereas patients who had at least SD at the end of Cycle 2 and had not progressed would continue with the first follow up visit.
L839: - Amendment 4 (Date: September 19, 2017) prolonged the extended treatment beyond Follow-up Visit 12 until progression, limited the amount of data collected beyond follow-up visit 12, and removed the ADA assessment from the end of study visit.
L840: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L841: The FDA did not independently confirm the protocol amendments above, but notes the above protocol amendments stated by the Applicant.
L842: ## Study Results
L843: ## Patient Disposition
L844: A total of 92 patients were treated with tafasitamab (ITT population) in this study. Of these, 75 (81.5%) patients completed the first 2 cycles, 44 (47.8 %) patients continued treatment in Cycle 3, and 16 (17.4%) patients entered the extended treatment (also referred to as maintenance therapy) phase. Six (6.5%) patients were still on treatment at data cut-off, and 1 patient had completed study participation with 46 months of treatment.
L845: Patients were considered to have completed the study if they finished the first 2 cycles of treatment plus the end of Cycle 2 assessments. A total of 15 of the 92 treated patients (16.3%, ITT population) discontinued from the study prior to completion of the first 2 cycles, the majority of these due to progressive disease (46.7%). Fifty-two patients discontinued the study in Cycle 1 to Cycle 3. Reasons for study discontinuation at any time during Cycle 1 to 3 were progressive disease (61.5%), Investigator decision (13.5%), new cancer therapy (3.8%), death (9.6%), other (13.5%), AE (3.3%), patient withdrawal of consent (3.3%) and protocol violation (1.9%).
L846: ## DLBCL Subset
L847: Thirty-five DLBCL patients were treated with tafasitamab in the ITT/safety population. Of these, 25 (71.4%) DLBCL patients completed the first 2 cycles, 12 (34.3%) DLBCL patients continued treatment in Cycle 3, and 6 (17.1%) DLBCL patients entered the extended treatment phase (maintenance treatment per protocol). One (2.9%) DLBCL patient was still on treatment at the DCO (September 28, 2018).
L848: A total of 10 out of 35 treated DLBCL patients (28.6%) discontinued from the study prior to completion of the first 2 cycles, the majority of these due to PD (50.0%). Reasons for discontinuation for the DLBCL patients were progressive disease (50.0%), death (30.0%), protocol violation (10.0%), and physician decision (10.0%). 25 DLBCL patients discontinued from the study during Cycle 1 to Cycle 3. Reasons for discontinuation at any time during Cycle 1 to Cycle 3 were progressive disease (68.0%), death (16.0%), Investigator decision (12.0%), and protocol violation (4.0%), Twenty-eight of the 35 patients (80.0%) in the DLBCL subgroup discontinued treatment during the first 3 cycles.
L849: ## The Applicant's Position:
L850: The most common cause of discontinuation of study drug was disease progression. A subset of patients benefitted from the tafasitamab monotherapy and remained in treatment for an extended period of time.
L851: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L852: The subtype relevant to FDA's evaluation of tafasitamab monotherapy activity is DLBCL. Per the data submitted to FDA, 13 (37%) patients completed cycle 3.
L853: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L854: In the ITT population, 1 patient (1.1%) had a critical protocol non-compliance (i.e., did not satisfy the study entry criteria). Major protocol non-compliance was reported for 42 patients (45.7%). This included 25.0% patients who had major protocol deviations belonging to the "Other critical or major protocol noncompliance" category (bone marrow aspiration/biopsy not done, site missed timely re-consent with revised informed consent forms, IRR reported with delay, ECG not performed, local laboratory parameters not collected, End of Study Visit not performed, SAE not reported within timeframe, CT images not performed with contrast as required, vital signs collected out of allowed timeframes, radiological evaluation not reported within timeframe, or biopsy at Screening not performed correctly). A further 21.7% patients had protocol deviations due to procedural errors during the study drug administration (e.g. incorrect infusion rates or infusion materials [lines and filters] than as specified per protocol were used).
L855: ## The Applicant's Position:
L856: After a critical review of these protocol deviations, the applicant concluded that the analysis and the overall interpretation of efficacy and safety results were not impacted. A Per Protocol analysis Set (PPS) was not prespecified in the study protocol.
L857: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L858: While it is not clear what is meant by the phrase "efficacy and safety results were not impacted", FDA considers the efficacy and safety results of tafasitamab monotherapy to be interpretable within the context of protocol deviations.
L859: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L860: Most patients were male (60.9%) and white (94.6%). The median age of the 92 patients was 66.5 (min, max: 35.0, 90.0); the majority (53.3%) were ≥65 years of age. Similarly, most patients in the DLBCL subset were male (68.6%) and white (94.3%). The median age of the 35 DLBCL patients was 71.0 (min, max: 35.0, 90.0); the majority (54.3%) were ≥65 years of age.

L865: ## The Applicant's Position:
L866: Overall, the demographic characteristics were similar in the entire ITT population and the DLBCL subset. The DLBCL subset in particular is representative of the proposed indication, i.e. a population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL, who are not eligible for (b) (4) ASCT.
L867: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L868: The clinical reviewer agrees with the data and demographic table. Similar to the L-MIND trial, there was severe underrepresentation of racial subgroups other than Whites in the single arm MOR208C201 trial similar to the L-MIND trial.
L869: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L870: Of the DLBCL subset, 34.3% of DLBCL patients had 1 prior treatment line, and 22.9% and 25.7% DLBCL patients had 2 or 3 prior treatment lines, respectively. The majority (88.6%) of DLBCL patients did not have previous ASCT. Nineteen (54.3%) DLBCL patients had an ECOG score of 0; all other DLBCL patients had an ECOG score of 1 (42.9%) or 2 (2.9%), respectively. Twenty (57.1%) DLBCL patients had an Ann Arbor Stage of IV, and 28.6% of DLBCL patients had an Ann Arbor Stage of III. Most (68.6%) DLBCL patients were RTX-refractory. Patients were considered RTX refractory if they had no response or a response lasting &lt;6 months to any RTX-containing therapy.
L871: ## The Applicant's Position:
L872: The DLBCL patients enrolled in this study were representative of the intended population in the BLA (R/R DLBCL, not eligible for ASCT). Their baseline characteristics were similar to the patients enrolled in the pivotal L-MIND study. Thus, this study provides an appropriate population to characterize the efficacy of tafasitamab monotherapy in R/R DLBCL patients.
L873: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L874: The size of the DLBCL cohort is small such that a characterization of its representativeness of the "intended population" is difficult to substantiate.
L875: Regarding the 35 DLBCL patients, 20 patients (57.1%) had a ECOG category of 0 at baseline, ECOG of 1 at baseline was 12 patients (34.3%) and 3 patients (8.6%) had a ECOG category of 2 at baseline. The FDA agrees with the remaining data presented.
L876: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L877: One patient in the FL subgroup received concomitant therapy excluded per protocol.
L878: ## The Applicant's Position:
L879: Treatment compliance for tafasitamab was ensured as it was infused under the direct supervision of study personnel. No statistical analysis for measurement of treatment compliance was planned for this study. The patient who received the excluded concomitant therapy did not change the interpretation of study outcomes.
L880: The FDA's Assessment: The FDA did not independently confirm the Applicant position above, but note the position stated above stated by the Applicant.
L881: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L882: For the DLBCL patients in the ITT population (N=35), the best ORR for patients based on Investigator evaluation was CR for 2 (5.7%) patients and PR for 7 (20.0%) patients. Based on these data, the ORR was 25.7% (95% CI: 12.5, 43.3). Five (14.3%) patients had SD as their best response. Eleven (31.4%) patients had PD (progressive disease) as their best response. No patients were not evaluable. The ORR based on the IRC was 17.1% (95% CI: 6.6, 33.6).
L883: The concordance analysis (responder vs non-responder) for the Investigator's assessment vs. IRC revealed 91.4% concordance for DLBCL patients.
L884: ## The Applicant's Position:
L885: Tafasitamab monotherapy showed encouraging clinical activity (ORR) in patients with R/R DLBCL based on the Investigator's assessment.
L886: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L887: Numerically, FDA agrees with the INV and IRC ORR results presented by the Applicant. Whether it is encouraging is an open question. With respect to concordance between IRC and INV assessments:
L888: - Out of 9 responders per INV, 6 (67%) were classified as responders by IRC.
- Out of 26 non-responders per INV, 26 (100%) were classified as responders by IRC.
L889: Concordance is more salient in non-responders.
L890: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L891: Based on the Investigator evaluation, the median DoR was 20.1 months for the DLBCL subgroup. Of the 9 responders, five patients were in remission for \ge 12 months and 2 were in remission for &gt;4 years. The 12-month PFS rate was 34.3% for DLBCL, and the median TTP was 3.1. The DCR was 40.0% for DLBCL patients. Similar results were seen across all endpoints for the IRC reads as well.
L892: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L893: The data from the DLBCL subgroup demonstrate a long duration of response with tafasitamab monotherapy. All time-to-event endpoints supported the primary analysis results. No statistical inference for the time-to-event endpoints can be made from this single-arm study, and results presented are descriptive only.
L894: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L895: FDA agrees with the Sponsor's general assessment. The phrase "demonstrate a long duration of response" is not statistically defensible as there were only 9 patients in the denominator.
L896: ## **Subgroup Analysis**
L897: Based on the investigator assessment, 5 out of 12 DLBCL patients with 1 prior treatment line showed a response resulting in an ORR of 41.7% (95% CI: 15.2 to 72.3%). For patients with 2, 3, or more than 3, prior treatment lines the ORR was 12.5% (1/8 patients; 95% CI: 0.3% to 52.7%), 33.3% (3/9 patients; 95% CI: 7.5% to 70.1%), and 0% (0/6 patients; 95% CI: 0% to 45.9%), respectively. The median PFS was 3.4 months (95% CI: 1.7 to 15.4 months) for DLBCL patients who had 1 prior treatment line. For patients with 2, 3, or more than 3, prior treatment lines the median PFS was 2.1 months (95% CI: 0.7 to 2.1 months), 23 months (95% CI: 1.6 months – not reached), and 2.6 months (95% CI: 2.5 to 2.7 months), respectively.
L898: DLBCL patients with low or low/intermediate IPI score exhibited an ORR of 26.7% (4/15 patients), which was comparable to the ORR of patients with intermediate/high or high IPI score who showed an ORR of 21.1% (4/19 patients). The median PFS for DLBCL patients with low/intermediate IPI score was 13.2 months (95% CI: 1.6 months to not reached), compared
L899: with those with intermediate/intermediate-high IPI score who had a median PFS of 2.1 months (95% CI: 1.7 months to 3.4 months).
L900: Selected subgroups were only analyzed in terms of PFS: DLBCL patients who were younger than 65 years of age demonstrated a median PFS of 23 months (95% CI: 1.8 months to not reached; n=16 patients), compared with patients who were 65 years or older who had a median PFS of 2.1 months (95% CI: 1.6 to 3.1 months; n=19 patients).
L901: The subgroup of DLBCL patients refractory to any prior rituximab-containing treatment regimen had a median PFS of 2.7 months (95% CI: 1.6 to 23 months; n=24 patients), while patients without rituximab-refractory disease had a median PFS of 3.1 months (95% CI: 0.7 months to not reached; n=11 patients).
L902: ## The Applicant's Position:
L903: The analysis of subgroups based on number of prior treatment lines, IPI, age, or refractoriness to anti-CD20 regimens, irrespective of whether ORR or PFS were analyzed did not reveal any clear patterns, based on the small number of DLBCL patients contained in the various subgroups.
L904: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L905: Results from subgroup analysis are difficult to substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes are small, and in general lead to estimates that are unstable.
L906: ## Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L907: ## **Primary Endpoints**
L908: The primary efficacy endpoint for the 3 studies was best ORR (CR + PR), for which each study used standardized response criteria based on the International Working Group Response Criteria (Cheson, 2007). Table 28 presents a summary of ORR for Studies MOR208C203, MOR208C206, and MOR208C201, as well as for 3 published prospective LEN monotherapy trials (Czuczman et al, 2007; Wiernik et al, 2008; Witzig et al, 2011).
L909: With tafasitamab + LEN combination therapy, ORR was 60.0% (34 [42.5%] patients with CR and 14 [17.5%] patients with PR) in the MOR208C203 study.
L910: With LEN monotherapy, ORR was 34.2% for the observational cohort in the MOR208C206 study (10 [13.2%] patients with CR and 16 [21.1%] patients with PR), and ORR ranged from 19% to 28% across the published LEN studies (Czuczman et al, 2007; Wiernik et al, 2008; Witzig et al, 2011).
L911: Tafasitamab monotherapy results showed an ORR of 25.7% (2 [5.7%] DLBCL patients with CR and 7 [20.0%] DLBCL patients with PR) in study MOR208C201.

L915: ## The Applicant's Position:
L916: In study MOR208C201, tafasitamab monotherapy showed activity in DLBCL patients. Study MOR208C203 demonstrated a strongly increased signal of efficacy with the combination regimen of tafasitamab + LEN combination therapy. The study was single armed, and the superiority of the tafasitamab + LEN combination over LEN monotherapy could not be statistically tested. Cross-study comparisons with published LEN monotherapy results are difficult to interpret given potential differences in the patient population for key baseline characteristics. Therefore, MOR208C206 was conducted to generate a control cohort of LEN monotherapy for comparison to the combination regimen. The prospectively designed ePS-based 1:1 matching in this study allowed for a robust estimation of the additional treatment effect attributable to tafasitamab when added to LEN, as in Study MOR208C203.
L917: Compared with LEN monotherapy, the tafasitamab + LEN combination resulted in a ORR that was higher by 33%, nearly two-fold the ORR observed for LEN monotherapy. Further, the CR rate was three-fold higher in the combination arm versus LEN monotherapy, suggesting
L918: substantial additional activity for the novel combination of tafasitamab + LEN versus LEN monotherapy in transplant ineligible R/R DLBCL patients.
L919: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L920: FDA's results for the L-MIND study are as described earlier and do not numerically agree with those in Table 28 above. As noted previously, FDA's analyses were restricted to patients who were DLBCL-confirmed. For the RE-MIND study, this reduces the denominator to 66 patients and the numerator to 39, resulting in ORR of 59% (95% CI: 46%, 71%). While the data suggest that tafa+LEN provides for a substantial improvement over LEN with respect to ORR, it is unknown how much of that improvement is due to confounding factors. While the RE-MIND study attempts to provide clarity, whether the study is robust in the sense of adequately controlling for confounding remains an open question.
L921: ## **Secondary and Other Endpoints**
L922: Table 29 presents a summary of DoR and other secondary endpoints for Studies MOR208C203, MOR208C206, and MOR208C201, as well as for 2 published prospective LEN monotherapy trials (Czuczman et al, 2007; Witzig et al, 2011).

L933: ## The Applicant's Position:
L934: Deeper, more durable responses were observed for the combination arm of tafasitamab + LEN versus LEN monotherapy. DoR results above show that patients who achieve a CR with tafasitamab + LEN combination therapy have a high probability to remain in remission for an extended period of time. Patients achieving a PR as best response tend to progress within the next 6 months. Median DoR was extended by approximately 14 months in the combination tafasitamab+LEN as compared to LEN monotherapy, suggesting substantial additional activity for the combination therapy as opposed to LEN alone.
L935: The observed DoR, PFS, OS, TTP, and DCR, with tafasitamab supported the meaningful clinical benefit of treatment with tafasitamab 12 mg/kg alone or in combination with LEN and represent a substantial improvement in a population of patients with a high unmet medical need.
L936: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L937: As noted earlier, FDA does not consider time-to-event endpoints relevant to the determination of efficacy outside of the randomized setting. However, FDA notes that the durability of
L938: response in the responders is approximately 2 years.
L939: ## Subpopulations
L940: Data for exploratory subgroup analyses is presented in each of the individual trial sections.
L941: ## The Applicant's Position:
L942: The primary endpoint ORR was consistent across all presented subgroups (i.e., age group, IPI score, number of prior treatment lines, primary refractoriness, refractoriness to last prior treatment line, prior ASCT, and NK cell count at baseline).
L943: Subgroups analyses did not identify any major demographic factor or baseline disease characteristic predictive of lack of response to the tafasitamab + LEN combination or tafasitamab monotherapy in any of the trials.
L944: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L945: Results from subgroup analysis are difficult to substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes are small, and in general lead to estimates that are unstable.
L946: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L947: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L948: As noted in previous sections, the RE-MIND observational study was designed to learn about the activity of tafa+LEN relative to LEN alone. However, there were a number of issues that limited the interpretability of results. The Statistical Issues subsection in SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS provides additional details.
L949: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L950: ## The Applicant's Position:
L951: All studies included in the development program for tafasitamab in DLBCL have been conducted in compliance with Good Clinical Practice and with the approvals of the relevant Ethics Committees or Institutional Review Boards. Informed consent was obtained for all patients, and the studies were performed in accordance with the version of the Declaration of Helsinki that applied at the time the studies were conducted. Where required, regulatory approval was obtained from the relevant health authority.
L952: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L953: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L954: ## Financial Disclosure
L955: The Applicant disclosed all financial interests/arrangements with clinical investigators as per 21 CFR Part 54. None of the investigators or sub-investigators involved in the clinical trials had
L956: financial interests to disclose or arrangements that required disclosure. Limited instances of missing financial disclosure information for each study is summarized below;
L957: - For MOR208C203, information was missing from 3 sub-investigators at 3 distinct locations, none of whom have made a significant contribution to the data due to no patients being treated at the site during the time the concerned sub-investigator was assigned to the study (2 cases), or a limited amount of time spent as a sub-investigator for that site (1 case; 1 month).
- For MOR208C201, information was missing from 1 sub-investigator who was assigned to the study 2 months after the last patient completed the study at the concerned site. Thus, the sub-investigator could not have made a significant contribution to the data.
- For Xmab5574-01, information was missing from 2 sub-investigators at 1 study center due to unresponsiveness or lack of contact information.
L958: ## The Applicant's Position:
L959: The lack of disclosures across investigators in the clinical trials does not raise concerns about the overall integrity of study data. The 3 studies which formed the basis for the demonstration of efficacy (MOR28C203, MOR208C201 and MOR208C206) were conducted over multiple sites and in multiple countries, thus limiting the potential for a single investigator or sub-investigator to make a significant contribution to the data.
L960: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L961: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the financial disclosures on for the L-MIND trial.
L962: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L963: For applicable studies, study monitoring was performed in accordance with ICH E6 GCP guidelines, the sponsor's and CRO's standard operating procedures, the sponsor's and CRO's written instructions, the protocol, and all applicable laws and regulations. The handling of data, including data quality control, complied with all applicable regulatory guidelines.
L964: ## The Applicant's Position:
L965: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of efficacy. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections by the Applicant.
L966: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L967: The datasets, Clinical Study Reports, narratives and responses to information requests were considered adequate to allow for review of the submission.
L968: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L969: This Summary of Clinical Efficacy presents results from 1 tafasitamab + LEN combination study (MOR208C203 [L-MIND]) demonstrating a clinically relevant effect size, consistent with the mechanism-of-action-based rationale for combining the 2 agents. In this difficult-to-treat population of R/R DLBCL patients ineligible for ASCT, combination treatment with tafasitamab + LEN resulted in a high overall response in 60% (95% CI: 48.4, 70.8) of patients and a CR in 42.5% of patients. Responses were durable, with DoR of 71.6% (95% CI: 55.1, 82.9), 71.6% (95% CI: 55.1, 82.9), and 47.7% (95% CI: 10.5, 78.4) at 12, 18 and 24 months, respectively. PFS rates were 50% (95% CI: 37.9, 61.2) and 45.8% (95% CI: 33.4, 57.4) at 12 and 18 months, respectively, and OS of 73.7% (95% CI: 62.2, 82.2), 63.6% (95% CI: 50.9, 73.7), and 56.6 (95% CI: 43.0, 68.0) at 12, 18, and 24 months, respectively. Secondary endpoint analyses were generally consistent with the primary analysis, and consistent results were observed across patient subgroups of interest.
L970: The contribution of each of the single agents (tafasitamab and LEN) in the combination was determined via 1) the MOR208C201 clinical trial for tafasitamab, which demonstrated the activity of tafasitamab monotherapy in DLBCL patients, and 2) the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) study, in which the efficacy of LEN monotherapy was assessed in a matching observational cohort to the MOR208C203 (L-MIND) study. The MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) study provides a statistical comparison of LEN monotherapy vs. tafasitamab + LEN.
L971: In summary, the effect of the tafasitamab and LEN combination therapy can be considered to lead to substantial additional activity versus each agent alone, since for physician-assessed best ORR, CR and median PFS, the effect of the combination is greater than the sum of the respective monotherapies when considering the results in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) trial for LEN and the results in study MOR208C201 for tafasitamab.
L972: Further, in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND study), the results reached statistical significance and all time to event endpoints supported the primary analysis results, with clinically meaningful and statistically significant differences between tafasitamab + LEN combination and LEN monotherapy.
L973: The aggregate patient population was similar for demographic and baseline characteristics. Patients were elderly and had DLBCL, an orphan indication. Thus, it is reasonable and valid to draw conclusions about the efficacy of the tafasitamab + LEN combination from this patient population.
L974: ## The Applicant's Position:
L975: Patient outcomes with available treatments for R/R DLBCL remain largely unsatisfactory, in particular for patients unable to receive high-dose chemotherapy followed by ASCT or treatment with chimeric antigen receptor T cell therapies. Efficacy in this application is based on IRC-assessed ORR for the combination of tafasitamab and LEN, for which a clinically meaningful ORR of 60% and CR of 42.5% were observed. The responses observed with the
L976: tafasitamab and LEN were durable, in particular in patients who achieved a CR. The contribution of tafasitamab to the combination was put into context by the matched observational cohort of LEN-treated patients, as the combination demonstrated a nearly two-fold increase in ORR and three-fold increase in CR over LEN monotherapy.
L977: Similar, statistically significant benefit was observed in terms of duration of response and overall survival for patients who received tafasitamab + LEN vs LEN alone.
L978: Overall, the data presented in this application demonstrates substantial evidence of effectiveness for tafasitamab in combination with LEN to treat patients with R/R DLBCL who are not suitable for HDC and ASCT. Furthermore, the effectiveness of this regimen compares favorably with that of LEN alone in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) study and also compares favorably in the context of many other salvage regimens evaluated in this setting.
L979: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L980: FDA agrees with the Applicant that patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL who are unable to receive high-dose chemotherapy followed by ASCT have limited treatment options. The efficacy data from the L-MIND study with supportive evidence of the response rate of single agent tafasitamab from the relapsed or refractory DLBCL population from the single arm MOR208C201 Study represents a clinically meaningful treatment benefit of tafasitamab in this patient population. FDA's analysis of efficacy from the L-MIND trial was based on 71 patients with central pathology confirmed diagnosis of DLBCL and are different from that stated by the Applicant. FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assertions regarding the statistical significance of the results from the RE-MIND study. FDA notes several limitations with the RE-MIND study and does not agree that the data can be used to confirm statistically significant improvement of response of tafa+LEN compared to lenalidomide. The response data from this study was used to provide context to the results from the L-MIND trial. FDA notes that the response rate of single agent lenalidomide from the RE-MIND study is consistent with the data from the literature.
L981: The time-to-event endpoints (PFS and OS) are difficult to interpret in single-arm studies. The time-to-event endpoints are not likely to constitute supportive evidence because their measurements in isolation are out of context and comparing them to historical controls is prone to bias. FDA also notes that the eligibility criteria for L-MIND trial did not specify a requirement for ineligibility for CAR-T therapy.
L982: See also the SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS section.
L983: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L984: Assessment of the safety of tafasitamab is based on data from 569 patients in seven studies, analyzed as follows:
L985: The primary safety analysis comprising two major components:
L986: - The pivotal study MOR208C203 in patients with DBLCL, treated with a combination of tafasitamab plus lenalidomide, followed by tafasitamab alone (N=81)
- Three (3) studies with tafasitamab monotherapy (N=141), which were analyzed individually and pooled:XmAb5574-01 in patients with CLL/SLL (N=27)
- MOR208C201 in patients with NHL (N=92, of whom 35 were of the DBLCL subtype)
- MOR208C202 in patients with ALL (N=22)
L987: This analysis comprises safety data from 222 patients, treated with at least one (1) dose of tafasitamab in different indications. Two-hundred eleven (211) of these 222 patients (95%) were dosed with 12 mg/kg of tafasitamab.
L988: The primary analysis was complemented by safety data (deaths and SAEs) from three (3) studies with tafasitamab in combination with other therapies:
L989: - MOR208C204 (B-MIND) in patients with R/R DLBCL, treated with bendamustine and tafasitamab, or bendamustine and rituximab (N=283, blinded, with 1:1 randomization therefore estimated 141 patients on tafasitamab combination arm); ongoing
- MOR208C205 (COSMOS) in patients with R/R CLL/SLL, treated with tafasitamab with idelalisib or venetoclax (N=24); completed
- OSU 13031 – Investigator Initiated Trial in patients with CLL/SLL, treated with tafasitamab with lenalidomide or ibrutinib (N=40); ongoing
L990: ## The Applicant's Position:
L991: The total safety data available allow for a comprehensive assessment of the safety profile of tafasitamab, at a dose of 12mg/kg, in combination with lenalidomide followed by tafasitamab monotherapy and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk, in adult patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, who are not eligible for, or refuse autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT). This safety population is considered adequate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management.
L992: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L993: The safety review primarily focused on the pivotal trial, MOR208C203 in patients with DBLCL. In addition to the pivotal trial, the safety of the three monotherapy trials, XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201 and MOR208C202 was evaluated.
L994: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L995: To demonstrate the safety of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide followed by tafasitamab monotherapy, the primary analysis included detailed summaries of the clinical safety data from the pivotal trial MOR208C203 in 81 patients with DLBCL, of whom 80 received at least one dose each of tafasitamab and lenalidomide, which were evaluated to characterize common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management. In order to understand the relative contributions of each component to the safety profile of the combination, in addition to the analysis of safety data from the tafasitamab monotherapy phase of MOR208C203, a pooled analysis of data from a total of 141 patients in three tafasitamab monotherapy trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, and MOR208C202) was performed. Of these, MOR208C201, in NHL, included 35 patients with the DLBCL sub-type.
L996: A comparison of the safety data from the combination phase of MOR208C203 with safety data for lenalidomide monotherapy in the lenalidomide USPI was also performed.
L997: This primary analysis was supported by a review of deaths and SAEs from the patients in the three trials of tafasitamab in combination with other therapies: MOR208C205 (COSMOS), MOR208C204 (B-MIND), and OSU-13031 trials (see Table 10).
L998: ## The Applicant's Position:
L999: The pooling strategy was agreed with FDA as part of a Type B pre-BLA meeting (July 01, 2019). This approach enables characterization of the risk profile for tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide followed by monotherapy in the target population.
L1000: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1001: The FDA safety evaluation focused primarily on the pivotal trial MOR208C203. The safety of the pooled analysis of data from the three monotherapy trials was also evaluated.
L1002: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1003: ## Overall Exposure
L1004: The Safety Analysis Set was defined as all patients who received at least one dose of study drug. For studies (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, and MOR208C202), tafasitamab was the only study drug. For the L-MIND study, tafasitamab and lenalidomide were the study drugs. Patients were analyzed as treated. The primary safety analysis for this submission includes 81 patients (80 of whom received both tafasitamab and lenalidomide, and all of whom received tafasitamab) in study MOR208C203 and 141 patients from the tafasitamab monotherapy studies.
L1005: In MOR208C203, the median time on tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide was 190.0 days (range: 3 to 436 days) (Table 30).
L1006: The median time on tafasitamab monotherapy was 375.5 days in MOR208C203 (Table 30) and 51.0 days in the pooled monotherapy trials (Table 31).
L1007: The proportion of patients who received \ge 12 cycles of tafasitamab monotherapy (Table 30) in MOR208C203 was 28.4% and 7.8% in the pooled monotherapy trials (Table 31). The proportion of patients in MOR208C203 who received &gt; 20 doses of tafasitamab was 53.1% and 9.9% in the pooled monotherapy trials (Table 31). The differences between studies are expected due to differing numbers of treatment cycles to be applied by study design.
L1008: In addition, data on deaths and SAEs are available from three studies of tafasitamab in combination with other therapies, 64 patients from MOR208C205 (COSMOS) and OSU-13031, and 283 patients in MOR208C204 where treatment allocation is still blinded and exposure to tafasitamab is estimated as 141 patients (see Table 37).

L1020: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1021: The exposure to study drug was considered adequate to allow for a comprehensive assessment of safety in patients who were representative of the intended target population.
L1022: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1023: In the Applicant's table, Table 30, Total Number of Study Treatment Cycles and Duration of Exposure to Study Treatment (days) is referring to exposure to the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide. The data provided by the Applicant in Table 30 and Table 31 was verified by the reviewer. For duration of exposure in the pivotal trial MOR208C203, the exposure obtained by the clinical reviewer is displayed in Table 32. The table displays the total number of cycles as long as at least 1 drug was administered and the total number of cycles of tafasitamab regardless of lenalidomide administration.
L1024: As shown in Table 32, a total of 35 patients (43.2%) received \ge 12 cycles of treatment. For the pooled monotherapy trials, 11 patients (7.8%) received \ge 12 cycles of treatment. A total of 116 patients (82.2%) of the pooled monotherapy patients received between 1-3 total treatment cycles. In general, the duration of exposure for the pooled monotherapy patients is lower compared to trial MOR208C203, the pivotal trial.
L1025: For trial MOR208C203, a total of 46 patients (56.8%) were exposed to tafasitamab for &gt; 6 months, a total of 34 patients (42.0%) were exposed for greater than 1 year and a total of 19 patients (23.5%) were exposed for &gt; 2 years.

L1029: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L1030: The patients' baseline characteristics, demographics and disease characteristics are summarized in Table 33 and Table 34.
L1031: In MOR208C203, 56/81 (69.1%) patients were &gt;65 years of age, with a median age of 72.0 years. In the pooled monotherapy trials, 64/141 (45.4%) patients were &gt;65 years of age, with a median age of 63.0 years.
L1032: In MOR208C203, 52/81 (64.2%) patients had a baseline ECOG performance status graded \ge 1, and 7/81 (8.6%) of Grade 2. In the pooled monotherapy trials these proportions were 63/141 (44.7%) and 11/141 (7.8%) patients respectively.
L1033: The proportion of patients with an elevated baseline LDH level (&gt;190 U/L) was 52/81 (64.2%) in MOR208C203, and 106/141 (75.2%) in the pooled monotherapy trials.
L1034: In MOR208C203, 61/81 (75.3%) patients had an Ann Arbor Stage of III or IV and 41/81 (50.6%) patients had a high and intermediate-high International Prognostic Index (IPI) category risk.
L1035: Overall the patient population in MOR208C203 had a poor prognosis, as indicated by a median age of 72 years (range: 41 to 86 years), high and intermediate-high IPI category in 41 (50.6%) patients, advanced Ann Arbor disease staging (61 patients (75.3%) Stage III and IV) and elevated baseline LDH levels (52 patients (64.2%) &gt;190 U/L) (see also Table 33).
