L1049: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1050: The characteristics of the patients in the pivotal study MOR208C203 were representative of the target population. The characteristics of the patients in the three studies with tafasitamab monotherapy are sufficiently consistent to enable an assessment of the contribution of tafasitamab to the safety profile of tafasitamab/lenalidomide combination therapy in this population.
L1051: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1052: The clinical reviewer verified the data discussed in the above section. A few minor differences were seen. The majority of the patients were white. The race of trial MOR208C203 was 99.9% white, 2.5% Asian, 0% Black/African American 7.4%, 1.2% other and 7.4% missing. The race of the pooled monotherapy patient population was 92.9% white, 0.7% Asian, 3.5% Black/African American, and 2.8% other.
L1053: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of the safety database
L1054: An integrated safety database was created to support the integrated safety analyses described in this application. Integrated Clinical Data Interchange Standards Consortium (CDISC) Analysis Data Model (ADaM) datasets were created from individual study ADaM or SDTM datasets and followed the CDISC Analysis Data Model Implementation Guide version 1.1. A process of data reconciliation ensured that data on the safety database were consistent with those on the clinical trials database. The datalock date for the safety analyses was 30th June 2019.
L1055: The evaluation of safety is based on the primary safety analysis comprising data from the 81 patients in the pivotal MOR208C203 study, and the 141 patients in the three tafasitamab monotherapy studies as described in Section 8.2.4
L1056: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1057: The safety database is sufficient to evaluate the safety of the combination of tafasitamab with lenalidomide followed by tafasitamab monotherapy in the target population.
L1058: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1059: The clinical reviewer agrees the safety database was adequate.
L1060: ## Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1061: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1062: All individual case safety reports were followed up as necessary to obtain complete information on the case. A process of source data verification during study monitoring ensured the accuracy of these data. A thorough QC check was performed on all data entered to the safety database, and data were reconciled with the clinical trials database. No data integrity concerns were reported from site audits.
L1063: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1064: No meaningful concerns were detected in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets or individual case narratives; these are sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety.
L1065: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1066: Based on the datasets, narratives, Clinical Study Reports, and other submitted reports a sufficient review of the safety was able to be conducted.
L1067: ## Data:
L1068: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1069: The safety of treatment with tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide and as monotherapy was evaluated on the basis of the following:
L1070: - Frequency, type, severity, and causal relationship of AEs to study treatment
- Frequency of deaths, serious AEs, and other clinically significant AEs (including AEs leading to discontinuation and AEs requiring dose interruption and/or reduction)
- Frequency and type of AEs related to key intrinsic and extrinsic factors (e.g. age and geography)
- Changes in laboratory variables, with particular attention to Grade 3/4 abnormalities
L1071: Adverse events and medical history terms were re-coded in the integrated ADaM datasets using the MedDRA dictionary version 21.0. Laboratory and AE toxicities were graded according to CTCAE, version 4.03.
L1072: Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were defined as those adverse events that either started or worsened in severity on or after the First Treatment Date/Time and on or before the End of On-Treatment Phase Date/Time (i.e., during the On-Treatment Phase). Any AE that occurred more than 30 days after the date/time of last dose of study treatment was considered treatment emergent (TEAE). If the End of Treatment Date was missing, any AE occurring after the treatment start date was considered treatment-emergent. As disease progression was collected as an adverse event in trials MOR208C201 and MOR208C202, but not in trials XmAb5574-01 and MOR208C03 (L-MIND), AEs of disease progression (identified as events with preferred term = 'Disease Progression') were not included in the adverse event summaries.
L1073: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1074: The approach to categorization of AEs was consistent with regulatory guidance and appropriate for evaluation of the safety data.
L1075: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1076: A TEAE was defined as any AE reported from the first date of administration of study drug to the last date of administration of study drug + 30 days. The FDA agrees with above.
L1077: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1078: Adverse events could be directly observed, reported spontaneously by the patient or elicited by questioning the patient at each study visit. Per protocol, patients were to be questioned in a general way, without asking about the occurrence of any specific symptoms. In addition, AEs could also be detected when reported by the subjects during or between visits or through physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Adverse events of Special Interest (AESIs) were selected based on the mechanism of action of tafasitamab and lenalidomide as well as nonclinical and early clinical observations. Laboratory data from all sources (central and local laboratories) were combined as described in the ISS Statistical Analysis Plan.
L1079: The hematological parameters in relation to Grade 3 or more anemia, leukopenia, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia were summarized as the number and percent of patients demonstrating any such value at least once during the On-Treatment Phase in the pivotal MOR208C203 (L-MIND) trial, and for the tafasitamab monotherapy studies.
L1080: Evidence of drug-induced liver injury was sought by review of ALT, AST, bilirubin and alkaline phosphatase data and by respective MedDRA PTs.
L1081: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1082: The clinical monitoring and summarization of patient safety (methods and time points) described in the protocols and in the ISS Statistical Analysis Plan followed a standard approach and were appropriate for a comprehensive assessment of the safety of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide and as monotherapy for the target population and indication.
L1083: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1084: For the pivotal trial, MOR208C203, central laboratory values were collected at screening and on day 1 of each treatment cycle. Local laboratory values were done at other times up to 24 hours before administration of study drugs. These local laboratory values were not collected in the clinical database. Investigators were asked to report clinically significant laboratory abnormalities based on central and local laboratory test results as adverse events which resulted in higher reporting rates of laboratory AEs in the AE dataset as compared to the lab dataset.
L1085: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1086: ## Deaths
L1087: As of the submission cutoff date of June 30, 2019, a total of 50 (22.5%) deaths was reported in the 222 patients in the primary safety analysis (a summary overview of all fatalities is provided in Table 35.
L1088: - 10 (10.9%) deaths out of 92 patients in MOR208C201,
- 6 (27.3%) deaths out of 22 patients in MOR208C202, and
- 34 (42.0%) deaths out of 81 patients in MOR208C203
- No deaths were reported in XmAb5574-01 (27 patients).
L1089: Of the 50 deaths,
L1090: - 38/222 (17.1%) these resulted from disease progression
- 8/222 (3.6%) were due to TEAEs. These 8 patients had 10 TEAEs with a fatal outcome assessed as not related to disease progression (For patient [REDACTED](b)(6) in MOR208C201 three TEAE with fatal outcome were reported, for all other patients 1 TEAE with fatal outcome each was reported). All 10 TEAEs with fatal outcome except one event 'sclerosing cholangitis' (see short narrative for MOR208C202, patient [REDACTED](b)(6)) were considered not related to study medication by both the investigator and applicant.
- 4 fatalities not related to disease progression and not caused by TEAE occurred later than 30 days post last study drug administration.
L1091: Below are brief summaries for the eight patients with a fatal outcome due to TEAE which were considered unrelated to study medication by the investigators:
L1092: - Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C201) experienced 3 TEAE with fatal outcome, namely (i) sudden and severe pneumonia accompanied by (ii) respiratory failure and (iii) cardiac failure in Cycle 1 (Study Day 23). The patient died on the same day.
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C202) developed sclerosing cholangitis on Study Day 97 and died one month later on Study Day 124 due to fulminant liver failure. A causal relationship of the event to tafasitamab could not be excluded; however, the patient suffered from intercurrent acute hepatitis A, which was diagnosed 10 days prior to death, and had suspected acute Graft versus Host Disease (GvHD) after bone marrow transplant starting approximately 3 months prior to initiating study medication. The GvHD involved the gastrointestinal tract and liver, either of which could lead to liver failure. However, a causal relationship between death and study medication cannot be ruled out.
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C202) experienced sepsis with symptoms of hypotension, respiratory distress and acute kidney injury, secondary to sepsis on Study Day 33. The event was likely to have been caused by *Stenotrophomonas maltophilia* infection. The patient died two days after event onset (Study Day 35).
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C202) developed sepsis with hypotension on Study Day 20. Multi-organ system failure progressed, and patient died 23 days after event onset (Study Day 43). The event was considered rather to have been caused by progression of disease.
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination Therapy phase) experienced cerebrovascular accident with left-sided hemiplegia secondary to stroke on Study Day 109 and concurrently developed Grade 4 pulmonary embolism and Grade 4 deep vein thrombosis two days prior to stroke. Although the investigator stated biological evidence for disease progression, the stroke with fatal outcome (Study Day 111) was not just a consequence of disease progression, but rather potentially due to multiple reasons, including prolongation in bed, underlying comorbidities and the inability to administer preventive treatment with low molecular weight heparin.
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination therapy phase) experienced sudden death at home on Study Day 17 of Cycle 1. The event was likely to have resulted from cardiovascular comorbidities, to which the patient was predisposed due to treatment with R-CHOP twice, as frontline therapy for DLBCL as well as for the first relapse. No additional relevant details were available.
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination therapy phase) experienced multifactorial respiratory failure on Study Day 43 in the context of a respiratory superinfection (adenovirus infection), neutropenic fever, pleural effusion of neoplastic nature and mixed acidosis. The event was likely to have resulted from suspected lymphoma progression (pleural fluid was of neoplastic nature). The patient died 8 days after event onset (Study Day 51).
- Patient [REDACTED](b)(6) (MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination therapy phase) experienced a worsening of progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy. The patient had first
L1093: experienced non-specific neurological symptoms 10 days prior to initiation of study treatment, which then worsened on-treatment (Study Day 9). The patient received in total four infusions of tafasitamab and lenalidomide daily for 15 days, and died 68 days after end of treatment. The event was likely to have resulted from the patient's prolonged immunocompromised state due to multiple prior lymphoma treatments (immuno-chemotherapies) during the preceding two years.

L1099: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1100: A review of the deaths across the pivotal MOR208C203 trial and the 3 tafasitamab monotherapy trials (MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and XmAb5574-01) indicated that most deaths (38/50, 76%) were due to disease progression. Of the remaining 8 fatalities caused by TEAEs, 6 were related to infections, a pattern seen similarly with other B cell depleting antibodies, although none of these were considered to be related to study medication by the investigators. The only death for which a causal relationship to tafasitamab could not be excluded was a case of sclerosing cholangitis confounded by acute hepatitis A and Graft versus Host Disease after bone marrow transplant that started approximately 3 months prior to start of study medication in study MOR208C202.
L1101: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1102: The data presented in Table 35 was verified by the clinical reviewer. Two minor differences were noted for deaths within 60 days of first study treatment.
L1103: For trial MOR208C203, 8 patients (9.9%) died within 30 days of the last dose. For trials MOR208C201 this was 8 patients (8.7%) and 3 patients (13.6%) for trial MOR208C202. For trial MOR208C203, the 8 patients that died within 30 days of last dose 5 patients were due to disease progression and 3 patients were due to adverse events. One patient, (b) (6), (described above), died from PML 68 days after the last dose of treatment, however, the PML was diagnosed prior to this date. Thus, a total of 4 patients died as a result of adverse events on trial MOR208C203.
L1104: The clinical reviewer reviewed the 8 narratives regarding deaths within 30 days of last dose for trial MOR208C203. Below is a summary of these narratives.
L1105: Patient (b) (6) was a 81-year-old woman with DLBCL with ECOG score of 2 at screening. The patient had a PMH of TIA, hyperthyroidism, renal failure, PE, epilepsy, PRES, and hypokalemia. At the time of enrollment she had hypertension, atrial fibrillation, vertigo, AV block, hypogammaglobulinemia, anemia, thrombocytopenia, urinary tract infection and hypomagnesemia, Adverse events and SAEs during the trial included diarrhea, (SAE), neutropenia, muscular weakness (SAE), CMV infection (SAE), febrile neutropenia (SAE), and urinary tract infection. The patient was hospitalized on day 93 due to febrile neutropenia which was prolonged due to a grade 4 DVT and pulmonary embolism on day 107, the following day the patient had acute renal failure. Anticoagulation was not possible due to thrombocytopenia. Two days later the patient had a CVA. The last dose of tafasitamab was day 99 and the last dose of lenalidomide was day 93. A CT on day 109 did not confirm evolution of lymphoma, however, the investigator felt lymphoma lesions in the leg were evolving. The patient received palliative care on day 109. The patient died on day 111. The probable cause of death was reported as CVA, an autopsy was not done.
L1106: Patient (b) (6) was a 59-year-old woman with DLBCL, stage 3 prior treatment included two
L1107: lines of therapy, R-CHOP and R-DHAP. Concurrent conditions including polyneuropathy, hypothyroidism, anemia, ankle fracture, abdominal pain, decreased while blood cell count, hepatitis B core antigen positive and sinus tachycardia. On day 4 the patient had segmental resection of small intestine invagination due to progression of lymphoma She was withdrawn on day 3 due to disease progression. On day 14 she a surgery for burst abdomen. On day 24 the patient died from disease progression.
L1108: Patient (b) (6) was a 76-year-old man with DLBCL with prior treatment including R-CHOP. Past medical history included hypoacusis, gastric ulcer, peripheral arterial occlusive disease, peripheral edema and cardiac failure. Medications at the time of sudden death included lansoprazole, acetylsalicylic acid, allopurinol and enoxaparin sodium. On study day 17 during cycle 1 the patient had sudden death at home. According to the investigator probable cause of death was cardiac arrhythmia or ischemic heart disease. An autopsy was not performed. There was no medical data of his death.
L1109: Patient (b) (6) was a 75-year-old women with DLBCL, stage IV with prior treatment of 1 line of therapy including R-COMP (combined rituximab, cyclophosphamide, doxorubicin, prednisone and rituximab). The patient had an ECOG score of 0 at screening. Past medical history included thrombocytopenia. Concurrent medical conditions included hypertension and anemia. TEAEs during the trial included deep vein thrombosis, neutropenia, dyspnea and congestive heart failure which lead to treatment discontinuation during cycle 7. The patient was diagnosed with non-serious obstructive hypertrophic cardiomyopathy on day 190 with prior pneumonia and pleural effusion. Six days later, the patient was diagnosed with grade 3 congestive heart failure thought to be due to progressive disease. On day 211 treatment with both study drugs was discontinued and the patient died on day 214 with a cause of death reported as diffuse large B cell lymphoma.
L1110: Patient (b) (6) was a 55-year-old man with DLBCL, stage IV with prior treatment including R-CHOP as first line and R-ESHAP as second line. ECOG at screening was 1. Past medical history included tonsillectomy, vasectomy, prior tobacco use, dyspnea, hypomagnesemia and pyrexia. Concurrent conditions included glomerulonephritis, sleep apnea, pleural effusion and anemia. On study day 43 the patient had two TEAEs of grade 3 febrile neutropenia and grade 5 respiratory failure. The last dose of tafasitamab was on day 36 and the last dose of lenalidomide was day 42. The patient had dyspnea, temperature of 37.8°C, oxygen saturation of 88%, general malaise and a non-tender, nonpruritic maculopapular rash on both flanks. The PleurX catheter was noted to be leaking a small amount of fluid. On day 44 the patient was hospitalized due to multifactorial respiratory failure with a respiratory superinfection and pleural effusion of neoplastic nature. The patient was diagnosed with adenovirus infection. Despite treatment, the patient's condition progressed and the patient died on day 51 after being placed on palliative measures.
L1111: Patient (b) (6) was a 76-year-old man with DLBCL with prior therapy including RCHOP, DHAP
L1112: (cisplatin, cytarabine, dexamethasone) rituximab/pixantrone, cyclophosphamide/doxorubicin, radiation and right lower leg amputation. Prior medical history included prostatectomy, sleep apnea, atrial fibrillation, hiatal hernia, mitral valve incompetence, obesity, drug hypersensitivity, back pain, peripheral and localized edema and anemia. During cycle 1 day 8 the patient developed the SAE of soft tissue infection. The soft tissue infection was due to lymphoma necrosis, the infection was caused by several bacteria. The soft tissue infection prolonged an existing hospitalization. As a result of the soft tissue infection his study treatment was held for 10 days. The soft tissue infection resolved and the patient remained hospitalized for ongoing treatment with study medication. The patient died 12 days after resolution of the soft tissue infection due to progressive disease.
L1113: Patient (b) (6) was a 75-year-old woman with DLBCL with prior therapy including RCHOP, and R-GEMOX (rituximab, gemcitabine, oxaliplatin). The patient had a medical history of hypertension, sleep apnea, arthralgia, diabetes mellitus, breast neoplasm, atrial fibrillation, depression dyslipidemia, pulmonary hypertension and urinary tract infection. On day 19 of cycle 1 the patient developed cognitive disorder grade 2 associated with a urinary tract infection and was hospitalized. During the hospitalization the patient developed a deep vein thrombosis due to disease progression. The enlargement of the inguinal adenopathy produced compression. The patient died from disease progression 38 days after the start of treatment and 21 days after the last dose of study medication.
L1114: Patient (b) (6) was 68-year-old man with DLBCL with prior treatment including R-CHOP, and rituximab ifosfamide, gemcitabine, and vinorelbine for his second line of treatment. Medical history included nosocomial infection and nausea. Concurrent conditions included tuberculosis, myocardial ischemia, myelodysplastic syndrome, anemia and thrombocytopenia, hydronephrosis, fatigue and axillary mass. On cycle 1 day 1, the patient had an AE of acute kidney injury grade 2. On study day 2 cycle 1 that patient had an AE of loss of consciousness grade 1. The patient was put on palliative treatment after 2 infusions of tafasitamab, cycle 1 day 1 and day 4. The patient did not receive lenalidomide due to the kidney injury. He died on day 13 due to disease progression.

L1117: Of these serious TEAE, a causal relationship to tafasitamab alone was suspected for a total of 16 TEAEs in 14 patients (see Table 36).

L1126: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1127: These results indicate risks which are consistent with the target population of patients with R/R-DLBCL receiving B-cell depleting therapy.
L1128: The cases of tumor lysis syndrome seen in the monotherapy trials are indicative of tumor types treated. There were no cases of tumor lysis syndrome in MOR208C203 in target population.
L1129: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1130: Regarding the above analysis, for Table 36 a causal relationship to tafasitamab was suspected. In a single arm trial, attribution of causality is difficult and all AEs regardless of attribution should be considered for evaluation of safety. For trial MOR208C203 for an SAE to be included in this analysis the TEAE had to be considered not related to lenalidomide. The serious adverse events, limited to 1 per patient at the TEAE and at the System Organ Class level, obtained by
L1131: the reviewer is displayed in Table 38. As shown in the table, the majority of SAEs were in the System Organ Class of infections and infestations, with the most common SAE of pneumonia at 7.4% in this System Organ Class.

L1135: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1136: In MOR208C203 a total of 20 (24.7%) patients discontinued study medication due to a TEAE. Of these, 8 patients discontinued lenalidomide alone, 10 discontinued both tafasitamab and lenalidomide, and 2 patients discontinued only tafasitamab.
L1137: After the cessation of lenalidomide in the combination therapy phase in MOR208C203, one (1/40 [2.5%]) patient on extended tafasitamab monotherapy discontinued study treatment in response to a TEAE (marginal zone lymphoma recurrent).
L1138: For the pooled monotherapy trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, and MOR208C202), the majority of the 10 patients who discontinued tafasitamab for a TEAE did so as a result of myelosuppression or infection.
L1139: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1140: A low number of patients discontinue tafasitamab due to a TEAE. The pattern of TEAEs leading to discontinuation is consistent with a heavily pretreated population receiving B-cell depleting therapy.
L1141: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1142: The clinical reviewer obtained similar data to that presented above. A total of 20 patients in trial MOR208C203 discontinued study treatment, either both drugs or either drug alone. In reviewing the TEAE that lead to withdrawal of study medication for either drug or both, on trial MOR208C203 neutropenia was the most frequent TEAE reported for 3 patients; thrombocytopenia lead to discontinuation for 1 patient in this trial. At the system organ class level, 5 patients (6.2%) had a TEAE in infections and infestations that led to withdrawal of either tafasitamab or lenalidomide, 4 patients (4.9%) the TEAE was in blood and lymphatic system disorders, and 2 patients (2.5%) for each of the following system organ classes, cardiac disorders, neoplasms benign malignant and unspecified, nervous system disorders, respiratory, thoracic and mediastinal disorders and skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders.
L1143: ## **Dose Modification Due to Adverse Effects**
L1144: In the pivotal trial MOR208C203 (L-MIND), 55/81 (68.8%) patients experienced 184 TEAEs that resulted in study drug modification (i.e., study drug interruption or dose reduction) during the combination therapy phase. The most frequent TEAE resulting in study drug modification of either tafasitamab or lenalidomide was neutropenia (28 [35.0%]).
L1145: In the monotherapy trials, a total of 59 (41.8%) patients experienced in TEAE resulting in study drug modification, the most frequent of these being infusion related reactions, neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, in total 59 patients.
L1146: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1147: The pattern of TEAEs leading to dose modification (reduction or interruption) is consistent with a heavily pretreated population receiving B-cell depleting therapy.
L1148: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1149: For the L-MIND trial, a total of 57 patients (70.4%) had 222 TEAE that resulted in dose reduction, interruption of both drugs or either drug alone regardless of the phase of the trial. TEAE which resulted in a dose interruption or dose reduction is shown in Table 39. As shown in the table, the majority of these TEAE were in the SOC of Blood and lymphatic system disorders and the SOC of infections and infestations.

L1153: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1154: Three specific risks were identified which are explained in detail in Section 8.2.5.
L1155: Other significant adverse events leading to modification and/or discontinuation of study medication are described in detail under **Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects** in this section.
L1156: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1157: The most common and significant AEs were linked to myelosuppression and infections; infusion related reactions were seen in R/R DLBCL patients treated with tafasitamab/lenalidomide. The proposed USPI recommends pre-medication including antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor blockers, and glucocorticosteroids.
L1158: Overall, the adverse events seen in the clinical development program of tafasitamab are consistent with the mode of action of a B-cell depleting drug in the target population.
L1159: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1160: The FDA agrees that myelosuppression, infections, and infusion related reactions are significant adverse events and these will be Warnings and Precautions in the U.S. Prescribing Information.
L1161: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions:
L1162: ## Most Frequent Adverse Events by Preferred Term
L1163: TEAEs in the safety analysis are listed in Table 44.
L1164: When patients completed the tafasitamab/lenalidomide therapy phase and continued on tafasitamab monotherapy the frequency of hematological and non-hematological TEAEs decreased.
L1165: ## Hematologic TEAEs
L1166: The following differences in the incidences of the most frequently reported hematological TEAEs in the Combination Therapy phase in MOR208C203 compared with the pooled monotherapy trials were noted (see also Table 40).
L1167: - Neutropenia (48.8% versus 12.8%),
- Anemia (33.8% versus 10.6%),
- Thrombocytopenia (31.3% versus 10.6%),
L1168: The frequencies of hematologic events in the combination phase of MOR208C203 were similar to those described the lenalidomide monotherapy trials in the lenalidomide USPI (REVLIMID USPI, Revised: 10/2019).
L1169: ## Non-hematologic TEAEs
L1170: In the MOR208C203 combination therapy phase, 4/80 (5.0%) patients experienced an infusion-related reaction, compared with 43/141 (30.5%) patients in the tafasitamab monotherapy trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202).
L1171: The following differences (i.e., at least +10%) in the incidences of the most frequently reported non-hematological TEAEs in the Combination Therapy phase in MOR208C203 compared with the pooled monotherapy trials were noted (see also Table 40)
L1172: - Diarrhea (28.8% versus 14.2%),
- Asthenia (23.8% versus 5.7%),
- Decreased appetite (20.0% versus 5.0%),
- Edema peripheral (21.3% versus 7.8%),
L1173: The frequencies of non-hematologic events in the combination phase of MOR208C203 were similar to those described the lenalidomide monotherapy trials in the lenalidomide USPI (REVLIMID USPI, Revised: 10/2019).

L1189: ## Severity of Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
L1190: The Grade 3 and 4 TEAEs in the safety analysis are presented in Table 42. The most frequently occurring Grade 3/4 TEAEs are hematological (neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, leukopenia, anemia, febrile neutropenia).
L1191: When patients completed the tafasitamab/lenalidomide therapy phase and continued on tafasitamab monotherapy the frequency of Grade 3/4 TEAEs decreased.
L1192: ## Grade 3/4 Hematologic TEAEs
L1193: Compared to the pooled monotherapy trials, the following differences in the incidence (i.e., at least +10%) of the most frequently Grade 3/4 TEAEs reported in the Combination Therapy phase in MOR208C203 were noted for the following hematologic TEAEs Table 42:
L1194: - Grade 3/4 neutropenia (38/80 [47.5%] versus 14/141 [9.9%] patients, respectively).
- Grade 3/4 thrombocytopenia (14/80 [17.5%] versus 7/141 [5.0%] patients, respectively).
L1195: The frequencies of Grade 3/4 neutropenia and thrombocytopenia in the combination phase of MOR208C203 were similar to those described for the lenalidomide monotherapy trials in the lenalidomide USPI (REVLIMID USPI, Revised: 10/2019).
L1196: ## Grade 3/4 Non-hematologic TEAEs
L1197: None of the most frequent Grade 3/4 TEAEs in MOR208C203 Combination Therapy phase differed in frequency by \ge 10\% compared with the pooled monotherapy trials (Table 42).
L1198: Overall, the incidence of Grade 3/4 non-hematologic TEAEs reported in the MOR208C203 Combination Therapy phase did not differ from the incidence described for the lenalidomide monotherapy trials in the lenalidomide USPI (REVLIMID USPI, Revised: 10/2019).

L1234: ## **Laboratory Findings**
L1235: Hematology parameters to be summarized included platelets, hemoglobin, WBC count, absolute neutrophil count, and lymphocytes; blood chemistry parameters included sodium, magnesium, potassium, calcium, AST, ALT, serum creatinine, BILI, and ALP. Shift tables using CTCAE grades to compare baseline to the worst post-baseline CTCAE grade were produced for applicable hematology and biochemistry laboratory parameters. The grades for laboratory parameters are based on the numeric component of the CTCAE grading scale v4.0. Per protocol, abnormal laboratory values considered clinically significant by the investigator were to be reported as AEs.
L1236: Changes in hematological parameters were analyzed for patients with both baseline and post baseline values. Overall, the observed changes in MOR208C203 were consistent with the reported hematologic TEAEs:
L1237: - Hemoglobin: 4/81 (4.9%) patients had a shift of 2-3 grades toward worsening CTCAE grade.
- Neutrophil Count: 14 (17.3%) patients of 2-3 grades toward worsening CTCAE grade.
- Platelet Count: 1/81 (1.2%) patients had a shift of 2-3 grades toward worsening CTCAE grade.
L1238: The hematological parameters \ge Grade 3 anemia, leukopenia, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia are of special interest. The number and percent of patients demonstrating any of the following values at least once during the On-Treatment Phase are shown below for the pooled monotherapy trials and for MOR208C203:
L1239: - Hemoglobin &lt; 8 g/dL (16 [11.3%] versus 1 [1.3%])
- Leukocytes &lt; 2 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} (29 [20.6%] versus 10 [12.5%])
- Neutrophils &lt; 1 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} (41 [29.1%] versus 16 [20.0%])
- Platelets &lt; 50 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L} (28 [19.9%] versus 3 [3.8%])
L1240: Mean baseline and end-of-treatment values and change from baseline values for serum chemistries were analyzed. No clinically relevant changes were observed in any parameter. There were no cases with laboratory values that fulfilled the criteria for potential Hy's Law.
L1241: ## The Applicant's Position:

L1253: ## Vital Signs

The data collected in different units were converted into standard international (SI) units for summary. L1259: The clinical reviewer reviewed the Applicant's analysis.
L1260: ## **Electrocardiograms (ECGs) and QT**
L1261: An analysis of recordings from patients enrolled in L-MIND did not reveal any abnormal QTc prolongation or any other electrocardiographic abnormality potentially associated with the study drug.
L1262: ## **Immunogenicity**
L1263: The clinical assessment of immunogenicity for tafasitamab was evaluated in 245 patients, of whom 81 (33.1%) of the 245 patients were part of MOR208C203. The other 164 (66.9%) patients were derived from the four other clinical trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and MOR208C205 (COSMOS), in which tafasitamab was tested in different indications and/or different treatment settings compared with MOR208C203. In MOR208C203, mean exposure time to tafasitamab was 49.3 weeks, and anti-drug antibody (ADA) samples were collected for up to two years.
L1264: A low number of patients (17 [6.9%] of the 245 patients overall) tested ADA-positive before start of tafasitamab treatment, suggesting the presence of pre-existing antibodies. Only six of these patients with pre-existing ADAs were ADA-positive on an intermittent basis after start of tafasitamab treatment (i.e., ADA positive at one to seven sampling time points). The other 11 of these patients with pre-existing ADAs did not show any positive ADA assessment during treatment. No patients testing negative before the start of tafasitamab treatment subsequently tested positive. Thus, in all five clinical trials, no treatment-emergent or treatment-boosted ADAs could be detected. The ADA titers were low, and there was no apparent clinical impact of ADAs on PK, safety, or efficacy.
L1265: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1266: No clinically significant immunogenicity was detected for tafasitamab.
L1267: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1268: Refer to section 6.2.1 immunogenicity.
L1269: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1270: Specific AEs related to infusion-related reactions, myelosuppression, and infections were identified as part of the comprehensive safety review during the development of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide.
L1271: ## Myelosuppression
L1272: Treatment with tafasitamab can cause serious or severe myelosuppression including neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and anemia. In patients treated with tafasitamab and lenalidomide (n = 81), Grade 3 hematologic adverse reactions occurring in \ge 5\% of patients included neutropenia (25%), thrombocytopenia (12%), febrile neutropenia (10%), leukopenia (9%), and anemia (7%). Grade 4 hematologic adverse reactions occurring in \ge 5\% of recipients included neutropenia (25%) and thrombocytopenia (5%). Cytopenia's were the most common reason for treatment discontinuation (1% of patients due to tafasitamab; 5% of patients due to lenalidomide).
L1273: **Neutropenia, Grade 3 or Higher:** Neutropenia is a known adverse effect associated with lenalidomide treatment. Events of neutropenia occurred more frequently in patients receiving tafasitamab-lenalidomide combination therapy in MOR208C203 compared with patients receiving tafasitamab monotherapy in the monotherapy trials, and the number declined in the tafasitamab monotherapy extension phase of MOR208C203. There were no fatal neutropenic events.
L1274: In the pooled monotherapy trials, 14/141 (9.9%) patients experienced neutropenia Grade \ge 3 compared with 40/81 (49.4%) patients in MOR208C203. In the pooled monotherapy trials, 7/141 (5.0%) patients experienced febrile neutropenia Grade \ge 3 compared with 10/81 (12.3%) patients in MOR208C203. One patient in MOR208C203 experienced Grade 3 neutropenic sepsis while receiving combination therapy.
L1275: Most Grade \ge 3 neutropenia events (preferred terms of neutropenia, febrile neutropenia, neutropenic sepsis, neutrophil count decreased) were non-serious (140 events in 64 patients). Onset of Grade \ge 3 neutropenia events occurred earlier in the pooled monotherapy trials compared with MOR208C203 which may be explained by the different tumor types. The median duration of events was similar in pooled monotherapy trials and MOR208C203 (7 to 8 days) (Table 46).
L1276: **Table 46: Time to Onset and Duration of Neutropenia Events, Grade 3 or Higher (Safety Analysis Set)**

L1281: [d] Duration of AE is defined as start of AE to end of AE. For multiple AEs from same patient, all the events were considered.
L1282: There were 15 serious neutropenia events in 11 patients (6/141 [4.3%] patients in monotherapy trials; 5/81 [6.2%] patients in MOR208C203). Of the 15 SAEs, 14 were events of febrile neutropenia and one event of neutropenic sepsis. All patients recovered except for one. The remaining patient recovered with sequelae of disease progression, assessed as unrelated to tafasitamab (Patient (b) (6) in MOR208C202). For the five patients with six serious neutropenia events in MOR208C203, tafasitamab-lenalidomide combination therapy was temporarily interrupted for three events, was not stopped for two events, and action taken with drug was 'not applicable' for the remaining event. For the six patients in the monotherapy studies with nine serious neutropenia events, tafasitamab therapy was withdrawn for one event, interrupted for one event, continued unchanged for four events, and action taken with drug was 'not applicable' for the remaining three events. All 11 patients required treatment, typically with antibiotics, G-CSF, or other supportive treatment, as appropriate.
L1283: The neutropenia profile for the tafasitamab-lenalidomide combination therapy is consistent with that documented for lenalidomide therapy in multiple myeloma patients as described in the lenalidomide USPI (REVLIMID USPI, Revised: 10/2019).
L1284: ## Infections
L1285: Bacterial, fungal, and new or reactivated viral infections can occur during and following tafasitamab therapy. Grade 3 or higher infections occurred in 30% of patients treated with tafasitamab and lenalidomide (n=81). Infection-related deaths were reported in 1% (1/81) of patients within 30 days of last treatment. Pneumonia was the most frequently reported serious infection occurring in 7% (6/81) patients. Infections occurring in ≥5% of patients included
L1286: bronchitis (16%), nasopharyngitis (10%), pneumonia (10%), respiratory tract infection (10%), upper respiratory tract infection (10%), urinary tract infections (10%), gastroenteritis (6%), escherichia urinary tract infection (5%), rhinitis (5%), sinusitis (5%), and lower respiratory tract infection (5%).
L1287: **Infections, Grade 3 or Higher:** For the analysis of infections, all serious AEs were considered. In addition, to capture non-serious infections of interest (Grade 3 or higher), a Customized MedDRA Query (CMQ) with categories of infectious pneumonia, sepsis and urogenital tract infections was used.
L1288: As of the data cutoff date of June 30, 2019, a total of 16/141 (11.3%) patients experienced 22 serious TEAEs of infection in the pooled monotherapy trials. In MOR208C203 overall, a total of 21/81 (25.9%) patients experienced 32 serious such TEAEs. More infections were observed in the MOR208C203 Combination Therapy phase of (18/80 [22.5%] patients) than in the Tafasitamab Monotherapy phase (4/40 [10.0%] patients).
L1289: Grade 3 or higher infections were reported for 43 patients in total with 19/141 (13.5%) patients with 27 events in the pooled monotherapy trials and 24/81 (29.6%) patients with 36 events in MOR208C203 overall. The majority of these events of infections were pneumonia events (20/63) in 17 of 222 (7.7%) patients including the preferred terms of pneumonia, pneumonia viral, pneumonia pneumococcal, pneumonia Klebsiella, pneumonia Hemophilus. Pneumonia events led to sepsis events in nine of 222 (4.0%) patients (PTs: sepsis, bacterial sepsis, Klebsiella sepsis, Streptococcal sepsis, neutropenic sepsis reported).
L1290: In MOR208C203, 5/81 (6.2%) patients had infections of Grade 3 or higher assessed as related to both tafasitamab and lenalidomide; 2/81 (2.5%) patients had infection events of Grade 3 or higher assessed as suspected to tafasitamab alone; and 1/81 (1.2%) patients had infection events of Grade 3 or higher assessed as suspected to lenalidomide alone.
L1291: In the pooled monotherapy trials, one infection &gt; Grade 3 was assessed as related to tafasitamab alone.
L1292: ## Serious herpes virus or CMV infections of Grade 3 or higher
L1293: Three patients developed serious infections with a herpes virus or CMV:
L1294: - One 90-year-old male patient in MOR208C201 suffered genital herpes zoster which was of Grade 3, assessed as suspected to tafasitamab treatment; the patient recovered.
- One 70-year-old female patient in MOR208C203 developed disseminated varicella zoster in blood, gut, lungs and liver of Grade 4, which was assessed as not related to any study drug. Confounding factor was reported as decreased immunity due to the underlying leukemia. The patient recovered.
- Also in MOR208C203, one 83-year-old female developed a Grade 3 CMV infection suspected to be associated with colitis. The investigator assessed as related to tafasitamab, not related to lenalidomide; the patient recovered.
L1295: ## Infection events with a fatal outcome
L1296: In the pooled monotherapy trials, three patients had infection events with a fatal outcome, all in Cycle 1; all three deaths were not considered related to tafasitamab (Table 35):
L1297: - One patient died from *Stenotrophomonas maltophilia* sepsis.
- One patient died from *Enterococcus faecium* sepsis in multi-organ failure.
- One patient died from pneumonia in respiratory and cardiac failure.
L1298: Patient (b) (6) (MOR208C203) experienced multifactorial respiratory failure on study day 43 in the context of a respiratory superinfection (adenovirus), neutropenic fever, pleural effusion of neoplastic nature and mixed acidosis. The event was likely to have resulted from suspected lymphoma progression (pleural fluid was of neoplastic nature). The patient died 8 days after event onset (Study Day 51).
L1299: In the MOR208C203 study, one patient experienced a worsening of progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy with a fatal outcome. The patient first developed unspecific neurological symptoms ten days prior to initiation of study treatment, which worsened while on study drug. The case was assessed as not related to any study drug. The patient died more than 30 days after cessation of study therapy.
L1300: Infections are considered an identified risk of treatment with tafasitamab. Most patients recovered after receiving standard antibiotic treatment. The five patients who died were severely debilitated with underlying comorbidities that predisposed them to risk of infection with poor outcome.
L1301: ## Infusion-Related Reactions
L1302: In the tafasitamab monotherapy trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202) which were performed in the indications CSL/CLL, NHL and B-ALL) approximately 30% of the patient enrolled in the studies experienced an infusion related reaction. In the L-MIND Study in R/R DLBCL, 5 (6%) patients experienced an infusion reaction; all were Grade 1 and resolved on the same day of occurrence. The majority of these reactions occurred during the first infusion. Symptoms included chills, flushing, dyspnea, and hypertension. Tafasitamab infusion was interrupted then resumed for 3 patients and supportive treatment was required for 3 of these 5 patients. Premedication including antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor blockers, and glucocorticosteroids in the first cycle is recommended in the USPI.
L1303: **Infusion-Related Reactions, Grade 3 or Higher:** A Customized MedDRA Query 'Infusion-related reaction' was used to identify cases of possible interest for all toxicity grades. In the pooled monotherapy trials, a total of 43/141 (30.5%) patients experienced 49 events of infusion-related reaction (CMQ), of which two patients had an IRR of Grade 3 or greater. In MOR208C203, 5/81 (6.2%) patients experienced five events of infusion-related reaction (CMQ) during the first infusion, all of which were Grade 1 in severity.
L1304: Per protocol, patients in the monotherapy trials and in MOR208C203 received pre-medication including antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor blockers, and glucocorticosteroids in the first cycle, at the discretion of the Investigators.
L1305: In the monotherapy studies and in MOR208C203, the majority of infusion-related reactions occurred during the first infusion (86%). One case of infusion-related reaction (dyspnea) was considered to be serious and required discontinuation of tafasitamab treatment. The two Grade 3/4 cases are summarized in Table 47.
L1306: **Table 47: Analysis of Infusion-Related Reactions, Grade 3 or Higher (Safety Analysis Set)**

L1308: Abbreviations: C=cycle; D=day; F=female; ID=identification; M=male; MedDRA=Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities; NA=not applicable; VT=verbatim term.
L1309: The majority of infusion-related reactions were accompanied by symptoms of flushing (22/141 [15.6%]) and nausea (18/141 [12.8%] patients) in the pooled monotherapy trials. In MOR208C203, infusion-related reactions were characterized by chills (2/81 [2.5%] patients) and hypertension, increased systolic blood pressure, and pain in the renal lodge (1/81 [1.2%] patient each).
L1310: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1311: Specific safety issues of tafasitamab concern adverse events which are consistent with the targeted patient population and the pharmacological mode of action of a B-cell depleting drug.:
L1312: Adverse events linked to myelosuppression, in particular neutropenia, anemia and thrombocytopenia are the primarily expected events with the combination therapy with tafasitamab and lenalidomide due to its B-cell depleting properties.
L1313: Infections are expected events with the combination therapy with tafasitamab and lenalidomide due to its B-cell depleting properties. The most common infections are respiratory tract and urinary tract infections. High Grade infections and poor outcomes are also considered to be due to the underlying conditions of the patients.
L1314: Infusion related reactions events were observed, all of which resolved without serious sequelae. Infusion related reactions typically occurred during the first infusion and tended to be mild in severity. All patients in the clinical trials received standard prophylactic treatment which appears to be sufficient to manage these reactions.
L1315: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1316: The FDA agrees that infusion related reactions, myelosuppression, and infections are important safety risks with tafasitamab. The FDA analysis of the data from the L-MIND trial will be included in the USPI.
L1317: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1318: Patient-reported outcomes were not included in the trials of tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide.
L1319: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1320: The clinical reviewer is in agreement.
L1321: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1322: Subgroup evaluations of TEAEs were conducted for patients in MOR208C203 to identify potential safety issues in particular subpopulations defined by intrinsic/extrinsic factors: age, sex, race, geography, and manufacturing process. Findings are as follows:
L1323: ## Age
L1324: Among 80 patients treated with tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide in MOR208C203, 56 (69%) patients were &gt; 65 years of age, 45 (56%) patients were &gt; 70 years of age. TEAEs were reported for 100% of patients aged ≤ 65 years and for 98.2% of patients aged &gt; 65 years, and for 100% of patients aged &gt; 70 years.
L1325: Serious events occurred in 9/25 (36.0%) of patients ≤ 65 years old and 26/55 (47.3%) &gt; 65 years old. The numerical difference in serious events was driven by patients &gt; 65 years of age reporting more cardiac and nervous system events. Grade ≥3 TEAEs occurred in 18/25 (72.0%) patients ≤ 65 years and 43/55 (78.2%) patients &gt;65 years of age.
L1326: Incidences of hematological events and infections were similar between the age groups: Patients aged ≤ 65 years and those aged &gt; 65 years had similar incidences of hematological AEs (neutropenia [48.0% and 49.1%, respectively] and thrombocytopenia [32.0% and 30.9%, respectively]).
L1327: The overall incidence of infections was also similar between patients aged \le 65 years and those aged &gt; 65 years (68.0% and 67.3%, respectively). Events occurring more frequently with a difference of \ge 10% in patients aged &gt; 65 years compared with those \le 65 years included: diarrhea, abdominal pain, asthenia, peripheral edema, decreased appetite, back pain, hyperkalemia, hypokalemia, and hypomagnesemia.
L1328: ## Sex
L1329: Out of 80 patients receiving combination treatment in MOR208C203, 43 (53.8%) patients were male and 37 (46.2%) patients were female. Overall, the incidence of TEAEs was similar between male and female patients. Male patients and female patients had a similar rate of hematological events of neutropenia, anemia, thrombocytopenia and leukopenia.
L1330: For non-hematological events, the incidence of infusion-related reactions was similar between the sexes. Events occurring with a difference of \ge 10% in females compared with males included asthenia, nausea, and vomiting. Events occurring with a difference of \ge 10% in males compared with females included constipation, muscle spasms, back pain, and blood creatinine increased.
L1331: Grade \ge3 TEAEs occurred in 35/43 (81.4%) of male patients and in 26/37 (70.3%) of female patients. The incidences of Grade \ge3 hematologic events of neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia was similar between male and female patients. The overall incidence of infections was also similar.
L1332: Serious events occurred in 21/43 (48.8%) male patients and in 14/37 (37.8%) female patients. The incidences of serious neutropenia, thrombocytopenia and infections were similar between male and female patients.
L1333: ## Race
L1334: Of the 74 patients receiving combination therapy in MOR208C203 who self-reported race, 72 identified as White and two identified as Non-white. In the pooled monotherapy trials, a total of 131 patients were White and ten patients were Non-White. As the number of Non-White patients is very small, meaningful comparisons could not be made with respect to adverse event patterns between these two groups.
L1335: ## Geography
L1336: In the MOR208C203, six patients were from the U.S., and 75 patients were from Europe. Consequently, numbers of patients from the U.S. were too small to provide meaningful comparisons of TEAEs by geographic region.
L1337: ## Manufacturing Process
L1338: For the MOR208C203, an analysis of TEAEs by manufacturing process (b) (4) was performed. It was not possible to draw any meaningful conclusions, as there was a great degree of overlap in patients who received tafasitamab manufactured by both processes, leading to confounding. All patients received tafasitamab manufactured by (b) (4); 45 (55.6%) of these patients received tafasitamab from (b) (4) only while 36 (44.4%) switched from (b) (4) to (b) (4) during the course of the treatment. No patients received tafasitamab from (b) (4) only.
L1339: The median time to switch from (b) (4) was 11.925 months, indicating a longer co-exposure of the (b) (4) treated patients with lenalidomide as compared to (b) (4) patients. Therefore, the summary of TEAEs by (b) (4) is also confounded with toxicities induced by lenalidomide and is of limited value to compare with (b) (4).
L1340: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1341: The subgroup results were similar to those from the primary analysis for safety, which evaluated factors including age, sex, and race. The incidences of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia and infections were similar between the younger and older patients. Male patients and female patients had a similar rate of hematological events and Grade \ge 3 hematologic events.
L1342: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1343: The FDA subgroup analysis for age and sex is shown below. An analysis of TEAE based on age \ge 65 and &lt; 65 was done. For trial MOR208C203, 58 patients were \ge 65 and 23 patients were &lt; 65. All patients had at least 1 TEAE. TEAE that occurred in more than 10% of patients based on age \ge 65 and &lt; 65 are shown in Table 48.

L1390: The clinical reviewer agrees with the Applicant regarding an analysis based on race and geography and the inability to make meaningful comparisons.
L1391: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials

L1395: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L1396: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1397: In MOR208C203, the median time on tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide was 190.0 days (range: 3 to 436 days). The cumulative dose administered ranged from 1,567.9 to 88,854.6 mg for tafasitamab and 75.0 to 6,300.0 mg for lenalidomide. The proportion of patients in MOR208C203 who received \ge12 cycles of tafasitamab monotherapy was 42.0%. One patient in MOR208C203 completed 1,170 days (167.1 weeks) of tafasitamab treatment and one patient in MOR208C201 completed 1,998 days (285.4 weeks) of tafasitamab treatment (Table 31). The following five preferred terms were reported in the Combination Therapy phase in MOR208C203, with no event reported in more than one patient: osteoma, prostatic adenoma, squamous cell carcinoma, benign neoplasm of thyroid gland, and lipoma. In the tafasitamab Monotherapy phase, the events of marginal zone lymphoma recurrent and basal cell carcinoma were reported.
L1398: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1399: Treatment-emergent AEs of neoplasia were observed with tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide. Carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted, and limited long-term data preclude definitive evaluation of carcinogenicity.
L1400: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1401: The safety database was reviewed for possible secondary malignancies. For trial MOR208C203 a 58-year-old man developed basal cell carcinoma, a 71-year-old man developed prostatic specific antigen increase, a 81-year-old man developed squamous cell carcinoma, an 82-year-old man developed paraneoplastic syndrome and a 68-year-old woman was had a TEAE of myelodysplastic syndrome. On trial MOR208C201 a 69-year-old man had a TEAE of myelodysplastic syndrome and on trial XmAb5574-01 a 84-year-old man had a TEAE of pituitary tumor. Regarding trial MOR208C203 and secondary malignancies, as this is a single arm trial which included lenalidomide, attribution to tafasitamab cannot be determined.
L1402: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L1403: No pre-clinical reproductive and developmental toxicity studies have been conducted to date. The teratogenic potential of tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide has not been established. There are no data on use in pregnant women.
L1404: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1405: Use in pregnant women is not recommended.
L1406: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1407: Tafasitamab is used in combination with lenalidomide and the use of lenalidomide is contraindicated in pregnant women. This will be stated in the U.S. Prescribing Information.
L1408: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1409: Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma is a cancer of B cells, a type of lymphocyte that is responsible for producing antibodies. It is the most common form of non-Hodgkin lymphoma among adults, with an annual incidence of 7–8 cases per 100,000 people per year in the U.S. This cancer occurs primarily in older individuals, with a median age of diagnosis of 65 years, although it can occur in young adults and, in rare cases, children. Conducting pediatric studies for tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide in the treatment of relapsed or refractory DLBCL is highly impracticable.
L1410: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1411: No data are available for the use of tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide in combination; use in children is not recommended.
L1412: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1413: The safety and effectiveness of tafasitamab in pediatric patients has not been established.
L1414: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1415: There is currently no information available regarding overdose with tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide. The highest dose that has been administered is 12.0 mg/kg, and no dose-limiting toxicity has been observed. Drug abuse and dependence with tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide have not been studied and is not likely. No studies have been conducted to specifically assess withdrawal or rebound effects of tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide.
L1416: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1417: In the event of overdose, patients should be observed, and appropriate supportive care should be given, if required. Given the nature of tafasitamab and its IV administration by a licensed health care professional, no abuse or dependence is anticipated. Given the disease being treated and the nature of tafasitamab, no withdrawal or rebound effects are anticipated.
L1418: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1419: The clinical reviewer agrees with the statement above.

L1433: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1434: In decreasing order of frequency in trial MOR208C203, the most frequently reported (\ge 20\% of patients) TEAEs by PT were neutropenia (39/81 [48.8%]), anemia (27/81 [33.8%]), thrombocytopenia (25/81 [31.3%]), diarrhea (23/81 [28.8%]), asthenia (19/81 [23.8%]), edema peripheral (17/81 [21.3%]), pyrexia (17/81 [21.3%]), decreased appetite (16/81 [20.0%]).
L1435: When patients completed combination therapy with tafasitamab and lenalidomide and continued with tafasitamab monotherapy the incidences of hematologic TEAEs decreased. and non-hematologic TEAEs tended to decrease, some by as much as 10% (neutropenia, anemia, thrombocytopenia, febrile neutropenia, decreased appetite, asthenia, hypokalemia, constipation, nausea, muscle spasms, and dyspnea).
L1436: As of the data cutoff date of June 30, 2019, there were 59 serious TEAEs experienced by 37/141 (26.2%) patients in the three tafasitamab monotherapy trials (MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and XmAb5574-01) and 76 serious TEAEs experienced by 42/81 (51.9%) patients in MOR208C203.
L1437: Of these serious TEAEs, a causal relationship to tafasitamab alone was suspected in two serious TEAEs (febrile neutropenia and tumor lysis syndrome) reported for 2/27 (7.4%) patients in XmAb5574-01; four serious TEAEs (genital herpes zoster, febrile neutropenia, myelodysplastic syndrome, and infusion-related reaction) reported for 4/92 (4.3%) patients in MOR208C201; six serious TEAEs (fungal infection, pneumonia viral, febrile neutropenia, cholangitis sclerosing, and tumor lysis syndrome [x2]) reported for 4/22 (18.2%) patients in MOR208C202; and four serious TEAEs (pneumonia, *Streptococcal* sepsis, pyrexia, and cytomegalovirus infection) reported for 4/81 (4.9%) patients in MOR208C203.
L1438: In the pooled monotherapy trials, 14/141 (9.9%) patients experienced neutropenia Grade \ge 3 compared with 40/81 (49.4%) patients in MOR208C203. In the pooled monotherapy trials, 7/141 (5.0%) patients experienced febrile neutropenia Grade \ge 3 compared with 10/81 (12.3%) patients in MOR208C203. One patient in MOR208C203 experienced Grade 3 neutropenic sepsis while receiving combination therapy.
L1439: Most Grade \ge 3 neutropenia events (preferred terms of neutropenia, febrile neutropenia, neutropenic sepsis, neutrophil count decreased) were non-serious (140 events in 64 patients). Onset of Grade \ge 3 neutropenia events occurred earlier in the pooled monotherapy trials compared with MOR208C203. The median duration of events was similar in pooled monotherapy trials and MOR208C203 (7 to 8 days).
L1440: Grade 3 or higher infections were reported for 43 patients in total with 19/141 (13.5%) patients with 27 events in the pooled monotherapy trials and 24/81 (29.6%) patients with 36 events in MOR208C203 overall. The majority of these events of infections were pneumonia events (20/63) in 17 of 222 (7.7%) patients including the preferred terms of pneumonia, pneumonia viral, pneumonia pneumococcal, pneumonia klebsiella, pneumonia hemophilis. The IgG profile was investigated; patients from trials MOR208C203 and MOR208C201 were grouped in two categories: IgG levels &lt; 400 mg/dL and \ge 400 mg/dL. Patients with IgG levels &lt; 400 mg/dL did
L1441: have a higher incidence of infections of interest (in particular pneumonias) than patients with IgG levels \ge 400 mg/dL.
L1442: The incidences of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia and infections were similar between the younger and older patients. Male patients and female patients had a similar rate of hematological events and Grade \ge3 hematologic events. Regardless of age and race, hematologic events were manageable and rarely led to dose discontinuation.
L1443: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1444: Combination therapy in MOR208C203 was well tolerated and resulted in long-term therapy adherence for the 30/80 (37.5%) patients who completed 12 cycles of combination therapy. Of these, 23/80 (28.8%) subsequently went on to complete &gt; 12 cycles of tafasitamab monotherapy.
L1445: The safety analyses indicate that the safety profile of the combination is mainly driven by lenalidomide side effects. Adding tafasitamab to lenalidomide therapy does not substantially change the incidence of AEs nor the type of AEs reported. Infections are an expected AE with monoclonal antibody therapy due to its B-cell depleting properties. Grade 1 or 2 infections were experienced by 35/81 (43.2%) patients in MOR208C203. The most common infections were bronchitis (16.0%), nasopharyngitis (9.9%), pneumonia (9.9%), respiratory tract infection (9.9%), upper respiratory tract infection (9.9%), and urinary tract infection (9.9%).
L1446: Infusion-related reactions related to tafasitamab occurred in 5/81 (6.2%) patients; all were Grade 1, occurred during the first infusion, and did not require discontinuation of treatment. Prior to infusion, all patients received premedication with H1 blockers, antipyretics, and/or glucocorticosteroids. Infusion-related reactions were easily managed with appropriate standard supportive therapy.
L1447: In MOR208C203 a total of 20 (24.7%) patients discontinued study medication due to a TEAE. Of these, 8 patients discontinued lenalidomide alone, 10 discontinued both tafasitamab and lenalidomide, and 2 patients discontinued only tafasitamab.
L1448: The combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide can cause neutropenia, including febrile neutropenia. Complete blood counts should be monitored prior to initiation of tafasitamab and prior to each dose, and as clinically indicated. Based on the severity of neutropenia, tafasitamab may require dose interruption or treatment discontinuation.
L1449: The incidence and severity of hematologic TEAEs observed in MOR208C203 Combination Therapy are similar to those published for lenalidomide. The incidence of hematologic and non-hematologic TEAEs decreased when patients after completion of the combination therapy phase continued with tafasitamab monotherapy. Tafasitamab monotherapy showed lower rates of hematologic TEAEs compared with published lenalidomide monotherapy trials or with the Combination Therapy.
L1450: Overall, tafasitamab was well tolerated at the proposed dose of 12.0 mg/kg according to the schedule noted above in the target population of patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for, or refuse ASCT. Adverse events were generally manageable by dose modification and standard clinical practice.
L1451: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1452: The safety of the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide was evaluated in trial MOR208C203, this was supported by three monotherapy trials.
L1453: For the pivotal trial, MOR208C203, a total of 46 patients (56.8%) were exposed to tafasitamab for &gt; 6 months, a total of 34 patients (42.0%) were exposed for greater than 1 year and a total of 19 patients (23.5%) were exposed for &gt; 2 years.
L1454: The exposure to tafasitamab is longer for patients in trial MOR208C203 compared to the pooled monotherapy trials. For trial MOR208C203, 35 patients received ≥12 cycles of treatment compared to the pooled monotherapy safety population where 11 patients received ≥12 cycles of tafasitamab. For the pooled monotherapy population, 84.4% of the patient population (119 patients) received between 1-5 cycles.
L1455: For trial, MOR208C203, the median age was 72 years with a range of 41-86 years and for the pooled monotherapy trials the median age was 63 with range of 16-90 years. Most patients in both the pivotal trial MOR208C203 and the pooled monotherapy population were white. In trial MOR208C203, 99.9% enrolled were white, 2.5% Asian, 0% Black/African American, 1.2% other and 7.4% missing. In the pooled monotherapy patient population, 92.9% were white, 0.7% Asian, 3.5% Black/African American, and 2.8% other.
L1456: The majority of the patient population in MOR208C203 had a higher ECOG status compared to the pooled monotherapy trials. A total of 55.6% of patients in trial MOR208C203 had an ECOG status of 1, while 55.3% in the pooled monotherapy population had an ECOG status of 0. For trial MOR208C203, 8 patients (9.9%) died within 30 days of the last dose. The majority of deaths within 30 days of last dose were due to disease progression, 5 patients. A total of 4 patients died as a result of TEAEs, 1 of these deaths occurred at day 68 although the TEAE of PML started prior. The TEAEs which resulted in death included CVA, sudden death, respiratory failure likely as a result of infection and PML.
L1457: There were more patients who experienced at least 1 SAE on trial MOR208C203, 42/81 (51.9%) patients compared to the pooled monotherapy trials 37/141 (26.2%) patients. For trial MOR208C203, the majority of SAEs were in the System Organ Class of Infections and infestations with pneumonia being the most common SAE in this System Organ Class. The only SAE that occurred in &gt;5% of patients on trial MOR208C203 was febrile neutropenia at 6.2%. A total of 20 patients (24.7%) discontinued either both drugs or either drug due to a TEAE. Neutropenia was the most frequent TEAE that lead to discontinuation in 3 patients. At the SOC
L1458: level, 5 patients (6.2%) had a TEAE in infections and infestations that led to withdrawal and 4 patients (4.9%) in the SOC of blood and lymphatic system disorders.
L1459: TEAE that occurred in more than 20% of patients on the L-MIND trial include neutropenia, fatigue, anemia, diarrhea, thrombocytopenia, cough, peripheral edema, pyrexia, respiratory tract infection, and decreased appetite. The more common grade \ge 3TEAE which occurred in more than 10% of patients on the L-MIND trial are neutropenia, thrombocytopenia and febrile neutropenia.
L1460: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1461: ## 8.1. Statistical Issues
L1462: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1463: The activity of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide (tafa+LEN) was investigated in Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND), a single arm study. Per FDA's analysis, the estimated ORR was 55% (95% CI: 43%, 67%) in patients with centrally-confirmed DLBCL. Because this is a combination therapy, the contribution of effect question that is being raised is whether the observed ORR 55% is attributable to lenalidomide (in part or in whole). Per the MOR208C201 study, an estimated investigator-assessed ORR for single agent tafasitamab was approximately 26% (95% CI: 13%, 43%) in DLBCL patients. The Applicant also cited single agent lenalidomide (LEN) data from the literature showing ORR estimates that range from 21% to 29%. As the Applicant noted, the comparison of trial results with external results to learn about contribution of effect is not straightforward for a number of reasons, including differences in the target population.
L1464: The primary objective of Study MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) was to learn about LEN single agent activity in DLBCL patients in non-clinical trial settings. The RE-MIND study induced a LEN external cohort that resembled patients in the tafa+LEN cohort from the L-MIND trial and compared their estimated ORRs, with a view towards eliciting supportive information to clarify the contribution of effect question. At face value, the results of the RE-MIND study suggested that tafa+LEN is a substantial improvement over LEN as the respective ORRs per FDA's analyses were 59% (95% CI: 46%, 71%) and 32% (95% CI: 21%, 43%). Had this been a randomized study, these results would resolve the contribution of effect question. In an observational study context however, the interpretation of these RE-MIND results are not straightforward as they are likely to be contaminated by external factors, some of which include
L1465: - Outcome misclassification
L1466: - Covariate misclassification
- Residual confounding
- Length-biased sampling
L1467: This raises the question of whether the difference in the size of the estimated ORRs between the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts are credible or whether the difference can be ascribable to these contaminants, in part or in whole. Unfortunately, these issues cannot be resolved in an unambiguous way. FDA performed additional analyses to address some of these issues.
L1468: ## Outcome Misclassification
L1469: Outcome misclassification occurs when the reported response data are not correct. For example, a patient may be incorrectly reported as a non-responder when in fact the patient was a responder (false negative). Similarly, a patient may be incorrectly reported as a responder when in fact the patient was a non-responder (false positive). The L-MIND trial captured both IRC- and INV- assessed disease status, which enabled an assessment of ORR based on IRC. In the RE-MIND study, the response in the LEN cohort was physician-reported.
L1470: As part of the RE-MIND study, some LEN patients were selected for a validation study where their disease status were retrospectively assessed by a review committee. Although the validation study sampled approximately 70 patients, the study was small in the sense that only 22 physician-reported responders were sampled; thus, the utility of the results of the validation study is an open question. It is likely that the outcome misclassification process is differential between the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts in the RE-MIND study.
L1471: As outcome misclassification is likely to be differential between patients in the LEN and tafa+LEN cohorts, FDA conducted additional analyses to assess the variability of the RE-MIND results. The setting for the analyses assumes that IRC-assessed response status is the desired endpoint while physician-reported response status is subject to potential misclassification. This implies that the response status of patients in the LEN cohort who were not in the validation study were considered subject to misclassification. Assuming a true positive of no less than 75%, the most favorable scenario to the LEN cohort is a true negative of 100% which leads to an estimated adjusted ORR of 39% (95% CI: 26%, 51%) for the LEN cohort. This resulted in an ORR difference between tafa+LEN and LEN of 21% (95% CI: 3%, 38%). While the true positive of lower than 75% can lead to higher adjusted LEN ORRs, the plausibility of such scenarios is less plausible as it would imply a false negative of more than 25% in the assessment of patients' disease status in clinical practice. It is not clear a false negative of 25% or more in clinical practice is plausible.
L1472: Assuming other aspects of the data are valid, FDA's sensitivity analyses suggest that the observed results were robust to outcome misclassification.
L1473: ## Residual Confounding
L1474: Residual confounding stems from the fact that regardless of attempts to analytically address confounding when comparing two cohorts in an observational study, the efforts may not be sufficient. Reasons for the insufficiency include the following.
L1475: **Prognostic for ORR.** The nine factors used in the RE-MIND study are potentially prognostic for OS/PFS. However, there appears to be little data to suggest that they are prognostic for ORR. To the extent that this is true, achieving balance on these nine covariates would not address the issue of confounding. At this time, it is unclear that the nine covariates are prognostic for ORR.
L1476: **Remaining Imbalance.** Assuming that the factors being controlled for are prognostic for ORR, it is clear that some of the attributes were not balanced. For example, the marginal distribution of Ann Arbor staging, ECOG, and number of prior lines were not balanced and were more favorable to the tafa+LEN cohort. To this point, FDA performed an additional propensity score analysis (results not shown) on the MAS25 analysis set to induce greater balance on all 9 covariates. The results of this analysis were similar to the Applicant's results and thus did not change the general conclusion. However, the similarity of results between FDA and the Applicant's analyses should be interpreted carefully as it could simply mean that the covariates were weakly or not prognostic for ORR.
L1477: We also note that similarity in the marginal distributions is not sufficient to justify claims of achieving balance as it must also be supported by similarity in the joint distributions. Due to the small size of the RE-MIND study, it is not feasible to adequately assess similarity of the joint distributions of baseline characteristics. However, we noted in Section 8.1.3 that the joint distributions of refractory to last line, number of prior lines, and primary refractory disease do not appear to be balanced in the MAS25 matched analysis set.
L1478: **Unmeasured Confounding.** Even if it was feasible to perfectly balance the pre-specified factors, the fact still remains that there are yet to be determined/unmeasured factors (e.g., cytogenetics) that are prognostic for ORR in the studied population and may be less favorable to the LEN cohort. Addressing unmeasured confounding is analytically difficult despite methods that suggest otherwise (e.g., Schneeweiss 2006; Lin et al 1998; VanderWeele et al 2017). Furthermore, the results of such analyses are often over-interpreted. This difficulty stems from the fact that the underlying assumptions cannot be rigorously justified and from the fact that it is difficult to conclude whether the results of such analyses are plausible. Consider FDA Table 54 below. This table was obtained through an FDA simulation study that examined the sensitivity of the observed results to various distributional scenarios of an unmeasured binary confounder, denoted as U, across the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts and across responders (ORR=1) and non-responders (ORR=0). FDA's analyses examined over 2700 scenarios. The results in FDA Table 54 were obtained after selecting only scenarios where the 95% confidence interval of the difference in ORR rates contains 0, and after eliminating all scenarios that were viewed as not
L1479: likely to occur.
L1480: - Column 1 is the difference in ORR rates between tafa+LEN and LEN after adjusting for an unmeasured confounder U.
- Column 2 (\Gamma_0) is the relative odds of responding between patients who are U-positive and U-negative in the LEN cohort.
- Column 3 (\Gamma_1) is the relative odds of responding between patients who are U-positive and U-negative in the tafa+LEN cohort.
- Column 4 is the prevalence of patients who are U-positive in the LEN cohort.
- Column 5 is the prevalence of patients who are U-positive in the tafa+LEN cohort.
L1481: From this analysis, there are two things to note. First, potentially there exists an unmeasured binary confounder that may lead to a lack of statistical difference. The simulation study also suggested that difference in the prevalence would have to be at least 27% for the simulation results to be credible. While this is statistically possible, it is not clear that it is plausible given that the study employed inclusion/exclusion criteria that were similar to L-MIND and adjusted for 9 potentially prognostic covariates. Second, it is unclear how \Gamma_0 and \Gamma_1 in columns 2 and 3 could be used to make decisions. Row 1 for example says that a binary unmeasured confounder U will induce a lack of statistical difference in ORR if we believe the following:
L1482: - The prevalence of U-positives in the LEN cohort is 36% while it is 70% in the tafa+LEN cohort.
- In the LEN cohort, the odds of responding among U-positives is 2.85 times that of U-negatives.
- In the tafa+LEN cohort, the odds of responding among U-positives is 1.89 times that of U-negatives.
L1483: Again, while these are possible, there is little information to suggest that these values are credible.
L1484: **Table 54: A Sensitivity Analysis of Unmeasured Confounding Looking at Scenarios of an Unmeasured U that Results in Lack of Statistical Difference in ORR**

L1487: **SOURCE:** FDA-analysis
L1488: ## 8.1. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1489: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1490: Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) was the primary study upon which the activity of tafa+LEN was assessed in DLBCL patients who had relapsed after or were refractory to at least 1, but no more than 3 previous systemic regimens, and who were not candidates for high dose chemotherapy and subsequent ASCT (or relapsed after ASCT). The results from the L-MIND study (per FDA's analyses) suggested that tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide resulted in an estimated ORR of 55% (95% CI: 43%, 67%), CR of 37% (95% CI: 25%, 49%), and PR of 18% (95% CI: 10%, 29%) in the 71 patients with central pathology confirmed diagnosis of relapsed/refractory DLBCL patients. Estimated DOR was 21.7 months. Based on the ORR and DOR and the lack of approved therapies for this patient population, the efficacy results from the L-MIND trial are considered to provide a meaningful treatment benefit for the intended patient population.
L1491: Additionally, the activity of single agent tafasitamab was assessed in Study MOR208C201, which showed an estimated ORR of 26% (95% CI: 13%, 43%). Although study MOR208C206 (RE-MIND), an observational study, was designed to learn about the contribution of effect question, due to the limitations of the study however, it can only be concluded that the results from the study regarding LEN activity were consistent with what was reported in the literature.
L1492: Based on the favorable risk-benefit profile of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide, the clinical and statistical reviewers recommend approval of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL) not otherwise specified, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT).

L1503: ## 10. Pediatrics
L1504: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1505: Tafasitamab received Orphan Drug Designation on December 1, 2014. Drugs with orphan drug designation have a statutory exemption under Section 505B(k)(1) of the FD&amp;C Act from the requirement to conduct pediatric studies under the Pediatric Research Equity Act; therefore, pediatric studies are not required for tafasitamab.
