L1576: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1577: The Applicant disclosed all financial interests/arrangements with clinical investigators as per 21 CFR Part 54. None of the investigators or sub-investigators involved in the clinical trials had financial interests to disclose or arrangements that required disclosure. Limited instances of missing financial disclosure information occurred for 6 sub-investigators across the studies. Of these six, three did not see any patients while delegated on the study, one was delegated for one month only, and information was missing for the remaining two.
L1578: The lack of disclosures across investigators in the clinical trials does not raise concerns about the overall integrity of study data. The three studies were conducted over multiple sites and in multiple countries, thus limiting the potential for a single investigator or sub-investigator to make a significant contribution to the data. Further, all efficacy assessments were conducted by an IRC, or both the Investigator and an IRC.

L1591: ## 19.4 OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1592: ## 19.4.1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1593: Tafasitamab concentrations in human serum were quantified using validated ligand binding assays. The pivotal Study **MOR208C203 (L-MIND)** used the 2nd generation PK assay, an electrochemiluminescence (ECL) assay based on MSD technology. Briefly, tafasitamab was captured on the surface of a MSD plate coated with the Fab-fragment AbD18759.1 (human monoclonal idiotypic anti-tafasitamab antibody), and the bound analyte was detected using the antibody 6B11-E5-ECL, an ECL-labeled mouse monoclonal idiotypic anti-tafasitamab antibody. The lower limit of quantification was 50 ng/mL in human serum. Other than Phase 1 Study **XmAb5574-01**, this PK assay was used in all other clinical studies (**L-MIND**, **COSMOS**, **MOR208C201**, **MOR208C202**). The PK assay has been validated for intended use. Summary method performance of the bioanalytical method to measure tafasitamab in human serum, with method performance in pivotal Study **L-MIND**, is shown in **Table 55**.
L1594: Table 55: Summary Method Performance of the Bioanalytical Method to Measure Tafasitamab Concentrations in Human Serum

Method validation report MOR208L016 Amendment 1, entitled: "STABILITY STUDY REPORT: Stability evaluation of MOR00208 ((b)(4) production process) in human serum from DLBCL patients, CLL patients and healthy volunteers"

L1601: ## 19.4.2 Pharmacometrics Review
L1602: A population pharmacokinetics (PPK) model was developed by the Applicant to characterize the PK of tafasitamab in patients with either relapsed/refractory (R/R) chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL)/small lymphocytic lymphoma (SLL), R/R Non-Hodgkin's lymphoma (NHL) (including diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL)), or R/R acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL). Patients intrinsic and extrinsic factors which influence the PK and PK variability of tafasitamab were identified. Individual tafasitamab exposure metrics were generated for subsequent exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety. In this review, the FDA Pharmacometrics Reviewer verified the Applicant's PPK model and E-R analyses for both safety and safety.
L1603: ## 1. Population PK analyses
L1604: Four clinical trials were included in the PPK analyses: XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202 and MOR208C203/L-MIND. In L-MIND, subjects received tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide (LEN), whereas in the other trials tafasitamab was administered
L1605: as monotherapy. The final PPK analysis was conducted based on 221 subjects and 3972 measurable tafasitamab serum concentrations.
L1606: The intrinsic and extrinsic factors of sex, age, body weight, race, ethnicity, disease type, tumor size, LDH, cancer stage, ECOG, B-cell count (time-varying), absolute lymphocyte count, immunogenicity, total protein, ALB, liver enzymes, CrCL and LEN administration were analyzed in the analysis.
L1607: The final model was a 2-compartment model with time-dependent elimination. The time-dependent clearance was defined as CL \cdot \exp(-t^\gamma/(T_{50}^\gamma + t^\gamma)) to describe the increasing C_{min} in L-MIND trial observed beyond one year of treatment. The effect of baseline body weight (WTB) was found to be significant on CL and V. The effects of ALB on CL, sex on V and disease type on V were also included in the final PPK model. The final model parameters are shown in Table 56. The covariate effects on exposures were generally moderate (5th percentile – 95th percentile of the respective covariate range caused a change of &lt; ±50% of the respective primary PK parameter or derived exposure parameter).
L1608: **Table 56: Final Model Parameter Estimates for Tafasitamab**

L1618: In addition, the overall prevalence and incidence of anti-drug antibodies (ADA) was low across studies (8/27 subjects in XmAb5574-01, 5/92 subjects in MOR208C201, 0/22 subjects in
L1619: MOR208C202 and 2/92 subjects in MOR208C203/L-MIND). All subjects tested positive were ADA-positive already at baseline. No treatment-emergent or treatment-boosted ADAs were detected. There appears to be no impact of ADA on the PK of tafasitamab at the studied dose levels.
L1620: Next, the Applicant derived exposure parameters (AUC_{0-28} (simulated cumulative exposure in treatment cycle 1, i.e. from 0-28 days), AUC_{0-56} (simulated cumulative exposure in treatment cycle 1 and 2, i.e., from 0-56 days), average C_{min}, the highest C_{min}, C_{max} after the first dose, and overall maximal C_{max}) for each subject based on post hoc analysis of the final PPK model. The calculated exposure metrics were used in the exposure-response analyses that will be discussed in the following sessions.
L1621: **Reviewer's comments:** *Overall, the Applicant's PPK model appears reasonable. The significant effect of body weight on PK, with higher CL and V expected at higher body weight, supports the proposed mg/kg dosing regimen. The Applicant investigated the effect of renal function by evaluating CrCL on CL as a continuous variable, and hepatic function by evaluating AST, ALT, ALKP and GGT on CL as continuous variables. Upon information request, the Applicant conducted additional sensitivity analysis to re-evaluate the effect of renal and hepatic impairment using classification of renal and hepatic function (normal, mild, moderate, or severe) as categorical variables.*
L1622: The Applicant's sensitive analysis is summarized as below:
L1623: In the PPK analysis dataset, the 221 subjects were categorized as following:
L1624: - For renal impairment:109 (49.3%) subjects with normal renal function (CrCL \ge 90 \text{ mL/min}),
- 76 (34.4%) subjects with mild renal impairment (CrCL: 60-89 \text{ mL/min}) and
- 36 (16.3%) subjects with moderate renal impairment (CrCL: 30-59 \text{ mL/min}).
- For hepatic impairment (based on NCI-ODWG criteria):174 (78.7%) subjects with normal hepatic function (aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and total bilirubin (BILI) levels \le the upper limit of normal [ULN]),
- 42 (19.0%) subjects with mildly impaired hepatic function (AST &gt; ULN and BILI \le ULN, or AST any value and BILI &gt; 1.0 - 1.5 \times ULN); and
- 5 (2.3%) subjects with moderately impaired hepatic function (AST any value and BILI &gt; 1.5 - 3 \times ULN).
L1625: The Applicant conducted a sensitivity analysis using stepwise covariate modelling (scm) in order to elucidate a potential influence of renal and hepatic impairment categories on tafasitamab clearance (CL). The analysis result showed that, neither hepatic nor renal impairment categories were significant covariates. The sensitivity scm run resulted in the same covariates (i.e. body weight on CL and V, albumin on CL, sex on V, and disease type on V) as the original analysis.
L1626: **Reviewer's comments:** FDA Reviewer agrees with the Applicant's assessment that mild and moderate renal impairment, as well as mild hepatic impairment did not impact the exposure of tafasitamab. For other renal and hepatic impairment categories, no conclusion could be drawn as too few or no subjects were available in such categories.
L1627: ## 2. Exposure-response analyses for efficacy
L1628: The Applicant conducted exposure-response (E-R) analysis using ORR and PFS for each of the studies separately (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201 and MOR208C203/L-MIND). AUC_{0-28}, AUC_{0-56} and maximal C_{min} level were selected as exposure metrics for the current analysis, in which, AUC_{0-28} is the most relevant PK measure. In study XmAb5574-01, the dose levels tested were 0.3 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, 3mg/kg, 6 mg/kg, 9 mg/kg and 12 mg/kg, while in other two studies, the tested dose level was 12 mg/kg only. In the following sessions, the E-R analyses for efficacy for each study will be reviewed.
L1629: ## 2.1 E-R analysis for efficacy of study XmAb5574-01
L1630: Twenty-seven (27) patients with R/R CLL/SLL were included in the analysis. The objective response rate per dose level is shown in Figure 20. Overall, the ORR appears to increase in a dose dependent manner, with highest response rate observed at 12 mg/kg QW dosing group.
L1631: **Figure 20: Objective Response Rate Versus Dose Group in the Phase I Dose Escalation Study XmAb5574-01**
L1632: Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035
L1633: For the E-R analysis using PFS, a longer PFS for patients having higher exposures was observed for all of the three PK metrics (Figure 21). In a sensitivity analysis that excluded the subjects
L1634: received additional doses after the end of cycle 2, the overall trend of longer PFS for subjects with higher exposure was still visible (plots not shown in this review).
L1635: **Figure 21: Kaplan-Meier Plots for PFS Stratified by 50% Percentiles of Measures of Exposure in the Phase I Dose Escalation Study XmAb5574-01**
L1636: Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035
L1637: **Reviewer's comment:** A positive E-R relationship for efficacy was observed in this FIH study. However, very low number of subjects were enrolled in most of the dose cohorts in this study, and the variability in the analysis for ORR and PFS is considered large. Thus, the observed E-R relationship for ORR and PFS should be interpreted with caution.
L1638: ## 2.2 E-R analysis for efficacy of study MOR208C201
L1639: Ninety-one (91) patients with different NHL subtypes were included in the analysis. All of the subjects received 12 mg/kg QW tafasitamab for up to 3 cycles. Based on univariable logistic regression, positive E-R relationships for probability of response were observed for all tested exposure metrics. Figure 22 shows the univariable logistic regression result using AUC0-28 as the predictor variable. A similar positive trend was observed in a sensitivity analysis, which excluded 6 subjects who did not receive the full number of planned doses during cycle 1 due to early drop out (plots not shown in this review).
L1640: **Figure 22: Uni-Variable Logistic Regression of Objective Response Versus AUC_{28} for Study MOR208C201 (N=91)**
L1641: *Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035*
L1642: In addition, the Applicant explored the potential confounding factors impacting the analysis in Figure 22 and concluded that no pronounced imbalance of baseline characteristics identified among the exposure quartiles (Table 57). It should be noted that the record for IPI risk was missing for 12 subjects and NK cell count was missing for 20 subjects. Baseline characteristics and corresponding ORR were also summarized by means of a forest plot (Figure 23). The applicant concluded that no pronounced imbalances in ORR among the various covariate quantiles or categories were noticed.

Supporting analyses of AUC56 and maximal C_{min} revealed similar results. L1656: *Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035*
L1657: Furthermore, the Applicant conducted E-R assessment using PFS in R/R DLBCL subgroup (N=28) of clinical study MOR208C201. The measures of exposure were split into 50% percentiles instead of quartiles due to the low number of subjects and events. The analysis identified a significant positive E-R relationship (p=0.044). However, potential confounding factors were not analyzed due to the low number of events.
L1658: **Reviewer's comment:** Overall, the Applicant's E-R assessment for efficacy appears acceptable. In the monotherapy trial (study MOR208C201), higher tafasitamab exposures were associated with the better efficacy (ORR and PFS). However, based on the distribution of available baseline prognostic factors there appears to be some imbalance in IPI risk index, NK cell count and prior lines for therapy across the exposure quartiles (Table 57 and Table 58). The patients in Q1 appears to be associated with higher IPI risk, lower NK cell counts, and more&gt;3 prior lines of therapy compared with those in Q4, which may have contributed to lower response in this group and the overall positive E-R relationships for efficacy (Figure 22, Figure 23 and Figure 24). A trend of higher in ORR was observed in patients with higher body weight (Figure 23), which may be due to the higher exposure at higher body weight (Table 57).
L1659: ## 2.3 E-R analysis for efficacy of study MOR208C203/L-MIND
L1660: In study MOR208C203/L-MIND, clinical effectiveness of tafasitamab was studied in combination with LEN in subjects with R/R DLBCL. Tafasitamab was administered at a dose level of 12 mg/kg. A slightly positive but not significant (p=0.093) E-R relationship was observed in a uni-variable logistic regression using ORR versus AUC0-28 in the MOR208C203/L-MIND study. However, in a sensitivity analysis that excluded 7 subjects who did not receive the full number of planned doses during cycle 1 due to early drop out, a flat E-R relationship for OR from a uni-variable logistic regression was observed (Figure 25). In addition, the Applicant noticed that ORR from study MOR208C203/L-MIND were generally higher compared to that observed in study MOR208C201. The covariate analysis did not reveal any pronounced dependency of ORR on baseline characteristics (Figure 26).
L1661: **Figure 25: Uni-Variable Logistic Regression of Objective Response Versus AUC28 for Clinical Study MOR208C203/L-MIND (N=80)**
L1662: Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035

L1666: Similar to ORR, exposure-efficacy assessment using PFS also revealed a flat E-R relationship in PFS amongst exposure quartiles in the L-MIND study (Figure 27). After adjusting for potentially confounding baseline covariates (“IPI score” and “primary refractoriness”), quartiles of AUC28 were still not significant predictors of PFS, thus confirming the finding of a flat E-R relationship in the MOR208C203/L-MIND study.
L1667: **Figure 27: Kaplan-Meier Plot Showing Analysis of PFS Stratified by Quartiles of AUC28 in Study MOR208C203/L-MIND (Tafasitamab in Combination with LEN)**
L1668: Source: Applicant's exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035
L1669: **Reviewer's comment:** FDA Reviewer agrees with the Applicant's assessment. In contrast to a positive E-R relationship observed in monotherapy study MOR208C201, the efficacy of tafasitamab appeared to reach the plateau in the combination therapy study MOR208C203/L-MIND. Thus, from the efficacy perspective, the proposed dosing regimen of tafasitamab at a dose of 12 mg/kg is reasonable in the combination therapy with LEN.
L1670: ## 3. Exposure-response relationship for clinical safety
L1671: The Applicant conducted assessment for exposure-response for clinical safety. Adverse events (AEs) were analyzed vs tafasitamab exposure for R/R NHL subjects (study MOR208C201, including R/R DLBCL subjects) and R/R DLBCL subjects (study MOR208C203/L-MIND). AUC_{0-28} and C_{max} were used in the analyses. The individual exposure metrics were separated into exposure quartiles and analyzed against TEAEs, each AESI and Neutropenia \ge Grade 3.
L1672: The TEAEs that have been evaluated included Anaphylactic Reactions, Drug-Related Hepatic Disorders, Torsade de pointes/QT Prolongation, Tumor Flare Reaction, Tumor Lysis Syndrome, Non-haematological malignant tumors, Infusion Related Reactions, Grade 3 or Higher Hypersensitivity, Cytokine Release Syndrome, Overdose, Diarrhea, Non-allergic Rash/Skin
L1673: Reactions, Embolic and Thrombotic Events, Infections, Neutropenias and Acute Renal Failure. Overall, there were no obvious trends within each study for increasing TEAEs for any SOC with increasing exposure.
L1674: **Reviewer's comment:** The Applicant's E-R analysis for safety appears acceptable. Apparent E-R relationships were observed for several AESIs. However, the increase in odds ratio with increasing exposure quartiles for these TEAEs was moderate, and the incidence rates per exposure quartiles were low (&lt;20%). Therefore, the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen of tafasitamab at a dose of 12 mg/kg in the combination with LEN is supported by the E-R relationships for safety.
L1675: In conclusion, the Applicant developed a PPK model that reasonably described the PK behavior of tafasitamab in patients with either R/R CLL/SLL, R/R NHL (including DLBCL), or R/R ALL. Body weight had a statistically significant impact on both tafasitamab CL and V, supporting the body weight-based dosing regimen. A positive E-R relationship was observed for tafasitamab monotherapy clinical studies, while in combination with LEN, tafasitamab exhibited a flat E-R relationship, whereas ORRs increased almost two folds compared to tafasitamab in monotherapy. Based on above findings, the proposed dosing regimen for tafasitamab administered at a dose of 12 mg/kg as IV infusion Q1W for three cycles and Q2W afterwards, in combination with LEN is reasonable for patients with R/R-DLBCL.
L1676: ## 19.4.3 Genomics
L1677: ## FCGR Polymorphisms and Tumor Response
L1678: FCGR2A and FCGR3A polymorphisms affecting the binding affinity of the Fc region of therapeutic monoclonal antibodies to FCGRs have been reported to influence treatment outcomes in different cancers (Mellor et al., 2013; Nagelkerke et al., 2019). According to the Applicant, tafasitamab was engineered to allow increased binding affinity to FCGRs, independent of the patient's FCGR genotype. Binding affinities of tafasitamab for FCGR3A (p.158V and p.158F alleles) were increased 47 and 130-fold relative to the unmodified IgG1 antibody and binding affinities for FCGR2A (p.131R and p.131H alleles) were increased 11 and 5.4-fold relative to the unmodified IgG1, respectively.
L1679: The Applicant conducted exploratory analyses of responses by FCGR genotype in studies MOR208C203 (L-MIND) and MOR208C201 (DLBCL Subset). Germline DNA samples were collected with mucosal cheek swabs from consented patients and centrally genotyped for FCGR2A p.H131R (also denoted as p.H166R, rs1801274) and FCGR3A p.V158F (also denoted as p.V176F, rs396991) polymorphisms. Approximately 70% of patients in Study 203 and 66% in Study 201 had evaluable samples with response assessment. Almost all patients were White in both analyses.
L1680: The number of patients and overall response rate (ORR) in subsets defined by the FCGR genotype are summarized in Table 59. High affinity subgroups included patients homozygous for high affinity alleles (FCGR2A p.131H, FCGR3A p.158V), while low affinity subgroups included patients heterozygous or homozygous for the low affinity alleles. Although response rates were nominally lower in the subgroup of patients homozygous for the high affinity FCGR3A p.158V allele, results are limited by small numbers, missing data, and the exploratory nature of the analyses and are not conclusive.

L1698: Figure 1: Binding of Tafasitamab and Its IgG1 and Fc-KO Analogs to CD19+ Cells. A dose-response curve showing the binding of three antibodies (IgG1, XmAb5574, and Fc-KO) to CD19+ RS4;11 cells. The Y-axis represents the percentage of binding (0 to 7000) and the X-axis represents the Log[antibody] (mg/ml) from -5 to 2. All three curves show similar binding profiles, indicating that the Fc domain modifications do not significantly affect binding affinity.
L1699: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing the anti-proliferative and apoptotic activity of Tafasitamab (XmAb5574) compared to control antibodies (IgG1, Fc KO, XmAb control). The left graph shows Relative ATP luminescence (%) versus Antibody concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale, indicating dose-dependent inhibition of proliferation. The right graph shows Caspase activity (%) and Annexin V+ cells (%) versus Antibody concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale, indicating dose-dependent induction of apoptosis.
L1700: Figure 3 displays nine graphs showing the NK Cell Mediated Antibody-Dependent Cellular Cytotoxicity (ADCC) activity of Tafasitamab (XmAb5574) against various tumor cell lines. The Y-axis represents ADCC (%) and the X-axis represents Log [Ab] (ng/mL). The legend indicates three conditions: XmAb5574 (black squares), anti-CD19 IgG1 (white squares), and XmAb isotype control (black circles).
The cell lines tested are Raji, Namalwa, SU-DHL-6, Wac3CD5, Ramos, BV-173, RS4;11, Bonna-12, and SUP-B15. In all panels, XmAb5574 shows significantly higher ADCC activity compared to the anti-CD19 IgG1 and XmAb isotype control, demonstrating potent NK cell mediated cytotoxicity against these target cells.
L1701: Figure 4 displays two graphs (A and B) showing the Antibody-Dependent Cellular Phagocytosis (ADCP) activity of tafasitamab (XmAb5574) compared to an IgG1 analog (XmAb control) against two tumor cell lines: RS4;11 (A) and SUP-B15 (B). The Y-axis represents % ADCP, and the X-axis represents [Antibody] (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.01 to 100).
In both graphs, XmAb5574 shows significantly higher ADCP activity than the IgG1 analog across all tested concentrations. XmAb5574 activity increases dose-dependently, reaching approximately 28% ADCP for RS4;11 and 50% ADCP for SUP-B15 at 10 ng/mL. The IgG1 analog shows minimal activity, remaining below 10% ADCP.
Figure 4: ADCP Activity of Tafasitamab Against Tumor Cell Lines. Two graphs (A and B) show % ADCP versus [Antibody] (ng/mL) for RS4;11 and SUP-B15 cell lines, comparing IgG1, XmAb5574, and XmAb control.
L1702: Figure 5 is a line graph illustrating the Mean tumor volume (mm3) versus Days post tumor cell injection (0 to 40) for SCID mice bearing Raji cell xenografts, comparing various treatment groups.
The legend identifies the groups:
PBS (Control)
XENP4614, 10 mg/kg (Isotype control)
XmAb5574, 1 mg/kg
XmAb5574, 3 mg/kg
XmAb5574, 10 mg/kg
The PBS and XENP4614 groups show rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 2500 mm3 by Day 40. In contrast, the XmAb5574 treated groups show significantly reduced tumor growth. The XmAb5574 10 mg/kg group demonstrates the most potent anti-tumor activity, resulting in the lowest mean tumor volume, which remains below 500 mm3 throughout the 40-day observation period. The XmAb5574 3 mg/kg group also shows substantial inhibition, reaching approximately 1500 mm3 by Day 40.
Figure 5: Anti-Tumor Activity of Tafasitamab in SCID Mice Bearing Raji Cell Xenografts. A line graph showing Mean tumor volume (mm3) versus Days post tumor cell injection (0 to 40) for PBS, XENP4614, 10 mg/kg, XmAb5574, 1 mg/kg, XmAb5574, 3 mg/kg, and XmAb5574, 10 mg/kg groups.
L1703: Line graph showing Mean tumor volume (mm^3) versus Days post tumor cell injection (0 to 45) for five treatment groups. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 4500 mm^3. The X-axis shows Days post tumor cell injection, with arrows indicating Ab injection at Days 3, 10, 17, and 24. The legend identifies the groups: PBS (green circles), XENP4614, 10 mg/kg (black squares), XmAb5574, 1 mg/kg (purple triangles), XmAb5574, 3 mg/kg (red asterisks), and XmAb5574, 10 mg/kg (cyan diamonds). The PBS and XENP4614 groups show rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 3800 mm^3 by Day 40. The XmAb5574 groups show significantly reduced tumor growth compared to PBS and XENP4614, with XmAb5574, 10 mg/kg showing the lowest tumor volume, reaching approximately 1200 mm^3 by Day 40. Statistical significance (*p<0.005) is noted for XmAb5574 groups compared to PBS or XENP4614.
L1704: Figure 7 displays two scatter plots showing the Probability of Response (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus AUC28 (X-axis, 2000 to 10000 µg/mL*day) for two clinical studies. The left plot is for MOR208C201 (Tafasitamab Monotherapy), and the right plot is for MOR208C203/L-MIND (Tafasitamab in Combination with Lenalidomide). Both plots show a logistic regression curve (black solid line) and 95% confidence intervals (grey shaded area). Observed responses (black circles) and quartile-wise ORR (red dots) are plotted, along with their 95% Clopper-Pearson confidence intervals (red vertical lines). The left plot shows p = 0.096, and the right plot shows p = 0.798. The quartile borders are displayed at the bottom of the X-axis.

L1706: Figure 9 is a flowchart illustrating the Trial Design of Clinical Study MOR208C203. The timeline spans 5 years.
The study begins with R/R DLBCL (1-3 prior regimens) followed by Screening. The treatment phase consists of 12 cycles of combination therapy (Cycle 1 through Cycle 12).
During Cycles 1-3, LEN is administered on Days 1-21 and MOR00208 is administered on Days 1, 8, 15, and 22 (28-day cycles). During Cycles 4-12, LEN is administered on Days 1-21 and MOR00208 is administered on Days 1 and 15 (28-day cycles).
Following Cycle 12, the study enters a Disease Control (SD) phase, which includes an Additional Treatment Phase (Cycle 13 until disease progression). This phase is followed by MOR00208 administration (Day 1, 15 (28-day cycles)), End of Treatment (EOT), 30 Day FU Visit, and Survival FU (every 3 months).
* An additional loading dose of MOR00208 is to be administered on Day 4 of cycle 1

L1709: Figure 16 is a Kaplan-Meier plot showing the Duration of Response (Excluding Non-Responders) for two cohorts: Tafasitamab + LEN (blue line) and LEN monotherapy (gray line). The Y-axis represents the percentage of response (0% to 100%), and the X-axis represents Time in months (0 to 33).
The plot shows that the Tafasitamab + LEN cohort maintains a significantly higher percentage of response over time compared to the LEN monotherapy cohort. The LEN monotherapy cohort shows a rapid decline in response duration, reaching 0% response around 18 months. The Tafasitamab + LEN cohort maintains a response rate above 40% for the duration of the study.

L1711: Figure 18 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Tafasitamab Final PPK Model. The plots are arranged in two rows of three.
The top row shows Observed Concentrations (mg/L) versus Population Predictions (mg/L), Individual Predictions (mg/L), and Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Time after first dose (days). The bottom row shows Observed Concentrations (mg/L) versus Population Predictions (mg/L), Individual Predictions (mg/L), and Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Population Prediction (mg/L). In all plots, the observed data points generally follow the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
L1712: Figure 19 displays the Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (VPC) for the Final PPK Model. The plot shows Prediction Corrected Tafasitamab concentration (mg/L) on the logarithmic Y-axis (ranging from 10 to 1000) versus Actual time after the first dose (days) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 600).
The plot includes observed data points (black dots), simulated median (red solid line), simulated 5th and 95th percentiles (blue shaded regions), and median (lines) and 95% prediction intervals (shaded regions).
L1713: Figure 20: Objective Response Rate Versus Dose Group in the Phase I Dose Escalation Study XmAb5574-01. The chart displays ORR per dose level (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus dose (mg/kg) (X-axis, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0, 6.0, 9.0, 12.0). The sample size (N) for each dose level is indicated above the corresponding data point: N=1 for 0.3, 1.0, and 3.0 mg/kg; N=3 for 6.0 and 9.0 mg/kg; and N=16 for 12.0 mg/kg. The ORR increases significantly with dose, reaching approximately 0.4 at 12 mg/kg.
L1714: Figure 21 displays three Kaplan-Meier plots (A, B, and C) showing Progression-Free Survival (PFS) probability versus Time (days) for subjects stratified by 50% percentiles of exposure measures in the Phase I Dose Escalation Study XmAb5574-01. The plots compare two strata: one defined by the median value of the exposure metric (light gray area) and the other by the upper 50% percentile (dark gray area). The legend indicates that 12 out of 16 subjects in the 12 mg/kg cohort are in the upper quantile.
Plot A (median AUC28): PFS probability vs Time (days). The upper quantile group shows a significantly better PFS outcome (p = 0.017). The number at risk is shown below the plot: 14, 4, 0, 0 (upper quantile) and 13, 7, 6, 2 (lower quantile).
Plot B (median AUC65): PFS probability vs Time (days). The upper quantile group shows a significantly better PFS outcome (p = 0.0082). The number at risk is shown below the plot: 14, 3, 0, 0 (upper quantile) and 13, 8, 6, 2 (lower quantile).
Plot C (median max. Ctrough): PFS probability vs Time (days). The upper quantile group shows a significantly better PFS outcome (p = 0.0082). The number at risk is shown below the plot: 14, 3, 0, 0 (upper quantile) and 13, 8, 6, 2 (lower quantile).
